The Great Tantra of the Empowerment of Noble Vajrapāṇi (Āryavajrapāṇyabhiṣekamahātantra)

The Great Tantra of the Empowerment of Noble Vajrapāṇi, known in Sanskrit as the Āryavajrapāṇyabhiṣekamahātantra and in Tibetan as Pakpa Lakna Dorjé Wangkurwé Gyü Chenpo, is a lengthy scripture of the tantra class, catalogued as Toh 496 in the Kangyur, where it is placed among the tantras of the action (kriyā) class. It opens in the great palace of Vaśavartin, king of the gods, where the Blessed One is seated on a jeweled lion throne amid an assembly of bodhisattvas, and its narrative centers on Vajrapāṇi, the bodhisattva who holds the vajra, and on the empowerment associated with him. Over the course of its chapters it combines teaching discourse and accounts of past deeds with extensive ritual instruction, ending with a series of rites, recitations and fire offerings by which practitioners may gain worldly and spiritual accomplishments, including visions of Vajrapāṇi, Mañjuśrī and the Blessed Lady Prajñāpāramitā. According to its colophon, it was translated into Tibetan by the Indian preceptor Śīlendrabodhi and the editor-translator Yeshé Dé during the early period of Tibetan translation.

In the language of India: ārya vajra pā ṇi a bhi ṣe ka ma hā tantra. In the language of Tibet: The Great Tantra of the Empowerment of Noble Vajrapāṇi.

First fascicle.

Homage to all the buddhas and bodhisattvas.

Thus did I hear at one time. The Blessed One was in the great palace of Vaśavartin, king of the gods, a great mansion that is a king of jewels, brought forth from inconceivable karmic maturation and thoroughly adorned with great kings of jewels that accord with the conduct of all the tathāgatas, the dwelling of the bodhisattva great beings. There he sat upon a great lion throne with a lotus heart, both its sides adorned with great jewels, lords of power, radiant, brilliant and resplendent. Just as, for example, the orb of the sun is radiant, brilliant and resplendent in a cloudless sky, so too the Blessed One sat radiant, brilliant and resplendent upon that great lion throne with a lotus heart, surrounded on all sides by a great host of bodhisattvas. He had attained the supreme perfection of all mastery over mind; he had blessed body, speech and mind as equal; free from all wavering and conceit, he engaged spontaneously in the deeds of a buddha. He was teaching the Dharma, namely the Dharma discourse called The Abode of the Equality of Body, the Dharma discourse called The Abode of the Equality of Speech, and likewise the Dharma discourse called The Abode of the Equality of Mind.

Then, having heard this Dharma discourse, those bodhisattvas obtained the meditative absorption called The Essence of the Array of the Equality of Body, Speech and Mind. As soon as they had obtained that meditative absorption, through the blessing of that very Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Vairocana, they saw tathāgatas as numerous as the atoms in ten buddha fields, each surrounded by a host of bodhisattvas, teaching the Dharma, namely the Dharma discourse called The Array of the Abode of the Equality of Body, Speech and Mind.

Why is that? Whatever bodhisattva great beings there are who are definitely shown throughout these many boundless world systems endowed with the arrayed ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence, generating the mind set on awakening and striving in its conduct, accomplishing the grounds, fully awakening to complete enlightenment, turning the wheel of Dharma, and displaying the great final nirvāṇa, all of that is through the blessing of the Blessed One Vairocana. In short, whatever sentient beings perform wholesome formative actions, all of them too were gathered by the Blessed One Vairocana, when formerly he practised the conduct of a bodhisattva, solely through these four means of attraction, namely generosity, pleasant speech, beneficial conduct, and consistency of aim; and it is through that very cause and those very objects of focus that they perform wholesome formative actions.

Thus those bodhisattvas saw and entered into all that vast great wisdom of the Blessed One Vairocana; they beheld it and contemplated it: however much virtue is performed, it too comes about through that very cause and that very object of focus. Thus those bodhisattvas were amazed and astonished, and, their hearts filled with wonder and longing, they thought, "The Tathāgata is inconceivable!" Falling silent, they gazed upon the Blessed One himself and contemplated him.

Then the Blessed One, knowing the wonder and longing in the hearts of those bodhisattva great beings, sent forth from his throat a light ray called Fully Exhorting All Bodhisattvas, with a retinue of many thousands of light rays. When it had come forth, it illuminated the whole body of the Blessed One Vairocana, and to all the bodhisattvas endowed with power who dwelt throughout the boundless reaches of space and the oceans of world systems, it gave this exhortation:

"The omniscient, perfectly complete Buddha,
The Blessed One, free of all fevers,
In the palace of King Vaśavartin,
Free of every fault,
Upon a great and delightful lion throne
Three hundred leagues in size,
Is seated there upon a lotus heart,
Surrounded by bodhisattvas.
Whatever the Great Sage has emanated
In that place in a great array,
Their number and their measure,
And whatever their shapes and colors,
Could not be told
Even in countless eons.
Go there and behold the great hero,
The perfectly complete Buddha, supreme among two-legged beings,
Who has cast off wavering and doubt,
Who cuts through all uncertainty!"

Then those light rays, having exhorted all the bodhisattvas, turned back and settled in their own place, in the throat of the Blessed One Vairocana.

Then the Blessed One sent forth again, from the curl of hair between his brows, a light ray called The Essence of the Treasury of the Array of Wish-Fulfilling Jewels, which fully exhorts all the buddhas, with a retinue of a hundred thousand light rays. In all the buddha fields it proclaimed to those buddhas, the blessed ones:

"To behold the Guide, the Blessed Vairocana,
And to make offerings to him,
And, wishing to benefit beings, so that the Dharma may be spoken,
All of you, come here without delay!"

Then those light rays too turned back and entered the very curl of hair between the Blessed One's brows.

Then, as soon as they had been exhorted by that light, those bodhisattva great beings asked their own respective tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas: "Blessed Ones, if we are to go to the great palace of King Vaśavartin in order to behold and pay homage to that Blessed One Vairocana, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha, in which world system is that great palace of King Vaśavartin? What is the name of the world system in which the Blessed One Vairocana fully awakened to complete enlightenment? And what is its seat of awakening like?"

When they had made this request, those tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas declared to their own respective bodhisattvas: "Sons of good family, there are boundless world systems, definitely shown, called the series of world systems endowed with the arrayed ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence. You should understand that each and every one of those boundless world systems that is shown contains many boundless arrays, definitely shown, of linkages of world systems.

"Sons of good family, the measure of those linked arrays is this. That which comprises and includes a hundred koṭis of four-continent world systems, a hundred koṭis of Sumerus, kings of mountains, a hundred koṭis of great oceans, and a hundred koṭis of moons and suns, is a great trichiliocosm. That which comprises and includes a hundred koṭis of great trichiliocosms forms one unit comprised and included within the boundless linkages endowed with the arrayed ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence. That which comprises and includes a hundred koṭis of the boundless linkages endowed with the arrayed ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence is one series of boundless world systems arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence. That which comprises and includes a hundred koṭis of series arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence is one intermediate series of world systems arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence. That which comprises and includes a hundred koṭis of the series of intermediate arrays of boundless world systems is one array of world systems arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence, and it was purified by the Blessed One Vairocana when formerly he practised the conduct of a bodhisattva.

"Sons of good family, in that world system the one and only tathāgata, arhat, perfectly complete buddha is that very Blessed One Vairocana. Through the power of his former aspirations he is surrounded by a host of bodhisattvas; utterly transcending the actions of body, speech and mind, he has blessed space itself as the seat of awakening, and he dwells in that intermediate world system, in the great palace of King Vaśavartin.

"Sons of good family, such is the Blessed One Vairocana that we could not fathom the limit of his qualities, his conduct, his aspirations or his wisdom even in countless eons. Sons of good family, we too were set upon unsurpassed, perfectly complete awakening by that Blessed One when formerly he was a bodhisattva, and we are, every one of us, mindful of the kindness that Blessed One did us and grateful for it.

"Sons of good family, that Blessed One Vairocana has sent forth this great net of light rays because he wishes, beginning from the former conduct and aspirations of the bodhisattva Samantabhadra and the unbroken maṇḍala of the wheel of his speech, that the teaching be made to endure, that the unsurpassed conduct of the Great Vehicle continue unbroken, that it be understood just as it is, and that, to the full extent that the bodhisattva Samantabhadra has blessed it, beginning from the great maṇḍala of vajra wisdom, the boundless and endless world systems be pervaded by the vast maṇḍala of the wheel of manifold speech."

Then, in the east, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Ratnaketu gave to the bodhisattva Ratnamati this heart-essence of the great jewel vajra:

na maḥ s manta mu khe bhyaḥ sarba ta thā ga te bhyaḥ sarba thā ja na

"Son of good family, by means of this heart-essence of the great jewel vajra, abide in that world system!"

Then the bodhisattva Ratnamati took up this heart-essence and vanished from that world system. Surrounded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, in order to make offerings to the Blessed One Vairocana, he manifested a great miraculous display of a bodhisattva and, by means of this heart-essence, great clouds of offerings to the great jewel vajra such as had never been seen before. He went to where the world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence was, and to where the palace of King Vaśavartin was, and on arriving there he covered the whole world system, together with the great palace of King Vaśavartin, with great clouds of jewel and vajra flowers. Likewise he covered them with great clouds of incense, perfumes, garlands, ointments, parasols, victory banners, pennants, powders, garments, ornaments, jewels and vajras. He went also to where the Blessed One Vairocana was, and together with those bodhisattva great beings circumambulated the Blessed One many hundreds of thousands of times. Then, just as he had come, he took his place in the east. There he emanated great multistoried mansions of jewel and vajra, great kings of jewels, vajra staffs, and great lion thrones with lotus hearts, blessed them with the heart-essence of the great jewel vajra, and remained there, filling the ten directions far and wide with the heart-essence of the letters of the great jewel vajra.

In the south, the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Saṃkusumitarājendra blessed the youthful Mañjuśrī with the essence of the heart-essence of the great vajra and declared: "Son of good family, go to that world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence, to pay homage to the Blessed One Vairocana, to behold him and to make offerings to him." Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Saṃkusumitarājendra spoke this heart-essence of the great vajra:

namaḥ smanta mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mahā vajrāgra va raṃ hūṃ

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī received this heart-essence of the supreme vajra, and, with the permission of that Blessed One, surrounded and preceded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, he vanished from that world system. Together with those bodhisattva great beings he went to where that world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence was, to where the great palace of King Vaśavartin was, and to where the Blessed One was, and he illuminated this whole world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence with the great light of the supreme jewel vajra. He illuminated the great palace of King Vaśavartin with flowers of the supreme great vajra, with incense, perfumes, garlands, ointments, powders, garments, parasols, victory banners and pennants, and with the radiant light of the supreme great vajra and the supreme jewel. With the sound of the supreme vajra of great boon-granting he made it known in the ten directions. And having made it known, just as he had come, he took his place in the south and sat, together with those bodhisattva great beings, upon great kings of lotuses arisen from the supreme great vajra.

In the west, the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Amitābha bestowed upon the bodhisattva great being Avalokiteśvara this heart-essence of the supreme lotus of the great vajra, the supreme maṇḍala of the great compassion of all the tathāgatas. Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Amitābha spoke this heart-essence of the power of compassion, the maṇḍala of the supreme lotus of the great vajra:

namaḥ smanta mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarvathā karuṇa bala vajra padmāgra sa

Then the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Amitābha, having spoken this heart-essence and knowing that the time had come for that son of good family, sent the bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara forth, saying, "Go to that world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence, to behold the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Vairocana, and to pay homage to him."

Then the bodhisattva great being, noble Avalokiteśvara, surrounded and preceded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, vanished from that world system. Together with those bodhisattva great beings he came to this world system, went to where the great palace of King Vaśavartin was and to where the Blessed One was, and, having arrived, circumambulated the Blessed One many hundreds of thousands of times. Then, just as he had come, he took his place in the west and sat upon a great lotus, a king of vajras; and through the power of the supreme vajra of great compassion he filled and illuminated this whole great palace of King Vaśavartin and the whole world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence with clouds of the intimations of his own body.

In the north, the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Akṣobhya bestowed upon the bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha this heart-essence that holds the supreme seat of awakening, which overpowers by the great vajra and sports by the great vajra. Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Akṣobhya spoke this heart-essence that holds the supreme great seat of awakening:

namaḥ smanta mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarvathā varda manra a gri gri

Having spoken this, he sent the bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha forth, saying, "Son of good family, go to that world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence, to where the great palace of King Vaśavartin is, to behold the Blessed One Vairocana and to pay homage to him."

Then the bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha vanished from that world system. Surrounded and preceded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, together with those bodhisattva great beings he went to where the world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence was, to where the great palace of King Vaśavartin was, and to where the Blessed One was, and, having arrived, circumambulated the Blessed One many hundreds of thousands of times. Then, just as he had come, he took his place in the north, blessed it as an array of the ornaments of the great seat of awakening, and filled the ten directions far and wide with clouds of bodies of the supreme seat of awakening.

From the northeastern quarter, the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Supreme Jewel Holder bestowed upon the bodhisattva Jewel Crown this heart-essence of the great vajra, the vajra source of the Three Jewels. Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Supreme Jewel Holder spoke this heart-essence of the vajra source of the Three Jewels:

namaḥ smanta mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarvathā oṃ oṃ jaṃ saṃ

Having given him this, he sent him forth, saying, "Son of good family, go to that world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence, to that great palace of King Vaśavartin, to behold the Blessed One Vairocana and to pay homage to him."

Then the bodhisattva Jewel Crown, with the permission of that Blessed One, vanished from that world system. Surrounded and preceded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, together with those bodhisattva great beings he went to where the world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence was, to where the great palace of King Vaśavartin was, and to where the Blessed One Vairocana was. Then, just as he had come, he took his place in the northeastern quarter and sat upon a king of lotuses adorned with the vajra of the Three Jewels, and he filled all the buddha fields far and wide with clouds of the bodies of the Three Jewels.

From the southeastern quarter, the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha called Meghaśrī bestowed upon the bodhisattva Meghagarbha the heart-essence called The Essence of the Supreme Vajra Array of Great Clouds. Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Meghaśrī spoke this heart-essence of the supreme vajra array of great clouds:

namaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarvathā mahā vajra megha vikṝḍite viśva sambhava ha

Having given him this, he sent him forth, saying, "Son of good family, go to that world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence, to that great palace of King Vaśavartin, to behold the Blessed One Vairocana and to pay homage to him."

Then the bodhisattva Meghagarbha vanished from that world system. Surrounded and preceded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, together with those bodhisattva great beings he went to where the Blessed One was and circumambulated the Blessed One many hundreds of thousands of times. Then, just as he had come, he took his place in the southeastern quarter. With clouds of the bodily intimations of the supreme vajra of great clouds he blessed clouds of great kings of lotuses, and he remained there, filling all the world systems far and wide with clouds of supreme great offerings.

From the southwestern quarter, the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Mahāvajraketu bestowed upon the bodhisattva great being Vajravīra the heart-essence arisen from the vajra. Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Vajraketu spoke this heart-essence arisen from the great vajra:

namaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarvathā vajroddhube svāhā

Having given him this, he sent him forth. Then the bodhisattva Vajravīra vanished from that world system. Surrounded and preceded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, together with those bodhisattva great beings he went to where the world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence was, to where the palace of King Vaśavartin was, and to where the Blessed One Vairocana was, and he circumambulated the Blessed One many hundreds of thousands of times. Then, just as he had come, he took his place in the southwestern quarter, filled all the world systems far and wide with ornaments of the great vajra arisen from the great vajra, and sat upon a great vajra lotus adorned with vajra forms.

From the northwestern quarter, the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Sughoṣa bestowed upon the bodhisattva Thunder King the heart-essence that proclaims the vajra voice. Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Sughoṣa, he of the most melodious voice, spoke this heart-essence of the vajra letters:

namaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra rakṣa nirghoṣa vikrānta svāhā

Having given him this, he sent him forth. Then the bodhisattva great being, the great Thunder King, with the permission of that Blessed One, vanished from that world system. Surrounded and preceded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, together with those bodhisattva great beings he went to where the world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence was, to where the great palace of King Vaśavartin was, and to where the Blessed One Vairocana was. Then, just as he had come, he took his place in the northwestern quarter; with endless melodious chants he made this whole buddha field hear, and he sat upon a great jewel lotus that had come forth from his own aspirations.

From the nadir, the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Essence of the Pure Jewel Lotus spoke to the bodhisattva Lotus Limb this vajra heart-essence of the king of great conduct:

namaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarvathā haṃ he svāhā

Having given him this, he sent him forth. Then the bodhisattva Lotus Limb, with the permission of that Blessed One, vanished from that world system. Surrounded and preceded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, together with those bodhisattva great beings he went to where the world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence was, to where the great palace of King Vaśavartin was, and to where the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Vairocana was, and he circumambulated the Blessed One many hundreds of thousands of times. Then, just as he had come, he took his place at the nadir. He filled every world system far and wide with manifold multistoried mansions of great kings of lotuses, and he sat upon a great king of lotuses, the heart of a stainless maṇḍala.

From the zenith, the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Jinākara bestowed upon the bodhisattva Utterly Victorious in Battle the heart-essence of the power of awakening. Then the Tathāgata Jinākara spoke this heart-essence of the power of awakening:

namaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarvathā bodhyaṃ kara dharmma svāhā

Having given him this, he sent him forth. Then the bodhisattva Victorious in Battle, with the permission of that Blessed One, vanished from that world system. Surrounded and preceded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of bodhisattvas, together with those bodhisattva great beings he went to where the world system arrayed with the ornament of the Flower-Ground Essence was, to where the great palace of King Vaśavartin was, and to where the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the Perfectly Complete Buddha Vairocana was, and he circumambulated the Blessed One many hundreds of thousands of times. Then, just as he had come, he took his place at the zenith. Making known the boundless filling of the realm of phenomena far and wide, he filled all the world systems far and wide, and he sat upon a great lotus arisen from the nature of reality.

Then those bodhisattvas who had gathered from the world systems of the ten directions and taken their places saw, in these fields of the Blessed One Vairocana, arrays of the qualities of a buddha field never seen before. They saw, too, the Blessed One Vairocana, supremely radiant, brilliant and resplendent, free of all thought and conceptuality, primordially at peace like space, fulfilling every wish like a great king of precious wish-fulfilling jewels; and, filled with wonder, with a single voice they spoke these verses:

"Having cast off all conceptions,
Primordially at peace and unborn,
This wondrous and sublime Protector,
How is it that he appears upon the earth?

He performs no activity with his body,
Nor does he act with speech or mind,
Yet he is endowed with the thirty-two marks,
Like utterly pure crystal.

Though with his body he performs
The deeds of a buddha for all beings,
There is no change in his body:
That Sage is free of change.

His speech is like an echo;
Though it cannot be shown and has no form,
Like the great drum of the gods
It satisfies all beings.

His mind is free of conceit,
And for it there are no objects;
Yet though it is free of objects,
It has become the object of beings.

Just so his speech, just so those beings,
Just so his body, just so his mind:
Fully endowed with a single characteristic,
He appears supreme among those who speak.

Since there exists no Tathāgata
Released from the unborn,
Those who know non-arising
Are the ones who know the Guide.

The Protector is ever in meditative equipoise;
Though he is free of body, speech and mind,
Constantly, in the sphere of body and speech,
The Sage appears without cease.

Great bodhisattvas,
Who perform the hardest of deeds,
Search everywhere for such
A great blazing wisdom of the Buddha.

In it the eye does not appear,
Nor is there ear or nose;
Tongue, body and mind as well:
Therefore it is not their object.

It is not form, nor is it sound,
It is not smell, nor taste, nor touch;
It is not faculty, nor consciousness:
It is always at peace, in every respect.

Subject and those objects,
When examined, do not exist;
Since the subject does not exist,
Objects cannot come to be.

When things do not exist
In the way the childish imagine them,
Then the Protector comes to exist,
In the manner of what is unimagined.

He, everywhere and for all,
Fulfills their wishes just as they are;
The Teacher, like a wish-fulfilling jewel,
Is present before everyone.

In the manner of a reflection arising,
For those who have mastered samādhi,
By sight, by speech and by every conversation,
He becomes fit for all to enjoy.

Having seen the Protector Vairocana,
At that moment all attained
Samādhi, dhāraṇī, knowledge and liberation,
Inconceivable, all of them.

Having gained mastery over dhāraṇī,
Seeking the wisdom of the Buddha,
All of us without exception
Have come here from the ten directions.

The dhāraṇī of the perfectly complete Buddha
Is the supreme among dhāraṇīs:
May we truly attain it,
Perfectly complete awakening!"

Then, having spoken those verses, those bodhisattvas, since the body has no former limit and no later limit, and likewise speech and mind have no former limit and no later limit, entered into whatever is the body, speech and mind of the Blessed One Vairocana. Why is that? Because through fully understanding essential nature, no former limit or later limit of the body is observed; and likewise, through fully understanding essential nature, no former limit of speech and mind is observed.

From The Maṇḍala of the Empowerment of Vajrapāṇi, this is the first chapter, the introduction.

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Vairocana, knowing those bodhisattvas who had gathered from the world systems of the ten directions, and knowing too the bodhisattvas seated there who dwelt in the great palace of King Vaśavartin, entered into absorption in the samādhi of empowering the deeds of a buddha, called the Essence of the Ornament of the Array of Illumination, the samādhi by whose power the tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas, in all world systems and in all the maṇḍalas of buddha assemblies, in an instant, a moment, a brief while, display everything from the first generation of the mind up to the culmination of the great parinirvāṇa.

As soon as the Blessed One had entered absorption, those bodhisattva great beings saw the following. In this world system, in all the boundless world systems, in all the arrays of world systems joined one to another, in all other world systems, in all the four-continent world systems, in all the thousandfold world systems, in all the two-thousandfold world systems, in all that belongs to the world systems of the great trichiliocosm, in all the oceans of world systems, in all the established world systems, and here in this world system adorned with the array of the Flower-Ground Essence, the Blessed One Vairocana, through various arrangements of sounds and verbal expressions suited to beings of various dispositions, various hopes, various desires, various modes of vehicle in which they abide, various destinies, various places of birth, various lands, various colors, various shapes, and various kinds, fully displayed the deeds of a buddha, from the first generation of the mind up to the culmination of the great parinirvāṇa. By a buddha form arising from the maṇḍala of the wheel of his own speech and empowered by the ornament of the array of the buddha voice, he fully empowered the bodhisattva great being Samantabhadra. So that the great vajra maṇḍala would be fruitful, so that the teaching would endure, and so that the deeds of a buddha would be handed down from one to the next without interruption, he empowered him to take up births one after another as time required. And they saw him confer the great empowerment of Vajrapāṇi in the maṇḍala of the great vajra enclosure, through the great empowerment of vajra wisdom, the great vajra essence, the illumination that pervades everything with the radiating light of the great vajra, the conduct of the Great Vehicle, and the gateway of pure secret mantra.

Thus the Blessed One Vairocana, being free of all conceptual elaboration, is everywhere without elaboration. Being free of all conceptual thought, he is nonconceptual. Being free of essential nature, he is empty. Being pure from the very beginning, he is utterly pure. Being free of arising, he is unborn. Having utterly transcended the domain of thought, he is inconceivable. Being free of objects, he is without object. Having utterly transcended the path of words, he is inexpressible. On the ground where all sentient beings in every way come together, like the precious wish-fulfilling jewel of the great bodhisattvas, he accords with all, fulfilling every wish; in all world systems, in the form of reflections, he is present before every sentient being. Yet, like a great and priceless jewel wrapped in a soiled cloth, beings do not recognize him, asking, "Whose is this? What is this?" How astonishing this is!

Those whose intelligence is pure, free of hesitation and doubt, who have served the victors of the past, who have generated roots of virtue, who have first generated the awakening mind in the way of secret mantra and conduct of the Great Vehicle, who are blessed by all the vajra holders, and who have been illuminated by the Blessed One Vairocana with the great light rays of the Essence of the Ornament of the Great Vairocana, have entered the tathāgatagarbha. They have the good fortune to hear and to train in this way of secret mantra conduct, to draw the great vajra maṇḍala, and to accomplish the great siddhi.

Then, in this Sahā world system, the Blessed One, under the name Śākyamuni, in Jambudvīpa of the middle four-continent world system, defeated Māra and attained manifest, perfect, complete awakening. Beginning from his dwelling in the abode of Tuṣita, he gradually carried out his deeds, and empowering them up to the culmination of the great parinirvāṇa, he taught the Dharma. That Blessed One Śākyamuni, too, so that the teaching would not be interrupted, gathered the bodhisattvas, the bodhisattva Samantabhadra, Maitreya, Mañjuśrī, Avalokiteśvara, Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin, Ākāśagarbha, Kṣitigarbha, the bodhisattva Sarvāpāyajaha, and others. Then, in the presence of all, the gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, and the rest, he drew the great vajra maṇḍala, empowered the bodhisattva Samantabhadra by conferring the great empowerment of Vajrapāṇi, and entrusted the teaching to him.

From The Great Maṇḍala of the Empowerment of Vajrapāṇi, this is the second chapter, called the Account of the Perfect Accomplishment of Former Conduct.

The Noble Great Tantra of the Empowerment of Vajrapāṇi, second fascicle.

Thus did I hear at one time. The Blessed One was staying in the great city of Glorious Source of Qualities, in the supreme great forest, the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, a forest blessed as the outcome in kind of the play of the former conduct of all the tathāgatas. He was there together with a great Saṅgha of monks, twenty-two thousand five hundred monks. All of them had exhausted the defilements, were free of afflictions, and had gone to the far shore of supreme mastery over the mind in every respect. They were the venerable Subhūti, the venerable Gavāṃpati, the venerable Great Power, the venerable Madhyāntika, the venerable Mahākāśyapa, the venerable Mahāmaudgalyāyana, the venerable Nanda, the venerable Upananda, the venerable Bhadra, the venerable Rāhula, the venerable Aniruddha, the venerable Mahākapphiṇa, the venerable Rāṣṭrapāla, the venerable Gayākāśyapa, the venerable Uruvilvākāśyapa, the venerable Great Tooth, the venerable Mahākauṣṭhila, the venerable Sundarananda, the venerable Dravya Mallaputra, the venerable Khadiravanika, the venerable Piṇḍola Bhāradvāja, the venerable Kāśyapa Son of the Lord of Conduct, the venerable Close Union, the venerable Kumārakāśyapa, the venerable Cūḍapanthaka, the venerable Śāriputra, and others, twenty-two thousand five hundred monks in all.

There was also a great assembly of bodhisattvas, namely: the youthful Mañjuśrī, the youthful Jālinīprabha, the bodhisattva Ratnākara, the bodhisattva Ratnamati, the bodhisattva Ratnapāṇi, the bodhisattva Precious Seal in Hand, the bodhisattva Ever Diligently Abiding in the Dharma, the bodhisattva Distinguisher of the Words of All Dharmas, the bodhisattva Pacifier of All Torments, the bodhisattva Firmly Steadfast Intelligence, the bodhisattva Benefactor through Superior Intention, the bodhisattva Compassionate One, the bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara, the bodhisattva Mahāsthāmaprāpta, the bodhisattva Gandhahastin, the bodhisattva Gaganagañja, the bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha, the bodhisattva Ākāśagarbha, the bodhisattva Thunder King, the bodhisattva Gadgadasvara, the bodhisattva Vimalamati, the bodhisattva Inexhaustible Intelligence in Conduct, the bodhisattva Intelligence in Conduct, the bodhisattva Supreme Intelligence, the bodhisattva Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin, the bodhisattva Maitreya, the bodhisattva great being Samantabhadra, and others: bodhisattva great beings as numerous as the atoms in ten buddha fields, together with the sixteen excellent beings, Bhadrapāla and the rest. All of them had gone to the far shore of the bodhisattva grounds and perfections, of the dhāraṇīs, the powers, the factors of awakening, and the noble path, of the attainment of mastery, and of supreme mastery over the mind in every respect. They satisfied all sentient beings according to their wishes; they had gone forth in accordance with their former aspirations; they were skilled in making offerings and rendering service to all the tathāgatas; and they were skilled in seeking the sacred Dharma constantly and without interruption.

Surrounded and faced by such bodhisattva great beings, the Blessed One sat upon a great lion throne with a lotus heart, outshining the world with its gods, utterly radiant, brilliant, and resplendent.

The divine sons of the Pure Abodes also gathered and sat there. Brahmā, lord of the Sahā world, surrounded on all sides by the divine sons of the Brahmakāyika realm, also gathered and sat there. Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods, surrounded and faced by many hundreds of thousands of divine sons, also gathered in that assembly and sat there.

The asura lords Deeply Content, Vemacitrin, Supreme Field, Baṭi, Great Drum, Kālacakra, Vimala, Crest, Moon Face, Destroyer, Profound One, Bali, Rāhu, Proud Crest, Virocana, the asura lord Garlanded with Variety, and others, together with a thousand other asura lords, all these asura lords came forth from their own abodes. Through the great might and great magical power of the asuras they emanated flowers, fragrances, garlands, incense, perfumed ointments, powders, garments, parasols, victory banners, pennants, ornaments, cymbals, and clappers. They went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Śākyamuni was, and having arrived, they scattered over the Blessed One all the flowers, incense, fragrances, garlands, ointments, powders, garments, parasols, victory banners, pennants, ornaments, cymbals, and clappers that they had emanated. Having scattered them, they bowed to the Blessed One and sat down to one side.

The nāga king Sāgara, the nāga king Anavatapta, the nāga king Hundred Rays, the nāga king Lightning Tongue, the nāga king Jewel Crown, the nāga king Takṣaka, the nāga king Without Chaff, the nāga king Vāsuki, the nāga king Moving by Strength, the nāga king Cropped Ear, the nāga king Lotus Bearer, the nāga king Śaṅkhapāla, the nāga king Mahāpadma, the nāga king Kālika, the nāga king Kulika, the nāga king One Head, the nāga king Deceiver, the nāga king Voice of Indra, the nāga king Mighty One, and others, ten thousand nāga kings, came forth from their own abodes. Through the great might and great magical power of the nāgas they emanated clouds of water scented with gośīrṣa sandalwood, and went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they caused the clouds of scented water they had emanated to rain down upon the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees. Having made it rain, they bowed to the Blessed One and sat down to one side.

The great yakṣa general Vaiśravaṇa, the great yakṣa general Āṭavaka, the great yakṣa general Dhṛtarāṣṭra, the great yakṣa general Virūḍhaka, the great yakṣa general Virūpākṣa, the great yakṣa general Pūrṇabhadra, the great yakṣa general Maṇibhadra, the great yakṣa general Lord of Waters Who Holds the Confounder, the great yakṣa general Hundred Hands, the great yakṣa general Pāñcika, and others, ten thousand great yakṣa generals, came forth from their own abodes. Carrying flowers, incense, fragrances, garlands, ointments, powders, garments, parasols, victory banners, pennants, and cymbals, they went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they scattered over the Blessed One the flowers, incense, fragrances, garlands, ointments, powders, garments, parasols, victory banners, and pennants they had brought. Having scattered them, they bowed to the Blessed One and sat down to one side.

Druma, king of the kinnaras, with a retinue of many kinnaras, many hundreds of thousands of them carrying many kinds of kinnara flowers, incense, fragrances, garlands, ointments, powders, garments, parasols, victory banners, pennants, cymbals, and clappers, also came forth from their own abodes and went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they scattered over the Blessed One the flowers, incense, fragrances, garlands, ointments, powders, garments, parasols, victory banners, pennants, cymbals, and clappers they had brought. Having scattered them, they bowed to the Blessed One and sat to one side.

The garuḍa lord Vainateya, the garuḍa lord Terrible Goer, the garuḍa lord Hundred Feathers, and the garuḍa lord Crested One, with a retinue of many hundreds of thousands of garuḍas, also went to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed to the Blessed One and sat down to one side.

The lord of rākṣasīs Wrathful Face, the lord of rākṣasīs Fine Fangs, the lord of rākṣasīs Fierce One, Very Violent, Terrifying to Behold, Frightener, Great Terrifier, Ten-Faced, Yama, Bell of Yama, Utterly Terrifying to Behold, and Moon Host, with a retinue of many rākṣasas, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

The great sage Fire, with many sages, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, he bowed his head at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

The winds, Garlanded with Clouds, the Destroying Wind, the Forming Wind, the All-Sustaining Wind, the Scattering Wind, the Timely Wind, the Scattering Gust, the Gale, and the World-Upholding Wind, also went to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed to the Blessed One and sat down to one side.

The Moon, king of the stars, with a retinue of many hundreds of thousands of stars, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of great sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, he bowed his head at the Blessed One's feet and sat to one side.

The Sun Garlanded with Rays, the Heater, the Drier, the Smoke-Maker, the Blazing One, and the Sun Delighter, with a retinue of many hundreds of thousands of divine sons of exalted splendor, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

Varuṇa, king of the nāgas, with a retinue of many nāga kings, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, he bowed his head at the Blessed One's feet and sat to one side.

Viṣṇu with his retinue also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, he bowed his head at the Blessed One's feet and sat to one side.

Rudra, with a retinue of hungry ghosts, also went to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, he bowed to the Blessed One and sat down to one side.

Īśāna, lord of the bhūtas, with a retinue of many hundreds of thousands of bhūtas, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, he bowed his head at the Blessed One's feet and sat to one side.

The great king of the planets, the Son of the Pleiades, with a retinue of many hundreds of thousands of planets, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, he bowed his head at the Blessed One's feet and sat to one side.

The cloud gods, the divine son called Gray One, the divine son called Lightning Garland, the divine son called Destroyer of Dense Nets of Cloud, and the divine son called Rain-Bringer, with a retinue of many hundreds of thousands of divine sons, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

The lightnings called Thickener, Splendid One, Dense Light, Lightning Light, and Beautiful Garland, with a retinue of many hundreds of thousands of lightnings, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

Sumeru, king of mountains, the mountain king Gandhamādana, the mountain king Himālaya, the mountain king Vindhya, the mountain king Malaya, the mountain king Mahendra, the mountain king Enduring, the mountain king Hundred Peaks, the mountain king Citrakūṭa, the mountain king Vidyādhara, and other mountain kings too went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

Yama, Kālarātrī, the Bearer of the Black Club, the Black Man, the Lord of Death, Glory-Giver, and Kālakarṇī also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

She Who Repels Austerity, Cāmuṇḍā, the wife of Rudra, the wife of Brahmā, the wife of Viṣṇu, the wife of Kumāra, the wife of Kubera, the wife of the Boar, and the wife of the Great Terrifier also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

The great river Gaṅgā, the great river Sītā, the great river Sarasvatī, the great river Vidastā, the great river Candrabhāgā, the great river Yamunā, the great river Viśvāmitrā, the great river Vegavatī, the great river Lake-Reliant, the great river Godāvarī, and the great river Giver of Love also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

The gandharva king Garlanded with Variety, the gandharva king Ravisher, the gandharva king Great Force, the gandharva king Vanquisher, the gandharva king Water-Born, the gandharva king Utpala-Eyed, and the gandharva king Sandalwood Heap also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

The vidyādhara king Crest, the vidyādhara king Lesser Crest, the vidyādhara king Very Fierce, the vidyādhara king Proud One, the vidyādhara king Holder of Force, the vidyādhara king Citrasena, these vidyādhara kings and others too, with a retinue of many hundreds of thousands of vidyādhara kings, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

The rākṣasī called Toothed One, the rākṣasī called Distractor, the rākṣasī called Night-Roamer, the rākṣasī called Gnasher, the rākṣasī called Bewilderer, the rākṣasī called Wild One, the rākṣasī called Vajra Belly, the rākṣasī called Pot Belly, the rākṣasī called Copper Wing, the rākṣasī called Lightning Tongue, the rākṣasī called Puñcale, the rākṣasī called She-Ass, the rākṣasī called Dreadful One, and the rākṣasī called Vajra Nails, these great rākṣasīs and others, with a retinue of many rākṣasīs, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

The bhūta king Spear Bearer, the bhūta king Plough Bearer, the bhūta king Pestle Bearer, the bhūta king Plough Holder, the bhūta king Summoner, and the bhūta king Stiff Neck, with a retinue of many hundreds of thousands of bhūtas, also went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

All the piśācas, all the vitality-stealers, all the oppressors, all the shadow-oppressors, all the hungry ghosts, all the māras, all the gods of Māra's class, and the mighty Gaṇapati, all of these went to the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees, to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side. The four rākṣasīs, too, came from the four directions, gathered together, and went to where the Blessed One was. Having arrived, they bowed their heads at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side.

At that time the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees became exceedingly vast and wide, and soft to the touch like kācalindika cloth. Its ground was laid out in a checkerboard of vajra, beryl, and musāragalva. It was beautified with gateways, porticoes, and windows on both sides; above, a net of great jeweled bells and tinkling chimes was spread; many pearl necklaces were hung; many silk tassels were spread; cymbals and clappers gave forth sweet and pleasing sounds; and the whole area was strewn with divine flowers, utpalas, kumudas, white lotuses, and supreme fragrances. Thus, through the blessing of the Buddha and the miraculous power of the Buddha, the entire dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees was encircled by the ring of a great vajra enclosure and became the courtyard of a vajra maṇḍala, a million leagues in length and breadth, beautifully adorned with flowers of every season, its every quarter filled with divine water possessing the eight qualities, arising from the karma of bodhisattvas. Just as the abode of the king of the Paranirmitavaśavartin gods, arising from pure karma, abides radiant and resplendent, so too the dense grove adorned with banners of many kinds of sal trees shone in brilliant beauty through the power of the Blessed One.

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Śākyamuni, entered absorption in the samādhi called Vajra Source. As soon as the Blessed One had entered meditative equipoise, all the tathāgatas uttered the word "Excellent!" and spoke this heart-mantra called Vajra Source:

"Excellent, excellent, great hero,
Vajra Source of mighty strength!
Over this maṇḍala of the assembly
Of Śākyasiṃha, the protector,
Great vajra strength, keep guard!
namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ svahā"

As soon as this supreme heart-mantra of the Vajra Source was spoken, throughout all the maṇḍalas of the assembly and all the boundless world systems, the heart-mantra of the Vajra Source resounded with a roar, one sound after another, and this was heard:

"Vajra Source, great hero,
Benefactor of all beings!
This maṇḍala of the assembly
Is blessed by the Great Sage.
May all living creatures always
Hold this heart-mantra,
And thereby may all of them here
Attain the siddhi of the great way!"

Then the Blessed One Śākyamuni looked upon the whole assembly and said to the bodhisattvas Samantabhadra, Maitreya, Mañjuśrī, and the others: "Sons of good family, there is the supreme secret called the Maṇḍala of the Empowerment of Great Vajra Wisdom, the Conferral of the Great Empowerment of Vajrapāṇi. It is the former bodhisattva conduct of the Blessed One Vairocana, called the Essence of the Treasury That Enters the Gateway of Purity; it is the entry into secret mantra, the gateway of secret mantra that follows bodhisattva conduct; it is the abode of the Great Vehicle of the bodhisattvas, the secret maṇḍala called the Essence of the Empowerment of Vajra Wisdom, which fully reveals the wisdom of omniscience; like a king of wish-fulfilling jewels, it bears abundant fruit at once; it is the great secret of the bodhisattvas, well spoken by bodhisattvas; it is the magical display of the bodhisattvas, the play of bodhisattva illusion, the samaya of all the tathāgatas. Even all the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas do not fully know it, so what need is there to speak of other beings knowing it? Only bodhisattva great beings who do not depend on others, who are born into the family of the tathāgatas, and who are blessed by all the tathāgatas are the exception."

Then the whole assembly, together with the bodhisattva Samantabhadra and the others, spoke with one voice: "Blessed One, this is the time. Sugata, this is the occasion."

Then the Blessed One entered absorption in the samādhi called the Essence of Accomplishing the Full Display of Former Conduct, and spoke this king of secret mantras, the gateway of purified former conduct, the source of the great vajra:

namaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarvathā khaṃ

As soon as the king of secret mantras had been spoken, the whole assembly, their hearts filled with wonder, spoke with one voice:

"Ah, how great the joy of wisdom!
The supreme bliss of concentration is attained!
All have been brought under its power."

Then the Blessed One, knowing that the whole assembly was single-pointedly absorbed in wonder, sent forth from the śrīvatsa on his chest a light ray called Exhorting All Bodhisattvas. Having gone forth, it illuminated all the abodes of the bodhisattvas, and then it entered back into the Blessed One's śrīvatsa.

At that time the bodhisattva, the great being called Samantabhadra, had made the aspiration to display the former conduct of the Blessed One Vairocana throughout this entire world system called Kusumatalagarbhavyūhālaṃkāra, the Array of Ornaments of the Flower-Ground Essence. Having taken up the power to tame the yakṣas, he was dwelling here in the central Jambudvīpa of the Sahā world, in Alakāvatī, in the abode of the great yakṣa general Āṭavaka. Illuminated by the radiance of that light ray, his eyes and face grew wide with joy, and he gave voice to this inspired utterance; then, bringing to mind the very inconceivability of the Tathāgata, he remained silent.

Then the Blessed One at that time made a sign to the bodhisattva Samantabhadra to come, and he also spoke these verses:

"Pure being, like the moon, come here!
Blessed by the Tathāgata, bent on awakening,
You who have fully realized and understood inconceivable sameness,
Ever grateful to the buddhas, come here!
Ever abiding in the sameness of the three times,
Vajrasattva, supreme vajra, come here!
However many buddhas there are in the ten directions,
Who act for the sake of beings and possess inconceivable forms,
You go before them all in their aims:
Lord of Secrets, supreme being, come here!
Sublime secret one, essence of beings, come here!
You who ever tame the hard to tame, who have gained power, come here!
You are born of what has steadfastly arisen.
Knower of the right time and measure, come here!
You who have attained the sublime, come here!
Bestower of the sublime, come here!
Maker of glory, gatherer of glory, glorious one,
Son of the Sugata, come here!
Excellent being of great strength,
Like a vajra, come here!
Fearless son of the buddhas,
Supreme among the strong, come here!
You who ever gladden the hearts
Of the three realms of existence, come here!
Teacher of the true path,
Abiding in the ten powers, come here!
Pure being of great miraculous power,
Samantabhadra, come here!
All-beautiful one, come here!
Lord of the three realms of existence, come here!
You of lovely form, richly adorned,
You with the ears of an elephant, come here!
You of manifold forms, free from delusion,
A delight to behold, come here!
You whose form soars above beings, come here!
Come here, Nāḍakubera!
Ever wearing garlands of many-colored cloth,
With diadem and necklace, come here!
You who have gained fearlessness, come here!
Terrifier of terrors, come here!
Son of the buddhas, of great miraculous power,
Do not delay, come here!"

Then the Blessed One, having spoken these verses, uttered the queen of vidyā-mantras, the Door of Fearlessness for one who comes:

namaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathagātebhyaḥ sarvathā a bismaye vaiśaradya mukhe bhagabati vajra amalini mābilaṃba svāhā

Having declared this queen of vidyā-mantras, the Door of Fearlessness, the Blessed One remained silent.

Then, urged by the Blessed One through this queen of vidyā-mantras, the bodhisattva Samantabhadra said to the whole host of yakṣas, "Friends, come!" And in order to see that Blessed One, to pay homage to him and to attend upon him, he set out, surrounded by a host of many bodhisattvas, encircled by many hundreds of thousands of śrāvakas, and wholly surrounded and escorted in front by many thousands of koṭis of gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans and nonhumans, toward the dense forest grove adorned with banners of various sāla trees, where the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Śākyamuni was dwelling.

Then that host of yakṣas and the young yakṣas spoke with one voice: "As the great being has commanded, we go to the dense forest grove adorned with banners of various sāla trees, in order to see the Blessed One Śākyamuni, to pay homage to him and to attend upon him, and also to see, pay homage to and attend upon that assembly, complete in every respect."

Then at that time the bodhisattva Samantabhadra entered into the meditative absorption called Various Ornaments, which displays the natural outflow of all conduct.

As soon as the bodhisattva Samantabhadra had entered that meditative absorption, then, in that very instant, there came into being the pavilion called Vairocana's Array of Ornaments. It was as vast and wide as a great trichiliocosm world system. Many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of pillars of great jewels were set up in it. It was adorned with many gateways, porticoes and terraces, and embellished with work in Jambū River gold, fashioned as if into a single sāla tree. It was beautified along all its edges with rows like doves, and hung with nets of both kinds of pearls. Nets of jeweled bells and little bells encircled the sky above it, and it was beautified by many hanging tassels of divine fine cloth and silk. Divine cymbals and chime-stones gave forth sweet and delightful sounds. It was filled with hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of divine maidens who sang, danced, played music and spoke sweetly, adorned with bracelets, earrings and armlets, and possessed of divine bodies. Canopies of great jewels were spread over it everywhere, and its sides were finely embellished with great beryl. It was well adorned with flowering trees of every season, soft to the touch like kācilindika cloth, and embellished with rows of stairways of divine jewels. Geese, swans, ruddy shelducks, jīvaṃjīvaka birds, parrots, mynas, peacocks, cranes and many other flocks of birds gave voice there. It was beautified all around by ponds for the bodhisattvas to enjoy, filled with divine flowers, utpalas, kumudas, white lotuses, saugandhikas, lotuses and kalarakas, and with water endowed with the eight qualities, their beds silted with the mud of divine uragasāra sandalwood. Constantly and without interruption, a rain of divine flowers fell upon that pavilion: māndārava, paruṣaka and great paruṣaka flowers, earth-piercing flowers and great earth-piercing flowers. It was made beautiful by being covered with many clouds of the smoke of black aloewood incense.

Within that pavilion he emanated the individual abodes of all the bodhisattvas, produced by their karma, each just as they had intended. In each of those pavilions, moreover, were set sixteen wish-fulfilling jewels, beginning with the Powerful King of Jewels, and great precious jewels, beginning with the King of Jewels, to be enjoyed just as one wishes, which fulfill the wishes of all sentient beings. All the pavilions of Vairocana's Array of Ornaments were beautified with raised parasols, victory banners and pennants. In those pavilions he also emanated lion thrones with lotus hearts. All the pavilions, too, had the nature of great, clear beryl. Just as, for example, a well-polished mirror is, so the pavilion of Vairocana's Array of Ornaments was by its nature pure and clear; and because the beryl was clear, all the world systems of the great trichiliocosm appeared in it as reflections.

In the center he emanated a lion throne for the Blessed One, made of the king of jewels called Essence of the Array of All Beings. He emanated clouds of all the requisites that sustain sentient beings, raining down constantly everywhere in that pavilion. This was so because the bodhisattva Samantabhadra had practiced the ocean of the Blessed One Vairocana's former conduct.

Having thus emanated it in order to make offerings to the Blessed One, he set out in order to see the Blessed One, to pay homage to him, to attend upon him and to hear the Dharma, together with the great host of yakṣas, surrounded and escorted in front by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of young yakṣas. Through a bodhisattva's great miraculous power and a bodhisattva's great might, he took up that pavilion with both his hands, came down through the sky, and went to where the dense forest grove adorned with banners of various sāla trees was, where the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Śākyamuni was.

The light of that pavilion filled this whole dense forest grove adorned with banners of various sāla trees with a great illumination. The gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans and nonhumans who had gathered and were seated in that assembly were lit up by that light, and when they looked about in the four directions, they saw in the sky above, six million eight hundred thousand yojanas up, something blazing like a great mass of fire, gleaming like a thousand suns, splendid and beautiful. Having seen it, their hearts filled with wonder, they looked at one another and sat in silence.

Those bodhisattva great beings who had attained dhāraṇī, attained patience and attained the powers also looked, and saw it blazing like a great mass of fire, gleaming like a thousand suns, splendid and beautiful. Their hearts too filled with wonder, they asked the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Śākyamuni, "Blessed One, what is this that gleams, glows and shines in the sky six million eight hundred thousand yojanas above here, Blessed One, so hard to fathom and hard to describe?"

When they had asked this, the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Śākyamuni declared to those bodhisattvas and that great assembly of gods: "Sons of noble family, this is the bodhisattva great being called Samantabhadra, who has come to see me, to pay homage to me, to make offerings to me, to attend upon me and to hear the Dharma. In order to make offerings to me he has emanated the pavilion called Vairocana's Array of Ornaments. It is a miracle displaying the conduct of all the tathāgatas, the natural outflow of accomplishing former conduct. What gleams, glows and shines there is the light of that thing whose qualities could not be fathomed to their end even in a hundred eons."

"And this bodhisattva great being Samantabhadra will inquire fully into the Dharma discourse called The Pure Abode, the Door of Entry into the Great Vehicle."

As soon as the Blessed One had spoken this reply, the bodhisattva great being Samantabhadra, remaining in the sky at the height of about seven tāla trees, emanated clouds of divine flowers, incense, perfumes, garlands, ointments, powders, garments, parasols, victory banners, pennants and chime-stones, and rained them down over the whole dense forest grove adorned with banners of various sāla trees. Then he descended from the sky above and offered that pavilion to the Blessed One. Having offered it, he praised the Blessed One:

"Through knowing body, speech and mind,
You whose discipline is pure and excellent,
Wholly pure of every conception,
Pure being, homage to you!
With the eye, and likewise the ear,
With the nose, and likewise the tongue,
With the body, and likewise the mind,
I pay homage to the supreme of speakers.
Liberated from every thing
That the subject seeks, form, sound, smell, taste and touch,
To the supreme Victor
I pay homage.
Primordially at peace and unborn,
Through body, speech, mind and the rest,
Like a reflection in the world,
O Lord, you ever take delight.
You are not a sentient being, not a life;
You are not a person, not a ground of all;
You have not arisen from time or from nature;
You are not 'how,' not 'what.'
You are merely dependently arisen;
Like an illusion, you have left behind being an entity.
That phenomena are like illusions
You have understood, supreme among two-legged beings.
You are empty of essential nature,
And emptiness too does not exist;
Liberated from empty and not empty,
Homage to you, who are like space!"

Then the bodhisattva great being Samantabhadra, having praised the Blessed One, circumambulated the Blessed One many hundreds of thousands of times together with his retinue, bowed his head at the Blessed One's feet, and sat down to one side, gazing at the Blessed One.

Then from that pavilion, from those arrays, and from those cymbals and chime-stones, one after another, the sound of the initial conduct of the Great Vehicle rang out, resounding:

"Primordially at peace and without characteristics,
The nature of reality is solely pure.
Abandon your doubts and suspicions,
And oh, strive in this!
This abode of the Great Vehicle
Is the unsurpassed former conduct.
This king of conduct, of great happiness,
May all fully attain it!
Through the cause of wholly purifying
The oceans of former conduct,
The Victor Vairocana
Is ever beautiful here: behold!
The Teacher does not abide anywhere,
And though he does not abide anywhere in conduct,
Those who have trained in the conducts
Will come to abide in them all.
The Lord of beings does not appear
To the eye, likewise to the ear,
To the nose, to the tongue,
Likewise to the body, or to the mind.
When the conduct of first arising
Has been trained in,
Then the Blessed One Vairocana
Is constantly seen by the yogin.
Not far and not near,
Not inside and not outside,
Abiding always like space,
The great one appears to the yogin."

As soon as the bodhisattva great being Samantabhadra had heard these verses, he rose from his seat, draped his upper robe over one shoulder, set his right knee on the ground, bowed with palms joined toward the Blessed One, and said to the Blessed One, "If the Blessed One would grant me the opportunity to ask a question and have it answered, I would ask the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha about a few points."

When he had said this, the Blessed One said to the bodhisattva Samantabhadra, "Son of noble family, ask whatever you wish, and I shall satisfy your mind by answering each thing you ask."

When he had said this, the bodhisattva great being Samantabhadra said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, what is called 'entering the Great Vehicle, entering the Great Vehicle': of what is this a designation? What is the conduct of entering the Great Vehicle like? And who has realized and understood that conduct?"

When he had asked this, the Blessed One gave the bodhisattva Samantabhadra his approval: "Excellent, great being, excellent! Great being, it is good that you ask the Tathāgata about this matter. Therefore listen well and closely, and keep it in mind, and I shall explain entering the Great Vehicle and the abode of entering the Great Vehicle.

"Son of noble family, here 'Great Vehicle' is a designation for the inconceivable nature of reality. Its entry is awakening. Son of noble family, 'abode' means the ground. It is, moreover, conduct preceded by great compassion: taking up the power to tame all sentient beings, it displays its forms according to place and time.

"Further, son of noble family, the Great Vehicle is of two kinds: that distinguished as conditioned and that distinguished as unconditioned. Of these, what is distinguished as conditioned is conduct in its nature. The Great Vehicle itself, distinguished as unconditioned, is the Great Vehicle.

"Further, son of noble family, the conditioned is what is produced by conditions; it has the characteristic of dependent arising. Son of noble family, the unconditioned is the unborn nature of reality, and emptiness, its own essential nature.

"Further, son of noble family, what is the Great Vehicle that is conduct in nature? Having made emptiness manifest, the tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas, according to place and time, take up the power to tame sentient beings; and it is designated in the manner of being embraced by deeds and actions and set in motion by deeds and actions. Son of noble family, this is the Great Vehicle that is conduct in nature.

"Son of noble family, what then is the Great Vehicle itself as unconditioned? It is a designation for the nature of reality as it abides. Son of noble family, 'the nature of reality as it abides' is a designation for inconceivability itself."

"That which is the inconceivable nature of reality is the unconditioned. That which is unconditioned does not depend on the two extremes; it has no designated essential nature, nor does it have an undesignated essential nature. That which has not even a designated essential nature has no conduct. That which has no conduct cannot be expressed. That which cannot be expressed is free from all entities. That which is free from all entities is undisturbed. That which is undisturbed is the Dharma. That which is the Dharma is the Tathāgata's manifest and perfect awakening. That which is the Tathāgata's manifest and perfect awakening is the truth.

"Son of noble family, that which is the truth exists. That which exists is not false. That which is not false is genuine. That which is genuine is the nature of reality. That which is the nature of reality is non-conceptual and free of conceptualization. That which is non-conceptual and free of conceptualization is wisdom. That which is wisdom is consciousness. That which is consciousness is suchness. That which is suchness is designated. That which is designated is conception. That which is conception is taught to be the conventional. Because the conventional exists, conduct is designated. Because designation exists, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha is shown appearing in the world. The deeds of a buddha, defeating the four māras, turning the wheel of Dharma, and so on up to the great final nirvāṇa, as well as abiding, arising, perishing, birth, aging, sickness, prosperity and decline, are all displayed in the world.

"Son of noble family, taking up the power of time and place for these and the like, they set up the maṇḍala of conduct and teach the Dharma to sentient beings by various kinds of skillful means. That teaching of the Dharma is the genuine culmination: it converges perfectly in a single vehicle and brings about the perfect attainment of a single domain. Why is that? Because the Tathāgata is free from all conception.

"Son of noble family, in that which is the nature of reality there is no designation or cognition, and nothing is apprehended, because wisdom is non-conceptual. In it there is neither conduct nor non-conduct, neither training nor non-training, neither discipline nor non-discipline. In it no engaging or turning back of body, speech or mind is apprehended. In it there is no such thing as 'Tathāgata,' 'buddha,' 'bodhisattva,' 'śrāvaka,' 'pratyekabuddha,' 'Dharma,' 'Saṅgha,' 'god,' 'nāga,' 'yakṣa,' and so on up to gandharva, asura, garuḍa, kinnara, mahoraga, human and nonhuman; nor 'existent' or 'nonexistent,' 'merit' or 'non-merit,' 'saṃsāra' or 'nirvāṇa,' 'bondage' or 'liberation.' Son of noble family, in it there is no such thing as 'time,' 'hour,' 'instant,' 'moment' or 'brief while.' In it there is no such thing as 'moon,' 'star,' 'sun,' 'fire,' 'wind,' 'water,' 'earth,' 'night,' 'day,' 'season,' 'yearly cycle' or 'month.'

"Son of noble family, thus all the doors of conceptual imputation that fall to the extreme of worldly convention, and all the doors of imputation that transcend the world, do not occur in it and do not exist; they are not apprehended there. Why is that? Because the nature of reality has the characteristic of nonduality, and duality does not occur in it. Son of noble family, it is like this: since no second space has ever arisen, the element of space is nondual. Son of noble family, just so the nature of reality too is nondual.

"Son of noble family, therefore those who practice in such a nature of reality are taught to be bodhisattva great beings who practice the conduct practiced by the tathāgatas, arhats, perfectly complete buddhas. Why is that? Non-conduct is the bodhisattva's conduct. Non-training is the bodhisattva's training. Non-attainment is the bodhisattvas' attainment. Non-undertaking is the bodhisattvas' undertaking. Non-discipline is discipline. The undesignatable is designation. Not seeing is seeing the Buddha. Not attending is attending upon the Saṅgha. Not listening is listening to the Dharma. Not understanding is understanding. Not going is going. Not abiding is abiding. Non-emptiness is emptiness. Bodhisattvas have no knowing, no attaining, no clear realization and no direct experiencing.

"Son of noble family, by this enumeration too it should be understood thus: the bodhisattva great being has practiced the conduct. He is not attached to anything. He has no clinging to anything. He has no anger toward anything. He has no aversion toward anything. Free of appearances, undeluded and unerring, he is always in meditative equipoise: non-conceptual, peaceful, well established, without equal, utterly unmoving. He never wearies of working for the Buddha, the Dharma, the Saṅgha and sentient beings, and in all three times he knows no discouragement.

"Son of noble family, it is like this: the element of space is not defiled by dust, smoke or clouds, and it does not become discouraged. Why is that? Because the element of space is non-conceptual. Son of noble family, just so the bodhisattva who has practiced such conduct does not become discouraged and is not defiled."

"Son of noble family, a bodhisattva who abides in such conduct should, through the tathāgatas' conduct arisen from the great vajra, the conduct of the Great Vajra Crest, the ornament that is the array of the great vajra maṇḍala, take up the Dharma discourses on merit and the rest, read them, and also explain them extensively and correctly to others."

When he had said this, the bodhisattva great being Samantabhadra said to the Blessed One, "Blessed One, it is wonderful! Sugata, it is wonderful! Those bodhisattvas in whose ears these Dharma discourses will resound have gained a fine gain; what need is there to speak of those who listen to them, hold them, read them and bring them to mind? Blessed One, they are certain to attain unsurpassed, perfectly complete awakening in the Great Vehicle. Blessed One, they are fortunate enough for the inconceivable way, the abode of miraculous display of the unsurpassed Great Vehicle, the Tathāgata's manifest and perfect awakening. Out of loving concern for me, may the Blessed One please explain the unsurpassed Great Vehicle, which bears abundant fruit at once."

When he had made this request, the Blessed One remained silent and entered the meditative absorption called Great Vajra Source. The moment the Blessed One was in equipoise, then, in that very instant, in all the infinite world systems of every direction, all the tathāgatas proclaimed the sound of the great vajra, and with one voice uttered the syllable of Vajrasattva, the Vajra Source, called Essence of the Radiant Light of the Blazing Great Vajra. They went to where the dense forest grove adorned with banners of various sāla trees was, where the Blessed One Śākyamuni was, and having arrived they sat down with legs crossed on great vajra lotus seats.

Then those tathāgatas inquired after the health of the Blessed One Śākyamuni: "Are you free from harm?" They inquired after his health: "Are you free from illness? Is your movement light? Are you strong? Are you well? Are you living in comfort?"

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Śākyamuni, together with all those tathāgatas, spoke this vajra king of secret mantras that blesses the vajra maṇḍala:

namaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarvathā oṃ vajra kara khaṃ svāhā

Then those tathāgatas, having spoken the secret mantra that blesses the great vajra maṇḍala, remained in equipoise in the meditative absorption called Miraculous Display through the Blessing of the Buddha.

Then that great assembly, complete in every respect, their hearts drawn by the thought, "How great a marvel!", sat gazing at Mañjuśrī Kumārabhūta. Then Mañjuśrī Kumārabhūta, knowing that the assembly was single-pointedly absorbed in the thought, "How great a marvel!", said to the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Śākyamuni, and to those tathāgatas gathered from the world systems of the ten directions:

"Since, having declared the king of secret mantras,
The guides now do not speak,
It is certain that the Sugata's son
Is to be empowered with the vajra.
The conduct of secret mantra is great wisdom,
The supreme state of the Great Vehicle;
In it are attained omniscience
And the conduct that accomplishes every aim.
The Great Vehicle arisen from the conduct of secret mantra
Is omniscience itself;
The perfect buddhas of the past
Have always taught it in this supreme grove.
The sāla banner, so greatly delightful,
Is the place where true conduct is taught.
What has arisen from former conduct
Is said to arise from knowledge of secret mantra.
All the perfect buddhas who have passed,
Likewise all those yet to come,
And the protectors who have arisen now,
The guides who are present,
All of them, having come here,
Grant to the Sugata's son
This empowerment through the Great Vehicle
That arises from the conduct of secret mantra.
O great sage of the buddhas,
The empowerment of all is mine."

"Because I long to benefit the world,
I make this request of the perfect buddhas:
In order to guard the whole of the teaching
And to make the true Dharma abide forever,
Upon the crown of this great-souled one
May the perfect buddhas confer empowerment.
He has fully understood and realized the Great Vehicle
That arises from the conduct of secret mantra.
His former aspirations have surely come to pass;
He is the buddhas' son of great renown.
This one, surely endowed with primordial conduct,
What is he but the very likeness of a buddha?
The Sugata's son, free of attachment,
Has gained mastery over the culmination of awakening.
Since the protector of secret mantra
Has blessed this assembly in every way,
You who have proclaimed the king of secret mantra,
Why do you now remain without speaking?
All have been seized with wonder
And stay intent on understanding it well.
O great heroes, please dispel their doubts
And likewise their hesitation.
Now that the time has come,
Please make no delay, but explain it."

Then those tathāgatas knew that Mañjuśrī Kumārabhūta had made this request, and the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Śākyamuni, spoke:

"This nature of reality, to which the guides have awakened,
Is singularly supreme.
Those who always rest in meditative equipoise
