Should realize it with intense concentration."

Then the tathāgatas, Śākyamuni and the others, blessed into being a dense grove arrayed with banners of many kinds of sāla trees, laid out everywhere with the cloister of a vajra maṇḍala. It was square, with a plinth, and with four doors set facing outward. It was encircled by an outer enclosure of the seven kinds of precious substances, adorned with gateways, archways and terraces, canopied above with jewelled canopies, and hung with threads of the five kinds of jewels from which jewelled bells rang out. In its center was a vajra lotus throne, ringed in the eight directions by garlands of great vajras and encircled all around by a vajra outer enclosure. Beyond that vajra enclosure it was hung with vajra garlands and vajra threads, bound with vajra bonds, and staked with vajra pegs, and they blessed all of it to be of the very nature of vajra. Then from the uṣṇīṣa on the crowns of their heads they sent forth rays of light called Power Unobstructed Everywhere. Having sent forth those rays of light, they illuminated the maṇḍala of the assembly, complete in every respect, and the rays entered the crown of the head of the bodhisattva mahāsattva Samantabhadra.

As soon as those rays of light of the tathāgatas had entered, the bodhisattva mahāsattva Samantabhadra was delighted, pleased and overjoyed, and he spoke the heart mantra called Unobstructed Power:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyo viśva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā apratihata bala mahā vajra ājñā karaṇa namo stute svahā

Then, as soon as the vajra heart mantra of Unobstructed Power had been spoken, those tathāgatas blessed into being a vajra whose form was produced by meditative absorption, dhāraṇī, the powers, the factors of awakening, the path, the noble truths, emptiness, and the unshared qualities of the buddhas, fashioned from the limit of great emptiness, and generated by the six perfections, namely generosity, discipline, perfect restraint, gentleness and the rest. This they placed in the hand of the bodhisattva Samantabhadra.

As soon as those tathāgatas had placed the vajra in Samantabhadra's hand, throughout the boundless world systems of the ten directions and in all the maṇḍalas of the buddhas' assemblies, all the tathāgatas, all the bodhisattvas, all the śrāvakas, all the pratyekabuddhas, and all the gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans and nonhumans proclaimed with one voice, "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva is Vajrapāṇi! He is Vajrapāṇi!"

The Noble Empowerment of Vajrapāṇi, the third fascicle.

Having blessed this sacred pledge, those tathāgatas again spoke this heart mantra called Secret Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā guhya vajra dhara svāhā

Then, as soon as the heart mantra of the Secret Vajra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the maṇḍalas of the buddhas' assemblies the cry "Lord of Secrets! Lord of Secrets!" resounded with a roar from one to the next. And so, through the might of this heart mantra and the power of the blessing of all the tathāgatas, the perfect mind and wisdom of all the tathāgatas directly entered the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra called the Dhāraṇī of the Great Vast Vajra, the heart that guards the teaching everywhere and fully protects beings:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra dhara pira vira pikira svāhā

As soon as the heart mantra of the Great Vast Vajra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the maṇḍalas of the buddhas' assemblies, Vajrapāṇi, the crowned one who arises from the Great Vast Vajra and who knows the rite of perfectly displaying every form, appeared everywhere: in all the buddha fields and all the boundless world systems, at the feet of all the tathāgatas, before all the bodhisattvas, all the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, those who have first generated the mind of awakening, and beginners, and behind and before sentient beings, whether they were awake or asleep, teaching the Dharma, attaining complete and perfect awakening, working for the benefit of beings, or producing emanations. In the presence of all of them he appeared.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the supreme vajra heart mantra called Binding the Directions with the Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā dig vajra ṭa ṭa ṭa ṭa ṭaṃ svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra of binding the directions with the vajra had been spoken, all the malevolent beings in the ten directions appeared bound with vajra bonds.

Then, again, those tathāgatas spoke the vajra heart mantra called the Vajra Peg of the Earth:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā ṭha ṭha ṭha ṭha ṭhaṃ svāhā

As soon as this vajra heart mantra of fixing the earth had been spoken, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra that Fixes the Earth!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the supreme heart mantra called the Vajra Beneath the Earth:

namaḥ sarva tathagatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvatha yaṭa yaṭraṃ svaha

As soon as this heart mantra of the Vajra Beneath the Earth had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the realms beneath the earth, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra Beneath the Earth!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra called Cutting Off the Movements of All Vidyādharas:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā cchinda sarva vīryā ntu raga dim pravar mala bdha vijaya svāhā

As soon as this supreme heart mantra of cutting off the movements of the vidyādharas had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the vidyādharas, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the one who cuts off the movements of the vidyādharas!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Great Vajra that Crushes the Yakṣas:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra yakṣaṇi gṛhadata svāhā

As soon as the heart mantra of the yakṣa vajra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the yakṣas, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Yakṣas!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra called the Crest Banner Proclaiming the Nāga Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā hara hara nāga vajra svāhā

Then, as soon as this heart mantra of the nāga vajra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the nāgas, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Nāgas!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Great Gandharva Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā matha matha vajra gandharvahṛd ya svāhā

As soon as the heart mantra of the Great Gandharva Vajra had been spoken, at that very moment, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the gandharvas, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Gandharvas!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Great Vajra, Lord of the Garuḍas:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra pita tadhara kara haṃ ha svāhā

Then, as soon as this heart mantra of the Great Vajra, Lord of the Garuḍas, had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the garuḍas, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva, the great Vajrapāṇi, is the Vajra of the Lord of the Garuḍas!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Great Kinnara Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra kinnara danda vijaya śapta svāhā

Then, as soon as this supreme kinnara heart mantra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the kinnaras, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Kinnaras!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Vajra of the Great Mahoragas:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā mahorga vajra jvālāya svāhā

Then, as soon as this heart mantra of the Vajra of the Great Mahoragas had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the great mahoragas, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Great Mahoragas!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Great Rākṣasa Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra rākṣasa krura daha paca ṭuṃ ṭuṃ ṭuṃ svāhā

Then, as soon as this heart mantra of the Great Rākṣasa Vajra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the rākṣasas, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Rākṣasas!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra called the Great Ḍākinī Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra hṛda heha svāhā

Then, as soon as this heart mantra of the Great Ḍākinī Vajra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the ḍākinīs, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Ḍākinīs!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Great Bhūta Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā hale hūle vajra maha bhyateśvara svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra of the Great Bhūta Vajra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the bhūtas, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Great Vajra of the Bhūtas!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Great Piśāca Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā mahā vajra piśāceśvara viśvara viśvahṛdaya gaṃ mici mici maṭa maṭa vajra svāhā

As soon as these heart mantras of the Great Piśāca Vajra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the piśācas, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Piśācas!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Great Piśācī Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra dūti piśāci kiri miri mile svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra of the Great Piśācī Vajra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the piśācīs, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Piśācīs!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Rākṣasī Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra karāli rākṣasi hāri miri svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra of the Rākṣasī Vajra had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the rākṣasīs, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Rākṣasīs!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Great Vajra of the Oppressor Spirits:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā ostāraka vajra hisi svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra of the oppressor spirits had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the oppressor spirits, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Oppressor Spirits!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Vajra that Terrifies the Shadow Oppressors:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā kili kili vajra vidhahini svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra that terrifies the shadow oppressors had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the shadow oppressors, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Shadow Oppressors!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Vajra that Halts the Apasmāra Grahas, the spirits that cause loss of memory:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā graṃ graṃ spharaṇa vajra svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra of the Vajra that Halts the Apasmāra Grahas had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of the apasmāras, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of the Apasmāras!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Then, again, those tathāgatas spoke this heart mantra of the Vajra that Repels All Grahas:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vira vajra sarva graha nikṛntan jāṭi valaṃ podara raṭa raṭa svāhā

As soon as these heart mantras of the Vajra that Repels All Grahas had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the abodes of all the grahas, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra that Repels All Grahas!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the vajra heart mantra of all the gods and the rest:

namaḥ sarva tathagatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvatha varjraṃ varjrasaṃ saṃ svāhā

As soon as this vajra heart mantra of the gods and the rest had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in the abodes of all the gods and the rest, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra Heart of the Gods and the Rest!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Vajra that Crushes All the Grahas of Disease:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā bhaiṣajya vajra amṛta bhava svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra of the Great Vajra of the Great Medicinal Elixir had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems and in the places of the great medicinal elixirs, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra of Medicinal Elixir!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Vajra that Dispels the Grahas of Disease:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā traṭa traṭ vajra svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra of the Vajra that Dispels Disease had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra that Completely Pacifies Disease!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Vajra that Halts the Grahas of Old Age:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā amṛtodbhava vajra la la la la svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra that halts old age had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems the words "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra that Halts Old Age!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Vajra that Completely Pacifies Death:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā kati vajra jaya ha ha svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra of the Vajra that Completely Pacifies Death had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra Victorious over Death!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke this heart mantra of the Vajra that Repels the Grahas of Misfortune:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā parma vajra sarva śriyaṃ abhudayanda da mahā kāruṇika dhaka dhaka svāhā

As soon as this heart mantra of the Vajra that Halts All Misfortune had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is the Vajra that Halts All Misfortune!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Then, again, those tathāgatas spoke the vajra heart mantra of the Unobstructed Power of the Great Cakravartin:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā oṃ vajra cakravarti hūṃ svāhā

Then, as soon as this king of secret mantras of the Great Cakravartin had been spoken, throughout all the boundless world systems, in all the maṇḍalas of the buddhas' assemblies, in all the abodes of the bodhisattvas, and in all the abodes of the gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, rākṣasas, bhūtas, piśācas and the rest, the cry "This bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi is the Cakravartin of the Great Vajra!" resounded with a roar from one to the next.

Again, those tathāgatas spoke the heart mantra of the Cakravartin of the Great Vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā jī śrī hūṃ svāhā

Then those tathāgatas gave the heart mantras of the Great Vajra, the Great Cakravartin and the others, to the bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi. Having given them, they illuminated, with a great net of dense clouds forming a maṇḍala of a great net of light rays, the world system complete in every respect, the dense grove arrayed with banners of many kinds of sāla trees, and the maṇḍala of the assembly complete in every respect. Then, through the power of the blessing of the three dharmas, they blessed the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, and within the maṇḍala of the dharmadhātu they transformed themselves into the very nature of the dharmadhātu and vanished.

At that time the Blessed One Śākyamuni also said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, "Son of noble family, all the tathāgatas have empowered you, in the great vajra maṇḍala of the maṇḍala of the Great Cakravartin, with the empowerment of the vajra in your hand. They have given you a vajra made of many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of meditative absorptions. You have been blessed by the vajra of the great cakravartin king. All the vajra heart mantras have been taught to you. All the tathāgatas have entrusted you to accomplish the aims of the entire teaching and to protect it. Your great former aspiration has been completely fulfilled. Son of noble family, now display your own miracle of magical power! Son of noble family, proclaim the great maṇḍala of the Vajrapāṇi Empowerment!"

When he had spoken thus, Vajrapāṇi said this to the Blessed One: "Lord of the Dharma, I shall do just as you have commanded. Blessed One, that I have attained the supreme wonder and attained the victorious true Dharma is because the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha, has shown me loving kindness."

Then, at that time, the bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption called the Heart that Proclaims the Great Vajra Peak. And as soon as Vajrapāṇi had entered into equipoise in the meditative absorption called the Heart that Proclaims the Great Vajra Peak, at that very time all the world systems and all the fields moved in six ways and shook violently everywhere. This entire great trichiliocosm, too, moved in six ways: it moved, moved strongly, and moved strongly everywhere; it trembled, trembled strongly, and trembled strongly everywhere; it quaked, quaked strongly, and quaked strongly everywhere; it rocked, rocked strongly, and rocked strongly everywhere. When the east rose, the west sank; when the west rose, the east sank; when the south rose, the north sank; when the north rose, the south sank; when the center rose, the edges sank; when the edges rose, the center sank. A great radiance appeared in the world as well. Even the regions between the worlds that lie in gloom, darkness and pitch blackness were illuminated by that light, so that the beings who had been born there looked at one another, and seeing one another they thought, "Look, other beings have been born here too! Look, other beings have been born here too!" As they looked, the bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi taught the Dharma of the dhāraṇī of the heart of the vajra letters.

Having heard that Dharma, they gained faith, and when they died and passed on they were born among the gods. Then they went to the dense grove arrayed with banners of many kinds of sāla trees, wherever it was, in order to see, pay homage to, and make offerings to the bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi, who had been empowered with the great Vajrapāṇi empowerment.

Even the hot hells of sentient beings were cut off in that instant, in that moment, in that second. At that time not even the name of the animal realm was heard. All the abodes of the pretas, too, became encircled on every side by walls of the seven kinds of precious substances, and came to be endowed with divine food, drink and every requisite in full, and with divine bodies. This entire dense grove arrayed with banners of many kinds of sāla trees was illuminated by a great radiance. Even the two sons of the gods, the sun and the moon, of great miraculous power and great might, no longer shone out or blazed forth; apart from the light of the tathāgatas and the light of the bodhisattvas who have attained the ten grounds, every other light grew dim.

As soon as the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi had entered into equipoise in this very meditative absorption, wrathful kings emerged at that moment from all the pores of his body. They proclaimed the great sound hūṃ, held vajras in their hands, and creased their foreheads into wrathful frowns. With a single voice they made this entire great trichiliocosm hear them, and they uttered this inspired utterance:

"Hey! You savage rākṣasas,
Ancestral spirits and piśācas,
Mātṛkā grahas and bhūtas,
Apasmāras, siddhas and serpent seizers,
Oppressor spirits, obstructors, vināyakas and the rest,
All you hosts of grahas,
Give up your avarice entirely,
Settle in equipoise with tamed minds,
And, being wise, go for refuge
To the Buddha, the Dharma and the Saṅgha.
This glorious vajra holder,
Who holds a brilliantly blazing vajra,
Dwells in a lovely many-storied house
In a delightful, sublime grove.
Go, and from that protector
Receive the sacred pledges in full;
Rid yourselves of pride and of clinging to self,
And then roam through all the lands.
Otherwise, this very day,
By those blazing, wildly raging vajras,
Blazing and greatly terrifying,
Your heads will be utterly smashed.
Thus the wrathful kings
Throughout this whole world system
Have made it known in words that need no seeking:
Go before the vajra holder!"

At that time all the hosts of bhūtas sought this teaching of the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi and went for refuge to the Buddha, went for refuge to the Dharma, and went for refuge to the Saṅgha. They also paid homage to the bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi.

Then, at that time, the bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi spoke his own heart mantra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā caṇḍa mahāroṣaṇa vajrapāṇi hūṃ phaṭ

As soon as the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi had spoken his own heart mantra, throughout all the boundless world systems, in all the maṇḍalas of the buddhas' assemblies, and in all the abodes of the gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, mahoragas, humans and nonhumans, the cry "Oh! This bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi is Fierce Vajrapāṇi! He is Fierce Vajrapāṇi!" resounded with a roar from one to the next. Hearing it, they made fervent aspirations toward the great way of the bodhisattvas.

Then the bodhisattva mahāsattva Vajrapāṇi, having spoken this heart mantra of the Fierce Vajra, and with the permission of all the tathāgatas, looked upon all the maṇḍalas of the assembly, complete in every respect, and proclaimed the great maṇḍala:

"Now, the master of secret mantra
Is skilled in the meaning of reality and possessed of moral discipline,
Skilled in the arts,
Compassionate by nature and possessed of patience,
His mind set on the Buddha, the Dharma and the Saṅgha,
Steadfast, and knowing the right time,
Neither distracted nor listless,
Of great radiance, devoted to the profound Dharma,
Always diligent in recitation,
And delighting in the practice of meditative concentration.
A mantra practitioner who, on hearing the name of Vajrapāṇi,
Always sheds tears, his hair standing on end,
Who even in dreams has no hesitation
Or doubt regarding Vajrapāṇi,
Who knows the right time and likewise the right measure,
Who is grateful and likewise speaks the truth:"

Such a master should go before a stūpa containing relics or before an image, or enter a river that flows down to the ocean, and there recite each of the heart mantras mentioned above a hundred thousand times. After that, with the heart mantra of Fierce Vajrapāṇi, he should perform a fire offering ten thousand times with white mustard seed mixed with the three sweet substances. After that, seated in the center of the great maṇḍala of the Cakravartin, he should recite the mantra of the Blessed One, the Great Cakravartin. With the great fierce heart mantra he should perform the rite of purifying and protecting the disciples.

After that the ground should be examined. Ground without stones, gravel or potsherds, free of such flaws as living creatures and anthills, is auspicious in every case. If one begins the work there when the planets are not in a fierce position, on a date that is not inauspicious, avoiding unfavorable ingressions and conjunctions, when the day, the karaṇa, the muhūrta, the lunar mansion and the planets are manifestly favorable, it will be auspicious.

Red, yellow, white and black,
And mixed is the fifth.
In whichever of these grounds it be,
The wise one should dig a pit about a cubit deep;
Then the mantrin should fill that pit
With that very same earth.
If there is earth left over, it is supreme in every way;
If it is just level with the rim, it is said to be middling;
Ground that does not fill the pit should be abandoned:
The wise should not begin the work there.
White ground is taught to be for pacifying;
Red is held to be for the purpose of subjugation;
Yellow should be known as for increase;
Black, know it to be for fierce activity.
There, having carefully offered
Offering water, torma, lamps,
Scents and incense, one should guard the place,
And, settled in equipoise, address the earth.
That night, upon that ground,
One should sleep on a spread of kuśa grass.
If the ground proves suitable,
One should carefully draw the maṇḍala there.
The buddhas of the past, supreme among humans,
Have declared that such ground is free of obstructors.
Otherwise, one should not draw there;
With obstructors present, one will come to ruin.

Then, having risen, the mantrin,
The wise one, on that day,
In order to take full possession of the ground,
Should perform this fire offering:
Honey and milk
Mixed with butter make the best fire offering.
With a peaceful mind, the oblations
Should be offered twenty-one times.
Then the mantrin digs the ground
And fills it again with that same earth.
Or else, at the time of the maṇḍala rite,
The mantrin fills the ground with fragrant substances.
It should be well pounded, made level and smooth.
The quarters and the gates,
All of them, in every way,
Should be beautified with the five kinds of precious things.
In particular, in the center of the ground,
The five precious things should be placed.

Then disciples endowed with good qualities
Should be instructed and accepted.
With recitation and fire offerings, all of them,
One should carefully protect them.
Those who are to recite this secret mantra
Of the wise Vajra, the bestower of the supreme,
Should first be examined
By investigating their faults and qualities.
Those who possess these qualities
Should be accepted after thorough examination:
Those excellent in touch and in shape,
Bright, disciplined and diligent,
Endowed with wisdom, steadfast and grateful,
Devoted to the Three Jewels,
Beautiful in form, with all limbs complete;
Those who delight in the Great Vehicle,
And who toward all bodhisattvas
Are always truly respectful;
Respectful to father and respectful to mother,
Zealous in honoring the guru;
Those who, in acting for the benefit of others,
Always engage in it from the heart;
Those who are honest and gentle,
Without guile and diligent in the Dharma,
Pure in body, speech and mind:
These should be closely examined and accepted.
Those who from the heart have faith
In this world and in the world beyond,
Brahmins, kṣatriyas, vaiśyas
Or śūdras who are diligent in the Dharma
And who wish to understand the Mantra Vehicle:
These should carefully be accepted.
Not those of the Śrāvaka Vehicle,
Nor those of the Pratyekabuddha Vehicle;
The one-eyed, those with crippled hands, eunuchs,
Hunchbacks, the deaf, dwarfs,
Those who have flat noses,
Likewise the squint-eyed and the guilt-ridden,
The miserly and the ungrateful,
Those whose minds are proud of their learning, and the crooked,
Those with no devotion to the Mantra Vehicle,
The vehicle of great beings:
These should never be accepted.
They have no fortune in this teaching.
Those ignorant of the treatises are not to be accepted,
Nor those who grasp only scattered bits and pieces,
Nor likewise those without respect for the guru:
These contemptible ones should be shunned from afar.
Those whose limbs are incomplete,
The lame and the crippled,
And those who have extra limbs,
The wise should carefully reject entirely.

The disciples as described
Should then be examined according to the rite.
Even if there is only one, he should be accepted;
Or else four disciples may be accepted.
Then, having made them keep a fast,
One should honor them with scents and flowers.
On the ground inside the maṇḍala
A mat of kuśa grass is spread,
And the disciples, gentle and composed,
Should lie there together the whole time.
The master, moment after moment,
Should at that time fully explain
To the disciples this Dharma
Of the profound and vast Great Vehicle.
It is beneficial to describe in full
All the qualities that arise from reciting secret mantra.
The miraculous powers and so forth of the bodhisattvas
Should be described in full,
And their compassion for the continuum of others:
These he should speak of moment after moment.
When a quarter of the night remains,
If they dream good dreams,
Then, at daybreak, those devoted to the Three Jewels,
Sitting properly before the Buddha,
Are told: "You should relate them."
To be seated upon a lotus is supreme;
Holding or seeing one is likewise;
To dream of seeing the ocean,
And of swimming in it and drinking it, is likewise.
Seeing a buddha gladdens the heart;
Bodhisattvas, those weary of the world,
The pratyekabuddhas of the world,
The śrāvakas, and the learned:
Seeing these gladdens the heart.
A mass of fire, and renown,
A bull, a proud elephant,
And a horse that is white:
To see these and to ride them,
And a herd of cattle too, is auspicious.
Brahmins and kṣatriyas,
A king renowned for virtue,
Dear kinsmen and siddhas,
The sun and the moon:
To see these will be auspicious.
In a dream, to speak and tell tales,
To cut off one's own head,
To eat human flesh,
To bathe in blood,
Likewise to sink into filth,
To eat curd and cooked rice,
To drink liquor,
And to have one's hair shaved off: these are supreme.

For one's teeth to fall out,
And a pine tree or a nyagrodha tree:
To climb these will be auspicious.
To hear words such as
"Excellent, son!" or "Wise one!",
A lovely woman,
Making love in the daytime,
Brahmā, Rudra, the sun,
Viṣṇu and the moon,
Kubera and Dhanada,
Śakra (Indra) and Varuṇa:
If one sees them, speaks with them,
Or converses with them, one will certainly succeed.
Other dreams like these as well
Should be taken up by those who seek accomplishment.
Dreams contrary to these
The fearless should not take up.
Running and leaping on the ground,
Dancing and singing,
Laughing, and riding
A donkey or a camel are very bad.
Anointing the body with sesame oil,
Talking with naked ascetics,
Meeting the one-eyed, the lame, hunchbacks
And the ugly,
Pigs and dogs,
And likewise donkeys,
Buffaloes, camels and jackals:
To see them in a dream is always deplored.
Those brandishing tridents, raised tridents,
The red-eyed and the utterly terrifying,
The naked and the flat-nosed,
Fearsome ones making threatening gestures,
Those black in color and greatly ferocious,
Those clad in blue and ugly in appearance:
Whoever dreams of these,
For him there will be no accomplishment.
To hear harsh cries such as
"Seize him! Beat him! Crush him!" or "Eat him!",
To hear fierce, terrifying, wrathful words,
To hear "ruin" and "not found";
The cry of vultures, white birds,
Owls, crows, cats,
Rats, scorpions, weasels and snakes,
And any other malicious creatures:
If one sees these in a dream,
Or even touches them, it is inauspicious.

Then on the following day the master should empower the five products of the cow with mantra, using the vajra heart mantra. He should summon the disciples and purify them by sprinkling, using the fierce vajra heart mantra. He should give it to them in a vessel, a cone of aśvattha leaves or their cupped palms, and instruct them: "Today you must observe moderation in food." When the sun has set, he should make a fire offering with the Queen of Knowledge Mantras, the Fearless One, offering oblations and twenty-one sticks of firewood.

Then, from aśvattha wood,
Or else from udumbara,
Tooth sticks eight finger-widths long should be made.
The first one is adorned with flowers.
Having empowered them with the fierce vajra mantra,
He should give them to the disciples to chew.
Facing toward the east,
Or else facing north, they should set them down.
Recollecting the Lord of Secret Mantra,
One should know the outcome by the direction each stick points.
Then, on the following day,
Having made the disciples keep a fast,
That called Vajra Invincible Fire,
Which bewilders the three realms of existence,
So that they keep it constantly in mind,
He should give them this stainless dhāraṇī.
He should proclaim the pledges dear to the heart,
And proclaim them all the more to those who have broken them.
It thoroughly pacifies wrongdoing
And brings supreme good fortune.
It is the mother
Of all wise bodhisattvas,
The purifier of all wrongdoing:
It should always and everywhere be kept in mind.

The master, at every point,
With a mind always settled in equipoise,
Takes up an excellent marking cord
Made of threads of five colors,
And carefully, across this whole ground,
Should strike the lines well.
Sitting in the center of the maṇḍala,
The mantrin recollects the tathāgatas,
And likewise Vajradhara,
And the Cakravartin;
Having meditated on himself as that deity,
He recollects its mantra in his mind.
Then, facing east,
He strikes the line in the east.
Then, turning round again,
Sitting facing west,
He strikes the line in the west.
Then, turning, the wise one
Faces toward the north
And strikes the line in the north.
Then, turning round again,
Facing toward the south,
He who knows the divisions
Strikes the line in the south.
In this way the inner maṇḍala
Should be made with eight sections.
Then, in this same manner,
Going round all four directions,
One should very carefully strike the lines
So that it is square on every side.
Coming out from the central position,
Standing in the quarter of Agni (the southeast),
The mantrin, according to the mantra rite,
Strikes the line of the eastern side.
Then the one who knows the rite
Respectfully goes to the quarter of Īśāna (the northeast),
And, facing west,
Strikes the line of the northern side.
Going on from the quarter of Īśāna,
Standing in the quarter of Vāyu (the northwest),
And there facing south,
He strikes the line of the western side.
Coming out from that place,
He goes to the quarter of Nairṛti (the southwest),
And as far as the quarter of Agni
He should strike the marking cord well.
Having thus made the place
Square on every side,
Then likewise on the outside,
According to the rite, eight sections should be made.
The measure is sixteen cubits,
By the master's own cubit.
Then, coming out, the wise one
Should make the further sections.
On the outside, together with the ground,
The count is twenty-five cubits.
There too, just as before,
It should be divided into eight sections.
Taking from the eighth,
The mantrin, everywhere,
Should strike the lines of the retinue's area.
In the three tiers, without fail,
The first gate should be opened
In the size that suits the eight sections.
For the middle tier of the maṇḍala,
Knowing the gate to be double the former,
And triple for the third tier,
The wise one should make all the gates.
The measures of the porticoes
Should be made equal to the central section.
For the chief deities too
Gates should be made.
Having thus divided it well
Into three tiers and laid out the maṇḍala,
Sitting at the place of the Lord of Mantra,
The wise one should draw the maṇḍala.
As colors here, in particular,
The five precious substances are preferred.
Flour of ground śāli rice is also acceptable;
Or it may be made with whatever is at hand.
Wearing white clothes, having made himself clean,
Disciplined, with a cloth tied over his mouth,
Carefully, starting from the northeast corner,
He should pour out the colored powders,
In the manner of the vajra fist,
Or else with the hook mudrā,
Or in the manner of the vessel mudrā,
Or with the hand held as in reciting the Veda.

First the white should be poured;
After that the red is poured;
After that the yellow is poured;
After that the green is poured;
And inside all of them
The mantrin pours the black color.
Of what was taught as the eight sections,
The parts of the central section
Belong to the perfect buddhas;
They are held to be in the manner of the vajra binding.
Their seat is taught to be a lotus,
Supremely white and beautiful.
Upon it a vajra is placed,
And likewise a vajra uṣṇīṣa;
At its heart make a blaze of flames.
In short, it should be furnished with the seed syllable.

There, in the east, the tathāgata Ratnakusuma should be placed. In the south is the tathāgata Saṃkusumitarājendra. In the west is the tathāgata Amitābha. In the north is the tathāgata Akṣobhya. In the northeast corner is the tathāgata Śikhin. In the southeast corner is the tathāgata Vipaśyin. In the southwest corner is the tathāgata Viśvabhū. In the northwest corner is the tathāgata Krakucchanda.

In short, the lotus in the center
Is said to have eight petals:
The petal in the east is white,
In the west it is red,
In the north it is yellow,
And in the south it is black.
All the remaining petals
The victors have declared to be mixed.
In the center of its heart
Draw a vase like the light of the moon.
Above it draw the great wind,
Blazing and bearing a banner,
Shaped like a half moon and fierce,
Its light like the color of summer clouds.
Above the wind draw fire;
Its shape is triangular,
Its emblem a three-cornered fruit,
Like the fire at the time of destruction.
In its center draw a lotus,
Its light like the fire at the end of time.
In the center of its heart
The Cakravartin should be drawn.

There that Blessed One should be placed as a form, as a mudrā or as a syllable, as is fitting.

If the mudrā is placed, it is the vajra,
And carefully it should be furnished with the seed syllable.
The sections outside it,
The eight of them, are as before:
The mantrin, according to the rite,
Should arrange the deities.

There, in the east, in a wheel maṇḍala, seated in the middle of a fire maṇḍala, one should draw the Vajra Bestower of the Supreme with his retinue, with the mudrā of the vajra utpala. In the southeast corner, in a vajra maṇḍala, one should draw the bodhisattvas Samantabhadra and the rest with their retinues, with the mudrā of the wish-fulfilling jewel. In the south, in a vajra-wheel maṇḍala, one should draw Vajrapāṇi with his retinue, together with all the vajra holders, with the vajra mudrā. In the southwest corner, in a vajra-wheel maṇḍala, one should draw the bodhisattva Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin with his retinue, with the mudrā of the wish-fulfilling jewel. In the west, in a vajra-wheel maṇḍala, the bodhisattva Ākāśagarbha should be set out with his retinue, with the sword mudrā. In the northwest corner, in a lotus-vajra maṇḍala, one should draw the bodhisattva Maitreya with his retinue, with the mudrā of the water flask. In the north, in a great lotus-vajra-wheel maṇḍala, the bodhisattva Avalokiteśvara should be set out with his retinue, with the lotus mudrā. In the northeast corner, in a great vajra maṇḍala, the bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha should be set out with his retinue, with the mudrā of the great jewel.

In the four directions one should draw the four world protectors with their retinues, contained within the great vajra maṇḍala. There, the mudrā of Vaiśravaṇa is the club; the mudrā of Virūḍhaka is the cudgel; the mudrā of Virūpākṣa is the vajra; and the mudrā of Kubera is the noose.

The outer maṇḍala of the great enclosure is square and even, with four gates opened, with gate chambers, porticoes and terraces well laid out, and it is surrounded by a great enclosing wall. Within it one should draw the worldly gods with their retinues. There, in the east, one should draw Śakra (Indra), lord of the gods, with his retinue, with the vajra mudrā. To his north one should draw Brahmā with his retinue, with the lotus mudrā. To the north of Brahmā one should draw Sarasvatī with her retinue, with the mudrā of the vīṇā. Near her one should draw Pṛthivī, the earth goddess, with her retinue, with the vase mudrā. To her north one should draw Mahāśrī with her retinue, with the lotus mudrā or the mudrā of the wish-fulfilling jewel. In the quarter of Īśāna one should draw Īśāna, lord of the bhūtas, with his retinue, with the trident mudrā. To the south of Śakra one should draw Sūrya, the sun, with his retinue, with the chariot mudrā. In the quarter of Agni one should draw the sage Agni with his retinue, with the mudrā of granting fearlessness. In the south one should draw Yama with his retinue, with the mudrā of Yama's cudgel. To his east one should draw the bell of the Lord of Death. For Kālarātrī it is the crest. For Kālī it is the noose. For Raudrī, the consort of Rudra, it is the trident. For Brāhmī, the consort of Brahmā, it is the lotus. For Vaiṣṇavī, the consort of Viṣṇu, it is the wheel. For Kaumārī, the consort of Kumāra, it is the short spear. For Vārāhī, the consort of Varāha, it is the hammer. For Cāmuṇḍī it is the skull. For Aindrī, the consort of Indra, it is the vajra. For Kauberī, the consort of Kubera, it is the club. To the west of Yama one should draw the pretas with their retinues, with the mouth mudrā. The piśācas should be drawn with the mudrā of the vajra net. The oppressor spirits should be drawn with the mudrā of the iron vajra. The shadow oppressors should be drawn with the mudrā of the vajra parasol. The apasmāras should be drawn with the mudrā of the hands pressed to the chest.

The ḍākinīs should be drawn with the mudrā of the pressed-down hand. The rākṣasīs should be drawn with the mudrā of the vajra fangs or the mudrā of the vajra tongue. The piśācīs should be drawn with the hand mudrā of the carelessly held vajra. In the quarter of Nairṛti one should draw Nairṛti with his retinue, with the sword mudrā. To the south of the western gate one should draw Varuṇa with his retinue, and the nāga kings, Sāgara and the rest, with their retinues, with the noose mudrā. Then, near the gate, one should draw Maheśvara (Śiva) with the trident mudrā. Near him one should draw the Averter of Austerities with the mudrā of the raised trident. On the northern side of the gate one should draw Gaṇapati with his retinue, with the axe mudrā. To his north one should draw the asura kings, Vemacitrin and the rest, with their retinues, with the mudrā of the club and vajra. In the quarter of Vāyu one should draw Vāyu, the wind god, with his retinue, with the banner mudrā. In the north one should draw the yakṣa kings, Vaiśravaṇa and the rest, with their retinues, with the mudrā of the club bearing a vajra. To their east one should draw Viṣṇu with his retinue, with the wheel mudrā. Below him one should draw the garuḍas with their retinues, with the mudrā of wings. To their east one should draw the gandharva kings, Possessor of Many-Colored Garlands and the rest, and the kinnara kings, Druma and the rest, with their retinues, each with their own mudrā. There, the mudrās of the gandharvas are small drums and cymbals. The mudrās of the kinnaras are the vīṇā and other instruments, played and held aloft. Then one should draw the great mahoragas and the great rivers with their own mudrā of flowing water. Then one should draw Prajāpati, Lord of Creatures, with the mudrā of the hand reciting the Veda. To their east one should draw the moon with his retinue, with the mudrā of the white lotus and the vase.

At the western gate one should draw Kīlikīla's own mudrā; he should be drawn with the mudrā of the fierce vajra. At the northern gate two gatekeepers should be drawn: there, the yakṣa king Vaiśravaṇa should be drawn with the club mudrā, and the son of the nāga king Vāsuki should be drawn with the noose mudrā. At the eastern gate two kings of wrath should be drawn: there, Trailokyavijaya should be drawn with the vajra mudrā, and Nīladaṇḍa should be drawn with the cudgel mudrā. At the southern gate one should draw Durdharṣa with the hammer mudrā. Facing them one should draw the vajra mudrā.

On the lower side should be drawn the ocean, and on the shore of the ocean the four sisters, each with her own mudrā. There, the mudrā of Tumburu is the club; the mudrā of Jayā is the three-pointed short spear; the mudrā of Vijayā is the lotus; the mudrā of Ajitā is the wheel; and the mudrā of Aparājitā is the vajra. To the south of the four sisters one should draw Kārttikeya, son of the Pleiades, with his retinue, with the mudrā of the short spear. To the north one should draw the kings of the vidyādharas with the mudrās of the sword, the cloth and so on.

The places of all the retinue are drawn as circles. All those others thus mentioned are made as circles, and all of them are done after being consecrated with the fierce vajra. Whatever maṇḍala activity is to be done, all of it is done after being consecrated with the fierce vajra heart mantra.

From the Vajrapāṇi Empowerment, the rite for laying out the maṇḍala.

The one who knows the rite, having in this way
Drawn the supreme vajra maṇḍala,
And having performed the consecration,
Should very carefully make offerings.
Then vases whose bases are not black,
Whose mouths have a lustrous rim,
With long necks and large bellies,
Twenty-five of them, should be obtained.
They are filled with seeds and the five precious substances,
And filled to the brim with scented water;
Their mouths are covered with ornaments of leafy twigs,
And likewise their mouths are filled with fruits;
They are bound with cords about their necks,
And arranged in colors as is fitting.
At all of the four gates
The vases, as before,
Are said to be eight.
The ninth is for sprinkling:
With the fierce vajra, in every way,
It should carefully be empowered with mantra.
The tenth should be known to be in the center:
It is offered to the Cakravartin.
The porticoes are beautiful,
Made of the four kinds of precious materials,
Or else made of lovely wood:
Sandalwood, or any other will do,
Not in pieces, not broken,
Without cracks and without knots;
Varuṇa wood and śamī,
Or salakrī and kaṅkata will also do.
Precious bells are hung from them,
And they are adorned with silk, cloth and so on.
They are eight, seven or six cubits high,
And are to be furnished with garlands of flowers.
Above, a precious canopy is stretched,
And ornamental garlands of pearls are hung;
Silk and cloth should be hung as well;
It should be larger than the maṇḍala.
Banners of various colors
Should be hung in the four directions,
Strung upon threads,
And furnished with sweet-sounding bells.
Parasols of various colors
Should be drawn as is fitting.
Made of the four precious materials,
Or, lacking these, made of copper,
Four vessels of offering water
The wise one should set out.
The various scents that are taught
Are saffron and sandalwood,
Spṛkkā and the like: any of these will do;
Nothing from any part of a living creature is allowed.
Having offered various kinds of incense,
Aloeswood and resinous pine,
Camphor and śrīvāsaka,
Cepok and costus,
The resin of the sāla tree,
Or else pine resin should be offered.
One should recite the Vajrodbhava spell
Or the fierce vajra mantra.
Utpala and the supreme lotus,
Campaka and nāga tree,
Kesara and mallikā,
Aśoka and kyanar,
Tagara and punnāga,
And the beautiful dongka flower,
Karavīra and kumuda,
Jasmine and the flowers of dhanukari:
Flowers should be offered as is fitting.
Likewise various foods for the deities:
Milk porridge, curd and cooked rice,
The very best butter and honey,
Breads and laḍḍus,
Sweetmeats steeped in honey,
Aśovarti and venaka,
The offering known as khaṇḍa,
Śirebhedaka and modaka,
Garbhodakaraka,
Berek and dvetse,
Śaṣkula and parpaṭa,
Grain broth and drengbu,
Shunthapālikā,
Ghārikā and svastika cakes,
Peas, chickpeas and zhuteu,
Roots and tubers,
And various fruits should be offered.
Colorful pulse dishes
And moist sgeu should certainly be offered;
Pulse balls large and small,
Bhegana and saṃbhadhi,
Infusions mixed with molasses,
Prepared with great care,
And various boiled molasses confections:
All kinds of these should be offered in every way.
Curds of various kinds,
And churned curd with ground molasses, should certainly be offered;
Milk that has been well strained,
And likewise drinks, should be offered as well.
Foam of khari, sugar,
Butter, molasses, sesame oil,
Honey and salt,
Known as wholesome, should be offered.
Drinks that have been infused,
Betel and bedding,
Fine and beautiful utensils,
And medicinal ointments of various kinds,
Very carefully, according to the rite,
Should be offered to the bodhisattva protectors.
Then also to each one
A pair of cloth garments should be offered.
Mirrors, lovely eye medicine,
Conch shells and likewise gorocana,
In order to complete the accumulations for awakening,
Should be offered to all the protectors.
Having thus worshiped the protectors well
In every way,
The mantrin should then perform the fire offering;
He should do so with the heart mantra according to the rite.
Twenty-one oblations
Should be offered in the fire according to the rite:
Pleasing substances that accomplish everything,
Curd, milk and honey.
Four readers of texts as well
Should be stationed in the four directions.
There, what is placed in the east
Is the noble Perfection of Wisdom.
The sūtra placed in the north
The holy ones hold to be the Sublime Golden Light.
What is to be placed in the west
Is the Secret of the Tathāgata.
The great Array of the Stem
Should be known to be on the southern side.
Just as for the deities of the maṇḍala,
These too should be respectfully honored.
This reading of the holy Dharma
Is the best of all protections.
Then all the gods
Are delighted at that time,
And those in the world who are unruly
Go off in every direction.
Therefore, in every way,
The holy Dharma should be read at that time.

Having summoned all the disciples,
He should give them the three refuges,
And tell them to commit no wrongdoing.
He should have them take up the vows as well,
And the wise one, according to this rite,
Should have them generate the mind of awakening.
Then, with the water of the fierce vajra mantra,
He should sprinkle them all.
Having fully proclaimed the pledges,
The wise one goes inside,
Has them change all their clothes,
And should directly empower them with mantra.
Then the compassionate one
Covers the faces of those disciples,
And the mantrin has them bind
The pledge mudrā of the Cakravartin.
Having placed flowers in their hands,
He then imagines a syllable at their hearts,
And before all the protectors
He has them cast those flowers.
The wise one places at the disciple's
Right ear the vajra bell,
And at the left the great goddess
Called Vajra Drum.
Wherever the flowers fall,
That deity is held to be his.
Having thus brought the disciples in,
He should then teach them the pledges.
In order to bring about pacification, the wise one
Should perform the fire offering thoroughly, as follows.

Then Mañjuśrī, in youthful form, spoke these words to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi:

"Pacification and enrichment,
Subjugation, and likewise fierce conduct:
The rites of fire offering, in brief,
Great being, please declare.
Please speak too of all the rites in full,
And likewise of their distinct actions.
Great being, at what place
Is the hearth pit for the fire to be made?
Of what kind is the kindling wood,
And what action is to be undertaken?
All-pervading Lord, please explain the hearth pit,
And please dispel my doubts."

When he had said this, the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, spoke these words to Mañjuśrī, in youthful form:

"Excellent, excellent, excellent, great being!
Whatever questions you have asked,
All of them I shall declare.
Listen with an undistracted mind.
In the center of the second maṇḍala
Make the pit of the fire-offering hearth.
As for fixing its measure,
It is stated to be about one cubit.
Having dug it about a span deep,
A vajra should be placed in its center.
A rim too is made there,
And the wise one should make it beautiful in form.
Kuśa grass, its tips bound with its roots,
Is spread out there.
Thus, well and all around,
The mantrin should spread it thoroughly.
Not too near and not too far,
Having made the fire-offering hearth,
And having done so properly,
Then begin the fire offering there.
Seated on a mat of kuśa grass,
Having called the tathāgatas to mind,
Then kindle the fire,
And merely sprinkle it to purify it.
After that, into that fire
A single flower should be placed.
Then the mudrā should be bound;
With his own mudrā he is summoned.
Here the mudrā of fire is this:
Extend the right hand straight,
And set the thumb near the little finger.
With this mantra he should be summoned:
namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā agnaye hā vya kā vya vā ha na ya svāhā
Then the intelligent one, with the vidyā-mantra,
Makes the fire offering of oblations.
Having given the oblations to him,
The disciples should be placed on the left.
And for the sake of each one of them
Burn twenty-five oblations apiece.
Rites of many various kinds
Are performed in this way:
Pacification and enrichment,
Subjugation and fierce conduct.
The fire offering for pacification is with milk:
Butter, milk, and honey.
For enrichment, curd and cooked rice,
And whatever is taught elsewhere.
For fierce conduct, thorns are prescribed.
For subjugation, as before.
Those who desire the rite, the fearless,
Should perform the fire offering as is fitting.
From each of them, according to what is fitting,
The offering fee is drawn forth.
Or else, whatever one has in mind,
Let that be given as one pleases.
At that time, to the master
The disciples offer the fee.
If it is not given, the pledge is broken,
And accomplishment will not be attained.
Just as the sun and the moon, the pair,
Are held by the wise to be the eyes of the world,
So too for these people
The master is held to be like an eye.
Just as the element of fire
Burns up all firewood,
So should the master be known:
He burns the firewood of ignorance.
Just as the element of water
Cuts off all the torment of the world,
So too the master
Should be known to cut off all torment.
Just as the element of wind
Always sustains the whole world,
So the master, for the disciple,
Brings virtue fully about.
Just as, for all embodied beings,
The earth is renowned as their support,
So too the master
Is held to be the support of the Dharma.
Just as the Blessed One Vairocana
Has realized and fully understood all dharmas,
So should the master be known.
Just as the glorious Vajradhara
Holds all secrets in harmony,
So too the master
Here holds the secret of mantra.
He always speaks
The praises of the Three Jewels,
And he speaks as well the great praises
Of all the bodhisattvas.
Just as the Buddha is like an eye,
Showing the true path,
So too the fearless mantrin
Shows this abode of the Great Vehicle.
As I am, so is the mantrin;
As the master is, so is the Victor.
Hold to the master's good qualities;
Never take up his faults.
Holding to good qualities, one becomes accomplished;
With faults, accomplishment is lost.
If one disparages the master,
One is short-lived in the world,
And likewise goes to the lower realms,
And is born into castes such as fishermen.
This is the source of every wrong;
The wise do not slander the guru.
Those who desire the Dharma
Should always and continually please the master.
Wherever a child of the Victors dwells,
That ground is like a stūpa.
There all the children of the Victors,
And I as well, are lesser than his heart.
This supreme rite of fire offering,
Hero, that is declared to you,
Was taught in former times by the protectors,
Wishing to benefit sentient beings.
Whoever, in the rites of fire offering,
Understands them in this way,
Will, just as the buddhas have taught,
Swiftly attain accomplishment."

From the Vajrapāṇi Empowerment, the analysis of the words on fire, arranged as a chapter.

The Noble Vajrapāṇi Empowerment, the fourth fascicle.

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, spoke these words to Mañjuśrī, in youthful form:

"I shall declare the empowerment maṇḍala
Praised by the buddhas of the past.
Having set down one measure of three cubits,
The outer maṇḍala should be drawn.
Its measure is four cubits,
And one faces toward the side of the maṇḍala.
There too the mantrin, making the parts equal,
Should make it perfectly square all around.
The mantrin, abiding in the way of mantra,
Takes his seat in the quarter of fire, the southeast,
And then, facing north,
Snaps the chalk line according to the rite.
Leaving his former place,
He takes his seat in the quarter of Īśāna,
And as far as the region of the wind's quarter, the northwest,
Strikes the line of the northern side.
Seated in the quarter of the wind,
Facing toward the southern side,
As far as the quarter of Nairṛti,
He strikes the line of the western side.
Likewise, leaving the quarter of the wind,
He takes his seat in the quarter of Nairṛti,
And then, facing east,
The mantrin, recollecting the secret mantra,
As far as the quarter of fire,
Strikes the line of the southern side.

In accordance with this rite, all of it
Should be made perfectly square.
The wise one, at the very beginning,
Having divided the inner tier into eight parts,
Following the manner of a wheel maṇḍala,
Should then make the second tier.
The lotus in the center, to state it briefly,
Has eight petals together with their anthers;
In all the spaces between the petals
Vajras should be drawn.
On them, too, draw the heart mantras.
Having urged them on with their own heart mantras,
The eight guardians of the directions as well
Should be arranged in their proper places.
There, in the inner corners,
The world protectors are arranged.
In order to pacify all obstructors,
Set Amṛtakuṇḍalin at the gate.
Just as for the deities of the maṇḍala,
There too make offerings in full.
Cloths, canopies, and bells,
Parasols, and flowers should also be offered.
Garlands of pearls and the like, and garlands of flowers,
Should be offered as far as one is able and one's means allow.

In its center draw the image of a vajra that fully fills the ten directions. With vases of the four kinds of precious substance, filled with jewels and with all medicines, blessed by Ratnakusuma, Saṃkusumita, Amitābha, Akṣobhya, Samantabhadra, Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin, Maitreya, and Kṣitigarbha, one confers empowerment upon the crown of the head.

The mantrin, having placed the disciple well
Upon that sacred lotus,
Himself makes offerings with flowers and incense,
And should offer lamps and offering water.
With parasols, victory banners, and flags,
With the sounds of cymbals raised,
And with the sounds of music and many cymbals,
Having made him truly and openly rejoice,
In the presence of all the protectors,
With full attention he confers the empowerment.
Just as the lord of the four continents,
The Cakravartin king, is empowered,
So the mantrin confers empowerment
With wonders and with every auspicious thing.
With flowers and with incense,
And with the finest fragrances he again makes offerings,
And, placing a vajra in the right hand
And a wheel in the left,
He speaks to the disciple thus:
"You will become a vajra master.
From now on, you who possess the vajra,
Proclaim the vajra Dharma.
All the buddhas have placed in your hand
The vajra from which samādhi arises.
From this day forth, for the whole world,
You are a Vajrapāṇi of great miraculous power.
To cut down those who are hostile,
And to tame those
Who do harm to the teaching,
The guides have given you the vajra.
Just as a Cakravartin king
Is empowered so as to be made sovereign,
So, to empower a king of Dharma,
The wise one proclaims the vajra empowerment."
With the mudrā of the vajra wheel
Protection should be made for him.
The disciple proclaiming the vajra Dharma
Is taken by the hand,
Led into that great maṇḍala,
And the pledges are announced:
"You must not give up the mind of awakening.
You must never abandon loving-kindness, compassion,
And the wheel in your hand.
O intelligent one, the methods
And the vajra that has been placed with you
You must never give up.
O intelligent one, this is the pledge
Of the bodhisattvas and the buddhas.
Just as one guards one's own body,
So must you guard this.
Doing so, you repay the kindness of the buddhas,
And are called a child of the buddhas.
Otherwise the pledge is broken,
And you will not become a vajra-bearer.
Of all the vajra-holders
The family is the mind of awakening;
Their sign is said to be compassion;
Method is taught as mastery.
Having placed the vajra, you have donned the armor;
You bear the vajra weapon.
The gods bow down to you;
You provide protection everywhere.
The buddhas and bodhisattvas, too,
Protect you in every way."

From the Vajrapāṇi Empowerment, the section on the stages of empowerment is complete.

Then the glorious Mañjuśrī,
His eyes wide with wonder,
Spoke these words to the Vajra Holder:
"Excellent, hero of great splendor!
The maṇḍala that you have explained,
The sacred, supreme vajra maṇḍala
Through which accomplishment is attained,
Please teach it too, together with its great secret.
For all the deities, each in turn,
And for the wise bodhisattvas,
Their mudrās together with their mantras,
And their thorough application in the practices;
The firm establishment of meditative concentration, the instructions,
And the Dharma of the vidyās: please speak of these too.
Observances, conduct, and signs,
Every aspect of place and time,
Pacification and enrichment,
And likewise fierce conduct,
The summoning of bhūtas,
And the summoning of wealth too,
The general characteristics of secret mantra
And its particulars as well: please explain them.
Who in this world is cast aside?
Who goes elsewhere? Please tell.
The results in this world and in others,
Hard to find, which the Victor has taught,
And how an action done by one
Has its result experienced by another,
And the result of this maṇḍala and of the mantras:
Please explain these to me.
The appearance of a buddha is hard to find;
A human body too is hard to find;
The appearance of secret mantra is hard to find;
Perfect faith is hard to come by.
All the buddhas today intend that you,
Vajrapāṇi of great miraculous power,
Should hold the great secret
For the work of this teaching.
So please explain in full
The rites throughout this maṇḍala."

When he had said this, the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, spoke these words to Mañjuśrī, in youthful form: "Excellent, Mañjuśrī, excellent! These words of your question are well spoken. Therefore, Mañjuśrī, listen carefully and keep it in mind. Mañjuśrī, the great secret of the maṇḍala is this: whatever the deity is, that is I. Whatever I am, that is the maṇḍala. Whatever is the abode of the deity, that is my abode. Whatever is my abode, that is the maṇḍala. What abides in the maṇḍala is secret mantra; without the maṇḍala there is no secret mantra. Furthermore, Mañjuśrī, there are maṇḍalas with form and maṇḍalas without form. Of these, the one with form is drawn as a picture; the one without form is not drawn. Furthermore, what is to be drawn is what may properly be drawn; what is not drawn is what may not properly be drawn. Young one, of these, by the one that is not drawn all actions are performed.

The one that is drawn is no more than a cause for rites and actions; it serves to arouse faith in others. By the other, one blesses the abode of the deity: it is the maṇḍala that accomplishes both aims, the maṇḍala possessed of the maṇḍala in which rite and yoga are not separate. A master who possesses action, rite, time, agency, capacity, retention, what is to be done in meditative concentration, the instructions, and all the yogas, having himself obtained empowerment, should draw the maṇḍala.

Young one, if you ask what the instructions are, they are these: the master and the disciple. The master is one endowed with the qualities of a master. The disciple follows his conduct, acts according to his words, and does not deceive. That valid authority is the instruction. Mañjuśrī, in youthful form, if you ask further what valid authority is: having first understood the undeceiving meaning of the sūtras and of secret mantra, one tastes its flavor; and since that meaning is extremely subtle, one does not fall away from it but becomes steadfast in it; to designate that as "the nature of reality" is the explanation of the instructions. Whatever the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha has taught is just so and does not become otherwise; therefore it is the valid authority for the world with its gods; it is undeceiving. Its realization, its direct actualization, and the attainment of its result: that is the nature of reality. Furthermore, the very application to the yoga that brings about the attainment of that nature of reality is the method of reasoning.

Mañjuśrī, furthermore, a mantrin endowed with qualities such as these is regarded by the Victors as a master of the maṇḍala.

This great secret of the maṇḍala
Was taught by the Victors of old.
Otherwise, mere drawing
Is devoid of cause and result.

Mañjuśrī, furthermore, the deity is of two kinds: of the nature of the result, and of the nature of maturation. Of these, the nature of the result is the realization of the nature of reality. Whatever is the direct attainment of that nature of reality is the result. Whatever is the result is maturation. Whatever is maturation is an entity. Whatever is an entity has fallen into convention. Whatever has fallen into convention and whatever has the nature of convention is designated. That is activity. Whatever is activity is karma. In whatever is the nature of reality there is no result, no realization, no direct actualization, and no purification; yet the buddhas, the blessed ones, and the bodhisattvas who abide on the ground of karma and maturation teach result, attainment, realization, and purification. They speak of them, they express them, they designate them, they establish them, they elucidate them, they explain them; with various modes of teaching they apply conventions.

Mañjuśrī, furthermore, the buddhas, the blessed ones, and the bodhisattvas who abide on the ground free of karma and maturation designate what is called "this world," what is called "the world beyond," "birth," "old age," "death," "sickness," "decline," "prosperity," "the conditioned," "the unconditioned," "going," "coming," "knowledge," "ignorance," "the wise," "realization," "the attached," "aversion," "delusion," "bondage," "liberation," "self," "no-self," "craving," "suchness," "the true," "the undeceiving," "eye," "ear," "nose," "tongue," "body," "mind," "form," "sound," "smell," "taste," "touch," "Buddha," "Dharma," "Saṅgha," "śrāvaka," "pratyekabuddha," "stream-enterer," "once-returner," "non-returner," "one who has entered upon realizing the fruit of stream-entry," "once-returner," "one who has entered upon realizing the fruit of once-returning," "non-returner," "one who has entered upon realizing the fruit of non-returning," "arhat," "free from attachment," and "one who has seen the truth"; and, Mañjuśrī, furthermore, they designate what is called "god," "nāga," "yakṣa," "gandharva," "asura," "garuḍa," "kinnara," "mahoraga," "human," "nonhuman," "man," "woman," and "neuter." Mañjuśrī, in this way the buddhas, the blessed ones, and the bodhisattvas who abide in freedom from karma and maturation make designations in accordance with the differing dispositions of sentient beings.

Mañjuśrī said, "How do they designate, or make known, that dharma which is like space?"

Vajrapāṇi said, "Mañjuśrī, they designate it, or make it known, on that very ground free of karma and maturation. Mañjuśrī, freedom from karma and maturation is emptiness, and even upon that emptiness one designates 'one' or 'two.' Look at how they make designations in this way, by indications of number and by the conventions of names. Mañjuśrī, furthermore, although the element of space is free of karma and maturation, one still designates space as 'blue,' 'yellow,' 'red,' 'narrow,' 'vast,' 'pure,' and 'impure.' Mañjuśrī, in the same way the buddhas, the blessed ones, and the bodhisattvas who abide on the ground free of karma and maturation make designations. And although those designations are made by what is free of karma and maturation, childish ordinary beings, who are in error, simply fail to understand them. As the doorway for leading them to see correctly, just as things truly are, the Victors have taught this maṇḍala.

From the coming together of three,
The arising of mantra is here taught.

The mudrā, too, arises in the same way:
It has no form and cannot be shown;
At all times, throughout the three worlds,
It is unobstructed and uninterrupted.
Just as, relying on a base board,
When the fire drill is twirled,
From the cause, a person's effort,
A powerful fire arises,
So, from the coming together of three,
Secret mantra and the five great mudrās
Come to arise; and once arisen,
They perform the actions conceived in the mind.

Mañjuśrī, for example, the moment vidyādharas think of them, divine food, drink, ornaments, horses, elephants, chariots, women, houses, celestial palaces, and all such things arise. Mañjuśrī, in the same way, from the coming together of secret mantra, mudrā, and deity, everything arises. Mañjuśrī, it is like this: for example, so long as the precious wish-fulfilling jewel, free of every flaw, has not ceased to exist, whatever one thinks of will all arise. Mañjuśrī, the way of mantra too should be understood in the same way. Mañjuśrī, when one has wholly given up one's own form and attained the divine pride that is free of doubt, then that nature of reality of secret mantra reveals the nature of reality; and then, when it shows its aspect through words, it becomes capable of action. Mañjuśrī, see how the nature of reality, when relied upon, becomes able to perform actions.

Mañjuśrī, it is like this: for example, relying on a seal, the raised impression of the seal comes about. Without the substance, the impression of the seal does not come about; and even if the substance is there, without the act and the action of pressing, the impression of the seal does not come about. Mañjuśrī, this is because the two depend on each other. From the effort a person makes, is it the substance that comes about here, or is it the act of the action that comes about? Which is it? Because before and after contradict each other, nothing comes about apart from the mere arising, in dependence, of secret mantra or mudrā.

Mañjuśrī, as many afflictions as sentient beings have, so many applications should be seen that other bodhisattvas cannot overcome. If one asks who is able to prevail over these, it is the dharmadhātu endowed with mudrā and secret mantra that prevails. Mañjuśrī, it is because the dharmadhātu itself is conceived in many aspects that the tathāgatas and the bodhisattvas establish: "This is secret mantra; this is mudrā; this is the deity." That dharmadhātu is formless and cannot be shown; it is not graspable and cannot be disturbed. It is established by the Tathāgata's establishing it as dependent origination. Because it is dependent origination, it performs every action.

Mañjuśrī, furthermore, as to what was asked, "How is it that when one person performs an action, another enjoys its fruit?": Mañjuśrī, it is like this. For example, a sovereign son of the gods who has gained mastery over his own mind enjoys various pleasures just as he wishes. That is, having emanated divine houses, celestial palaces and so on, horses, elephants, chariots, women, food to eat and to chew and so on, he both enjoys them himself and gives them to others as well. Why is that? Because he himself has accomplished that mantra. Mañjuśrī, in the same way, what is done by one is enjoyed by another. Mañjuśrī, there is also the case in which one acts and many enjoy, as with a Cakravartin king or a farmer.

Mañjuśrī, furthermore, as to what was asked, "Who casts aside this world, and who goes elsewhere?": Mañjuśrī, when the assemblage of mind and mental factors abides in the conditioned element, that is this world. When that same assemblage of mind and mental factors comes to abide in the unconditioned element, then the tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly complete buddhas designate it, saying, "He casts aside this world and goes to the world beyond." This is a figure of speech for a person who has realized the nature of reality. Why is that? The nature of reality abides in the unconditioned element; it is never stained by the impurity of the conditioned. It is like this: for example, a vajra is not stained by any of the flaws of the impurity of murky mud. In the same way, that nature of reality is not stained by any of the flaws of the conditioned.

Mañjuśrī, childish ordinary beings, who belong to the conditioned element, have fallen into an afflicted continuum; born into existence through volition arising from grasping at "I" and "mine," they bring about what is afflicted. When the time of their death draws near and this assemblage of formations ceases, the very pride that thinks "I am a god" or "I am a human" attaches itself to that continuum. It is like this: for example, a person looking at his face in the disk of a well-polished mirror thinks, "My face is handsome, with such and such special qualities," or "It is not handsome." Although the reflection is neither different from him nor not different, yet because of confusion, grasping at "I" arises in his mind.

Mañjuśrī, what is the visible result? It is this: the removal of poison, fever and the like by means of secret mantras. What is the result not of this life? It is the attainment of the fruit of realizing meditative concentration, which is direct and free of the net of conceptual thought. The result is taught as established by trustworthy scripture, wholly beyond the domain of logic, of inference and of what is to be inferred; as endowed with yoga and reasoning; and as having the aspect of one's own experience. This is the result not of this life.

Mañjuśrī, furthermore, as to what was asked, "Speak of the actions of fierce conduct": since they come to be necessary, one should undertake wrathfulness itself. With a peaceful mind, with compassion, with a nature of loving-kindness, and looking to the result of one's own action, one joins the form of the deity and one's own wrathful form so that they are not different; by this, the fire too is in wrathful form, and the other as well is in wrathful form.

Having summoned the target by means of the wrathful one who wields mastery, in the form of the mantra, one swiftly presses down with the left foot on the chest, or the heart, or the head, or the belly, or the waist, or the calf, or the foot, or the back. Then, whether any living being is laughing, or making love, or playing, or staying put, or traveling on the road, or lying down, or sitting, or standing, or dwelling a hundred leagues beyond a river, or even protected by gods and the like, one kindles the fire within oneself and performs a fire offering of twenty-one oblations. For the peaceful it is declared to be peaceful; for the unpeaceful it is declared to be unpeaceful.

Then Vajrapāṇi, in order to accomplish the maṇḍala of the wheel of unobstructed speech and to make the teaching fully fruitful, again entered into the samādhi called Essence of the Array of the Buddha's Speech. As soon as the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi had entered into that samādhi, at that moment, with unequaled power, he spoke the secret mantra of the great pledge that enters the dharmadhātu:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra samaya dhṛke

As soon as the secret mantra of the vajra of the pledge had been spoken, throughout all the maṇḍalas of the buddhas' assemblies and throughout all the infinite world systems, a vajra sound that enters the pledge rang out with a roar.

Hearing this, the bodhisattvas,
In that very instant,
In the presence of all the buddhas,
Obtained the dhāraṇī of supreme vajra power.

Again he entered into the samādhi called Blessing of Vast Expanse That Defeats the Four Māras, and spoke this secret mantra, the great protection, mighty, arisen from the splendor of the ten powers:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra gini vajra śu u la ni vajra mu ta re va li nam agra mahā va le hi li hi li mi li mi li śi lo śi lo ṭi lo ṭi lo ṭa ṭa le mahā caṇḍi ku ruṃ gho na śve ta padma mā li ni hi hi he he rakṣa rakṣa mahā sa ma ya a nu ga te svāhā

Then that entire assembly spoke these words with one voice:

"Oh! This sacred protection
Vajrapāṇi has proclaimed
For the sake of all beings
Who follow the practice of secret mantra."

Then Vajrapāṇi again spoke the treasury of secret mantras for all the methods of the maṇḍala:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajra agra dha ra a na lo vi śva saṃ bha va svāhā

This is the mantra of the great blazing vajra flame.

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ śca ṇḍi vajra a rci spha ra ṇa svāhā

This is the mantra of the great vajra radiance.

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ mahā caṇḍa vajra ka va ca vi sphu ra vajrī ga svāhā

This is the mantra of the vajra armor.

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ mahā vajra agra dha ri te jo dag te svāhā

This is the mantra of the crown of the head of the supreme vajra.

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ vajra ananta ga ti hi svāhā

This is the mantra of the great vajra uṣṇīṣa.

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ tathāgate ā śā pa ri pūr cintā ma ṇi rā ja vajra hūṃ svāhā

This is the mantra of the vajra ūrṇā, the curl between the brows.

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ tathāgate lo ca na jñā na vajra svāhā

This is the mantra of the vajra eye.

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ oṃ vajra ā na naṃ svāhā

This is the mantra of the vajra mouth.

namaḥ sarva ta thī ga te bhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarva tathāgate sa tya dharma pra tiṣṭhi te vajra ga pu ru sa ra sva sti vu ha a laṃ kā ra garbha vajra ji hvā hūṃ bha ga va ti sarva sa tva santo ṣa ṇa ka ri svāhā

This is the mantra of the vajra tongue.

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ vajra agra sa me bhyo vi śva rū pa dha ra svāhā

This is the mantra of the vajra body.
