Blessed One, if one wishes to accomplish this essence, one should recite it a hundred thousand times. At the end, one should perform a fire offering a hundred thousand times in a maṇḍala of the mātṛkās with oblations of mustard seed mixed with beer. Then, at midnight, the consort of the Boar herself will come in the form of a cat. Or else the fragrance of divine incense will arise. Then one should know that one has succeeded, and from then on she will perform every action as one wishes."

From the Noble Vajrapāṇi Empowerment, the sixth fascicle.

Then the consort of Kubera spoke her own essence:

namaḥ sarvatathāgatebhyaḥ sarvamukhebhyaḥ sarvathā cidariṇi kurabinde dāndrapāṇi svāhā

"Blessed One, whoever keeps this essence in mind, no fear at all of royal punishment will arise for him. Day by day all his wealth will increase. My four handmaidens will always remain with him as servants. Every purpose he has in mind will be accomplished. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should eat alms food, hold a club in one's hand, stay at the foot of a solitary tree, and recite this essence thirty thousand times. At the end, in that same place, one should worship with whatever one can afford and perform a fire offering a hundred thousand times with oblations of puffed rice mixed with curd, honey and butter. Then one will succeed, and from then on she will do whatever one wishes."

Then the consort of the Great Dreadful-Looking One bowed to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi and said: "Blessed One, I too offer my own essence in this great maṇḍala:

namaḥ sarvatathāgatebhyaḥ sarvamukhebhyaḥ sarvathā he kunkhi mahānakhi śakuni ulūkamukhi yamadūtī bitace svāhā

Blessed One, whoever constantly keeps this essence in mind, all his wishes will be completely fulfilled. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should eat alms food, stay at a crossroads or at the foot of a solitary tree, and recite this essence a hundred thousand times. Then one should worship with whatever one can afford and perform a fire offering a hundred thousand times with oblations of ash from a childbirth. Then the consort of the Great Dreadful-Looking One will come, assuming the form of a pūtanā demoness, and one should offer her incense of owl feathers. Then one will succeed, and she will do whatever one wishes."

Then all the great rivers, the great river Gaṅgā and the others, bowed to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi and said: "Blessed One, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala:

namaḥ sarvatathāgatebhyaḥ sarvamukhebhyaḥ sarvathā manasi manonuge gali bimale svāhā

Blessed One, whoever constantly keeps this essence in mind, no fear of water at all will arise for him. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should enter water deep enough to cover the throat and recite this essence a hundred thousand times. Then one should worship with whatever one can afford, make figures about six finger-widths in size from flour of ground śāli rice kneaded with curd and honey, and perform a fire offering eight thousand times. Then one will succeed, and from then on, even if one enters rivers, no fear will arise. One may dwell with them for as long as one wishes; they will care for one as for their own child and grant whatever one wishes."

Then all the gandharva kings, Variegated Garland and the others, bowed with palms joined toward where Vajrapāṇi was, and said:

"Blessed One, we too offer this our own essence in this great maṇḍala:

namaḥ sarvatathāgatebhyaḥ sarvamukhebhyaḥ sarvathā bhagabate gandharbarājapriye svāhā

Whoever keeps this essence of ours in mind, we will always follow after him. With joyful minds we will grant him permission for all his purposes. We will remain all around him. Even at the time of his death, we will play cymbals and drums, hold a canopy above him, and grant him the recollection of the Buddha, the Dharma and the Saṅgha. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should first go into a great wilderness, eat fruit as one's food, and recite this essence two hundred thousand times. Then one should worship with whatever one can afford, burn incense of turuṣka, and recite throughout the night, reciting until some gandharva king or other comes and places a garland of flowers on one's head. Then one will be honored by all among the gandharvas, and even if one enters any of the abodes of the gandharvas, one will never fall from one's state. In short, they will grant whatever one wishes."

Then the kings of the vidyādharas, the vidyādhara king Crest and the others, bowed with palms joined toward where the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi was, and said: "Blessed One, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala:

namaḥ sarvatathāgatebhyaḥ sarvamukhebhyaḥ sarvathā gaganasaṃbhabe gaganagauri utpattike hiri khiri khiri sanasara bahaṃ gami svāhā

Blessed One, this is the essence of all vidyādharas. It is by this that we are called kings of the vidyādharas. It is by this that we are called those who make offerings and render service to all the tathāgatas. In short, by merely wishing, we accomplish every purpose. Blessed One, if anyone who engages in bodhisattva conduct through the gateway of secret mantra constantly keeps this essence in mind, we will bring about for him accomplishments, might and steadfast strength. If one is to accomplish this essence, one should go to a mountain peak and, following the previous rite, recite it a million times. Then one should worship with whatever one can afford, smear a maṇḍala with cow dung, and perform a fire offering a hundred thousand times with oblations of butter. Then one will succeed. One will go wherever one wishes without obstruction."

Then all the great rākṣasīs, the Toothed One and the others, bowed with palms joined toward where the great bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi was, and said: "Blessed One, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala:

namaḥ sarvatathāgatebhyaḥ sarvamukhebhyaḥ sarvathā krekari kotare pitike svāhā

Blessed One, whoever constantly keeps this essence in mind, whether he is in a great wilderness, in a charnel ground, in water, in fire, in the ocean, or among rākṣasīs, we will protect him. We will bring him peace and well-being. We will also turn away from him every punishment and the like. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should go alone to an empty house or a great charnel ground, riding a donkey, eating only food that has not been begged, holding a knife in one's hand, and wearing a skirt of jackal skin or cat skin, and recite the essence a hundred thousand times. At the end, one should worship with fish and whatever else one can afford, burn incense of cow dung, and recite throughout the night. One should recite until the birds called bheruṇaṭika cry out in the four directions, or until blazing flames come from the donkey's mouth. Then, without fear, one should say, 'Welcome!' and present the guest-water offering. She will say, 'Excellent!' and vanish. Then one will succeed, and she will do whatever one wishes. Riding the donkey, one will go wherever one likes."

Then all the kings of the bhūtas, the bhūta king Heaped and the others, bowed with palms joined toward where the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi was, and said: "Blessed One, for the sake and benefit of the bodhisattvas who engage in bodhisattva conduct through the gateway of secret mantra, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala:

namaḥ sarvatathāgatebhyaḥ sarvamukhebhyaḥ sarvathā hisi hisi misi misi gili gili bhūteśvari svāhā

Blessed One, whoever constantly keeps this essence in mind, we will do as he commands. In particular, if he offers us the first portion of his food, we will accomplish all his aims. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should make a torma for all the bhūtas at the foot of a pati tree. One should burn sandalwood incense and, without eating any food, recite until all the bhūta kings arrive and give one gold, jewels, pearls and the like. Or else they will lead one to their own home and entertain one with every kind of thing. They will grant whatever one wishes. They will also perform various other kinds of actions."

Then all the piśācas, Gopāla and the others, bowed to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi and said: "Blessed One, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala:

namaḥ sarvatathāgatebhyaḥ sarvamukhebhyaḥ sarvatha mici mici khisi khisi svāhā

Blessed One, whoever keeps this essence in mind, we will always follow after him. We will grant him permission for all his purposes. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should recite this essence three hundred thousand times in an empty house. At the end, one should worship with whatever one can afford and perform a fire offering ten thousand times with oblations of grit or oblations of lac. Then some piśāca or other will come and entertain one with fine things. One should ask for whatever one wishes, and one will succeed. He will do whatever one wishes."

Then all the oppressor spirits, the oppressor Shaker and the others, said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Blessed One, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala.

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā guru guru svāhā Blessed One, if anyone keeps this essence constantly in mind, no fear of bhūtas at all will arise for them."

Then the shadow-oppressors, the Sprawler and the others, said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Blessed One, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala. namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā ginija miñji svahā Blessed One, if anyone keeps this essence constantly in mind, no fear of shadow-oppressors at all will arise for them."

Then all the luster-stealers, Timiṅgila and the others, bowed to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi and said: "Blessed One, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala. namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā gili gili svāhā Blessed One, if anyone keeps this essence in mind, we will not steal their luster."

Then all the hosts of hungry ghosts, Durmukha and the others, said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Blessed One, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala. namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā bhimiri bhimiri durlabhe svāhā Blessed One, if anyone keeps this essence in mind, we will do everything they need, just as they command."

Then all the young gods of Māra's class, Fine Victor and the others, said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Blessed One, we too offer in this great maṇḍala the essence called All the Power of Māra. namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā taṃgira svāhā Blessed One, if anyone keeps this essence constantly in mind, we will protect them and not come before them.

"If one wishes to accomplish it, one should first of all make oneself clean and put on clean clothes. Then, in a pleasure garden or at a shrine of the Great God (Śiva), with people singing, dancing and sounding the music of instruments, one should recite this essence four hundred thousand times with a joyful mind. Then, in that very place, one should make lavish offerings with every kind of requisite, fashion an image of Kāmadeva, the god of desire, out of every kind of incense, and set out a hundred butter lamps. If one then recites all night long to the six-toned song or to the melody of the hundred-feathered one, at midnight he will come together with Rati and Prīti. At that time he grants the supreme gift: the state of a vidyādhara, or becoming invisible, or else having him follow one as a companion. If one does not succeed, he grants eight excellent villages. One will possess the highest good fortune and will roam the earth like a second Kāmadeva."

Then the four great sisters, Jayā and the others, came from the four directions and sat down, and they said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Blessed One, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala."

Then Jayā spoke her own essence: "namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā caye cayodbhave svāhā Blessed One, if anyone keeps this essence of mine in mind, I will always follow after them. I will protect them. I will increase their glory. I will give them many sons. I will grant them good fortune. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should recite this essence a hundred thousand times, whether standing in a river that flows into the ocean or staying on a mountain peak. At the end, alone, in a clean and secluded place, one should smear a maṇḍala with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground and light a lamp of butter from a tawny cow. If at midnight one performs a fire offering with white mustard seed, one will then succeed. One will become dear to the hearts of all people, will go wherever one wishes, will take on whatever form one desires, and will be endowed with glory."

Then Vijayā spoke her own essence: "namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā jayottare jaya vijaya ninandani svāhā Blessed One, if anyone keeps this essence of mine in mind, I will always follow after them. I will grant them long life and perfect freedom from illness. I will protect them. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should again, as before, recite it two hundred thousand times. At the end, one should carry out the accomplishment as before. Even if one does not succeed, one will become dear to the hearts of all people, will possess great wealth, and will be quick in reply."

Then Ajitā spoke her own essence: "namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā anta ajite svāhā Blessed One, if anyone keeps this essence of mine constantly in mind, I will always follow after them in order to fulfill all their wishes. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should recite this essence three hundred thousand times as before. At the end, one should make offerings with whatever one can afford, burn guggulu incense all night long, and at midnight perform a fire offering with black sesame. Then one will succeed, and she will do whatever one wishes."

Then Aparājitā spoke her own essence: "namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā annarjave akutile aparājite svahā Blessed One, if anyone keeps this essence of mine in mind, I will always follow after them in order to protect them. If one recites this essence, one will become dear to the hearts of all people; one's words will be heeded and one will have good fortune. If one wishes to accomplish it, one should recite this essence four hundred thousand times as before. At the end, one should make offerings with whatever one can afford and perform a fire offering a hundred thousand times with nāgakeśara flowers mixed with ghee. Then one will succeed: one will accomplish rites by mere words, and it will not turn out otherwise."

Then Tumburu bowed to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi and said: "Blessed One, for the sake of the bodhisattvas who engage in the bodhisattva conduct through the gateway of secret mantra, we too offer our own essence in this great maṇḍala.

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā oṃ he jaṃbe oṃ he mohe svāhā Blessed One, this essence of mine is the Four-Faced One, arisen from the four directions, abiding among creatures, bringing forth prosperity and gathering it in.

Grain, purity and auspiciousness,
Glory and abundant wealth increase;
It is endowed with many wonders;
This is the supreme elixir of rejuvenation.

Blessed One, if anyone keeps this essence in mind at all times, I and my retinue will follow after them. If one wishes to accomplish it, whether standing, sitting, lying down or walking on the road, one should bind the Person just as it has been set out on oneself. Having laid out the Four-Faced One, one should bind the mudrā. One should do this from the first day of the month until the full moon. Then, having made offerings with whatever one can afford, one should make from śāli-rice flour an eight-petaled lotus together with its pericarp, and set out the essence syllables oṃ and ja in the east. One should set out bā in the west. One should place mā in the north. Having placed hā in the south, the one who seeks accomplishment should sit on a spread of kuśa grass. If in a single night one performs a fire offering of ten thousand oblations of salt, then in the morning the fruit of whatever accomplishment one wishes is granted. When one recites it, one should know the syllables in their proper order."

Then Vajrapāṇi looked at that all-inclusive assembly and gave his approval, saying "Excellent!": "Friends, that you have thus spoken and offered your own essences in this great maṇḍala, and have rejoiced, is excellent, friends, excellent! These essences are drawn forth by the supreme essence. Therefore, since they are held by the supreme essence, they will bestow accomplishments. If anyone transgresses this teaching, the supreme essence, Vajra Blazing, spreading everywhere and blazing, will shatter his head; it will not be otherwise."

Then that entire assembly spoke with one voice: "May it be just as the great bodhisattva has declared!"

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī entered the samādhi called Arising from the Radiating Light Rays of Great Vajra Wisdom. With his hand he took the vajra from Vajrapāṇi, brought forth a stainless utpala lotus arisen from wisdom, and placed the vajra upon that utpala arisen from great wisdom. The very moment he placed that great vajra there, throughout all the boundless world systems and in all the maṇḍalas of the buddhas' assemblies, the sound "Oh! This youthful Mañjuśrī is Vajra of Sacred Giving!" resounded from one to the next with a roar. At that time, from the heart of the youthful Mañjuśrī there emerged the supremely secret essence: namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā mañjuvajra jaya rati tapilasa hūṃ kṛdana kumāra svāhā

The very moment the supreme vajra essence called hūṃ emerged, the buddhas, the blessed ones dwelling in the world systems of the ten directions, while remaining where they were, stretched out their right hands, stroked the head of the youthful Mañjuśrī, and declared:

"Great vajra, excellent, excellent!
Mighty one, Vajra of Sacred Giving,
In the Vajrapāṇi Empowerment
Explain within this teaching
The extensive accomplishment of siddhis."

Having spoken thus, those buddhas of great miraculous power,
While still remaining where they were,
Drew their hands back again
From the crown of the youthful Mañjuśrī's head.

Then, with the permission of those tathāgatas, the youthful Mañjuśrī at that time set out the stages of accomplishing siddhis: "First of all, in front of a stūpa containing relics, or an image, or a volume of the sacred Dharma, one should recite any essence whatsoever at length, please the master, and make this request: 'Master, in order to attain the wisdom of omniscience, please grant me the secret mantra.' Having made this request of the master, one should join one's palms, set one's kneecaps on the ground, and, with hands that are not empty, receive whatever mantra one wishes, saying the words: 'Until I abide at the seat of awakening.' If one takes a mantra by oneself in any other way, it will not succeed.

"Next, this is the general accomplishment of siddhis for these essences spoken by the Buddha. One should stay on a mountain, in a forest, in an empty house, in a cave, in a charnel ground, by a river, or in any secluded place whatsoever. Whether eating alms food or food that has not been begged, one should keep oneself clean and wear clean clothes, bathe at the three times of day, change one's clothes three times, and recite any one essence three hundred thousand times. Then, whether one has washed the substance with realgar, collyrium, bezoar or the five products of the cow, or has placed it on a heap of cow dung, one should leave it there from the first day of the waxing moon until the full moon. Making one's own deity and one's own form inseparable, one should recite until signs appear. The signs are these: one will hear teachings never heard before, see light, hear pleasing sounds, feel as though experiencing the perfection of the tathāgatas, gain confidence in oneself, and other such signs will appear.

"Then at the full moon, having made offerings with whatever one can afford, one should place the substance in the palm of one's hand and recite all night long. At midnight or at daybreak the substance will blaze, give off smoke, or grow warm. If it blazes and one anoints one's body with it, one will become a vidyādhara, with the color of the rising sun, living for an eon, moving without obstruction, roaming all the other world systems, and going wherever one wishes. If it gives off smoke and one marks oneself with three lines, one will become invisible. If it grows warm, one will gain power over all beings. If one does not succeed, one will become capable in every task and will obtain the office of a great minister."

"For one month one should practice recollection of the Buddha, make offerings before the Blessed One with whatever one can afford while using any essence whatsoever, and, gazing at an image of the Buddha, recite all night long. Then that image will be accomplished. Likewise, if one practices the accomplishment before any bodhisattva whatsoever, it will succeed in the same way and will do whatever one wishes. Even if one has not done the preliminary practice, one will succeed without doubt.

"In this regard, in the central country the cakravartin, the Blessed One, Great Rudra, is accomplished. In the southern region the fierce Vajrapāṇi is accomplished. In the land of the Soaring Ones the king of vidyā-mantras, the Soaring One, is accomplished. In the northern region the bodhisattva Padmapāṇi is accomplished. In the eastern land Vajra of Sacred Giving is accomplished. In great pleasure groves the hosts of yakṣas and the yakṣiṇīs are accomplished. In empty houses the piśācas and piśācīs are accomplished. In charnel grounds the mātṛkās, the ḍākinīs, the rākṣasas, the rākṣasīs and the like are accomplished. On mountains the vidyādharas and the kinnaras are accomplished. In great wildernesses the gandharvas are accomplished. In rivers the nāgas are accomplished. In great halls and at the foot of banyan trees the bhūtas are accomplished. In the northern region the great yakṣas and Kubera are accomplished. In the great ocean the four sisters are accomplished.

"Furthermore, sons of noble family, in great cities the queen of vidyā-mantras Tārā and Bhṛkuṭī are accomplished. At the confluences of rivers Brahmā is accomplished. In charnel grounds frequented by jackals Rudra is accomplished. In the abode of the Great Person, Viṣṇu is accomplished. In the northern region the Sun is accomplished. On snow mountains the Moon is accomplished. In the southern region Kārttikeya is accomplished. In mountain ravines the asuras are accomplished.

"Sons of noble family, in the great oceans the great mahoragas are accomplished. In the land of Magadha the great sages are accomplished. In deserts the hungry ghosts are accomplished.

"Furthermore, sons of noble family, in mountains jewels are accomplished. In rivers the great rivers are accomplished. In the land of Kaliṅga Īśāna, king of the bhūtas, is accomplished. In pits the oppressors are accomplished. In the spaces between houses the shadow-oppressors are accomplished. At shrines and dwellings the apasmāra grahas, which bring forgetfulness, are accomplished.

"Furthermore, sons of noble family, in the land of Laṅkā and on Mount Malaya, Yama together with his retinue is accomplished. Furthermore, the great rākṣasas are accomplished in the land of Laṅkā and on Mount Malaya. Furthermore, all the lords of the garuḍas are accomplished in the great ocean. Among the snows the young gods of the summer clouds are accomplished. Furthermore, on the summit of the eight-peaked mountain the seven winds are accomplished.

"Sons of noble family, in this way, because the intentions of sentient beings differ, the tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly awakened buddhas take account of place, and according to time they designate and establish the accomplishment of secret mantra."

Then the bodhisattva mahāsattva Śāntimati said to the youthful Mañjuśrī: "Mañjuśrī, of what is this word 'time' a synonym?"

When he had said this, Mañjuśrī said to the bodhisattva mahāsattva Śāntimati: "Son of noble family, this word 'time' is a synonym for the appearance of a buddha. Why is that? It should be understood that for as long as a tathāgata, an arhat, a perfectly awakened buddha remains and lives, for so long does secret mantra succeed.

"Śāntimati, it is like this: in the great ocean there is a great precious jewel called Essence of the Array of All Beings. As long as that great king of great precious jewels remains in the great ocean, the water does not dry up, and many great precious jewels come forth. That great ocean, in turn, becomes the livelihood of the humans and nonhumans who live on dry land and in the water. Some it sustains by drink, some by dwelling places, some by everything from filling them with jewels to every other kind of thing, and it never fails to sustain them. If that great king of great precious jewels were gone, then because there would be no water and nothing at all, the great ocean would not be the livelihood of any living creatures.

"In the same way, Śāntimati, as long as the Tathāgata remains, secret mantra succeeds, for the Tathāgata bestows accomplishments taking into account the different intentions of all beings. When the Tathāgata has passed into complete nirvāṇa, that accomplishment no longer sustains them.

"Look, Śāntimati: as long as there is wood, so long does fire blaze; when there is no wood, fire does not blaze. In the same way, Śāntimati, it should be understood that as long as the Tathāgata remains, so long does secret mantra succeed. Śāntimati, this is what time is.

"Furthermore, Śāntimati, the bodhisattva who engages in the bodhisattva conduct through the gateway of secret mantra should strive always and without interruption for the appearance of buddhas. Śāntimati, in this regard, the bodhisattva who engages in the bodhisattva conduct through the gateway of secret mantra should become skilled in the preliminary practice of secret mantra.

"Son of noble family, what is the practice of secret mantra? It is this: with a mind that never grows weary, with enthusiasm, and steadfast in one's commitment, one should practice the foundations of secret mantra one by one. Whether walking, sitting or lying down, one engages with a mind that is single-pointed alone, never divided in two. Alone, without a companion, in a secluded and delightful place free of bustle, staying in one's own region, one should first perform the approach.

"Then one should do this from the first day of the month until the full moon. Next, one should take any one of these: a sword, a wheel, collyrium, bezoar, or the like. One should smear a maṇḍala with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground mixed with cow urine, and draw the maṇḍala of accomplishment. If one is accomplishing a wheel, one should draw the vajra maṇḍala of the wheel. If one is accomplishing a sword, one should draw a vajra maṇḍala. If one is accomplishing collyrium, bezoar or the like, one should draw a vajra maṇḍala of the trident. One should make offerings with whatever one can afford, from the time the setting sun turns red until sunrise. Having paid homage to all the tathāgatas, with the awakening mind as one's starting point, having performed the protection, one should take up the substance with a mind of loving-kindness. If one recites all night long, at midnight or at daybreak the substance will certainly blaze.

"If it blazes, all the vidyādharas, Citrāṅga and the others, will come. They will empower one according to one's merit, and one will become a king of vidyādharas, going wherever one wishes. One's mind will be intent on making offerings to all the tathāgatas, on roaming many other world systems, and on bringing all sentient beings to maturity.

"Bezoar and the rest should be accomplished in the same way. If it blazes, one will travel through the sky, move about in the world system of the four continents, live and roam for an eon, and go unhindered wherever one wishes; and after death and transmigration one will be born in a pure buddha field. If it gives off smoke, one will become invisible, take whatever form one desires, roam Jambudvīpa at will, and take a sixth share of wealth; and after death and transmigration one will be born among the gods of Tuṣita. If it grows warm, one will gain power over all beings, retain whatever one hears, and know all the arts; and after death and transmigration one will be born in this very Jambudvīpa, in Alakāvatī, the palace of the yakṣas, obtaining the body of a young yakṣa. Even if one does not succeed, one will certainly obtain a domain."

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī spoke again to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "Son of noble family, listen to the accomplishment of the Blessed One, the Great Cakravartin, the lord of secret mantra, who is higher and higher above all secret mantras, mundane and supramundane, and whose power and might in overcoming others are immeasurable.

"Śāntimati, first of all, one who has done the preliminary practice, who has seen the maṇḍala, obtained the samaya, received permission, and been empowered in the maṇḍala of the Great Vajra Cakravartin, should draw the maṇḍala of the Blessed One and be empowered on the crown of the head with sixteen spouted vases filled with jewels, incense and every kind of medicine, over which the mantra of the Great Cakravartin has been recited one hundred and eight times. At all times one should behold the form of the deity.

"Then one should go to a mountain peak, eat alms food, bathe at the three times of day, change one's clothes at the three times of day, and stay silent like a tree. Maintaining perfect purity and pure conduct, one should recite the Blessed One, the great king Cakravartin, a hundred thousand million times. Then one should recite the essence of Fierce Vajra four hundred thousand times.

"Then one should have a goldsmith who is keeping the poṣadha vows forge a golden wheel with eight or six spokes, with a hub of sunstone jewel, and encircled by blazing rays of light. Then, having washed it with scented water, one should generate its splendor with the mantra of the Vajra of Supreme Giving. Then one should rouse it with the essence of Fierce Vajra. Having roused it, with the mantra of the Cakravartin one should place it inside a sandalwood casket set in the center of the maṇḍala.

"Then one should make a stūpa of the Sugata a span in height as its seat. Then, from the first day of the month until the full moon, one should do this, burning aguru incense. Reciting countless times and eating food that has not been begged, one should make offerings with whatever one can afford, smear a maṇḍala with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground, and draw the maṇḍala of the Cakravartin in the center of a vajra maṇḍala. Taking in one's hand the wheel that lies there, one should recite all night long until rays of light come forth from the wheel.

"Those rays illuminate the practitioner, and his body is transformed. At midnight or at daybreak the earth will quake. The sound of divine music will arise. A rain of flowers mixed with divine scented water will fall from the sky. All the vidyādharas, mundane and supramundane, will gather and empower him, and a great celestial palace of divine jewels will appear. Together with those whom he sees and those who see him, he rises up into the sky.

"He is endowed with a splendor surpassing a thousand suns and possesses the swiftness of thought. Making offerings to all the tathāgatas and endowed with the fortune of a great cakravartin, he becomes a vidyādhara higher and higher above all the gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, great mahoragas, śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, with an entourage of many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of divine maidens. Praised by those who make sweet and pleasing sounds with divine cymbals and clapping, he mounts that wheel and travels in order to attain the unsurpassed activity of making great offerings to the tathāgatas. In the maṇḍalas of the assemblies of all the buddhas, for the sake of making offerings, in every single instant and at all times, he offers all flowers, all incense, all garlands, all fumigants, all cloth, all garments, all powders, horses, elephants, chariots, all celestial palaces, all pleasing sounds of music, all foods both hard and soft, all food and drink, all divine maidens, divine wish-fulfilling trees, divine medicinal trees, all garlands of gold, jewels and pearls, and divine multistoried pavilions. Among the hungry ghosts he emanates clouds of food, clothing and ornaments. He goes wherever he wishes. He has attained the immeasurable ground of the Victor's children, the ground of conduct through aspiration. He is possessed of great renown. Apart from the tathāgatas, the arhats, the perfectly awakened buddhas, and the bodhisattva mahāsattvas who have attained the generation of the powers, there is no one at all who equals or surpasses him in splendor, power, wisdom, array, miraculous power, magical display, form, glory or eloquence."

Śāntimati said: "Mañjuśrī, how wondrous is the way this Great Cakravartin, through his array, his accomplished miraculous power, his meditative concentration, and his inexhaustible intention, fulfills every hope! How wondrous the degree to which this Blessed One, the great cakravartin of secret mantra, is the equal of the unequaled! Mañjuśrī, those who constantly recollect the one of great miraculous power will themselves come to possess immeasurable masses of good qualities."

When he had said this, the youthful Mañjuśrī said to the bodhisattva mahāsattva Śāntimati: "Śāntimati, so it is. So it is; it is just as you have said. The Blessed One, the Great Cakravartin, possesses immeasurable masses of good qualities.

"Śāntimati, it is like this: this great earth sustains all sentient beings, yet this vast great earth does not conceive and does not discriminate. In the same way, the Blessed One, the Great Cakravartin, does not conceive and does not discriminate. Why is that? Because the great earth is free of discrimination.

"Śāntimati, it is like this: this great earth gives rise to all crops and fulfills all the hopes of all sentient beings. In the same way, Śāntimati, the Blessed One, the Great Cakravartin, through hearing, reflection, meditative concentration, recitation and accomplishment, fulfills in every way all the intentions of those who engage in the bodhisattva conduct through the gateway of secret mantra.

"Śāntimati, those who, speaking truly, have said, 'The Tathāgata is inconceivable, the nāgas are inconceivable, meditative concentration is inconceivable, Māra is inconceivable, karma is inconceivable,' are, speaking truly, speaking of this very Great Cakravartin. Why is that? Because all these inconceivables arise from him.

"Śāntimati, whatever mundane or supramundane meditative concentrations there are, the accomplishment of meditative concentration, Māra, the lord of the desire realm, the nāgas, and the buddhas, the blessed ones endowed with inconceivable qualities, are all blessed by this very Blessed One, the Great Cakravartin. He also displays various miraculous transformations. Śāntimati, by this explanation too you should understand how this Blessed One, the Great Cakravartin, is the master, the chief and the sovereign of all secret mantras, and that there is no supreme state other than this.

"Just as with the wheel, one should accomplish the sword, the trident, the raised trident, the javelin, the hook, the axe, the single-pointed lance, the plowshare, the noose, the kaṇaya dart, the iron arrow, the iron chain, the bow, the club, the spear, the pestle narrow at the waist, and staffs of various kinds. One should also accomplish various substances such as collyrium, bezoar and realgar, and their accomplishments too are likewise threefold.

"If one wishes to accomplish pills, one should recite the mantra of the Blessed One, the king Cakravartin, three hundred thousand times. Then one should mix together equal parts of realgar, collyrium, adamagupta root and andhapuṣpikā root, make pills the size of a juniper berry, and encase them in the three metals. Holding a wheel or a sword in one's hand, before a stūpa containing relics or an image, without eating, one should put a pill in one's mouth. From the time the red of the evening sky has faded, one should recite until blue or yellow-tinged rays of light come forth from the wheel or the sword. They illuminate the practitioner and he succeeds: he takes on the color of the rising sun, travels like the wind, lives for an eon, becomes invisible, and assumes whatever form he wishes; and after death and transmigration he is born in the abode of the king of the Vaśavartin gods.

Whether it is done with a painted image or without an image, when one practices, he bestows accomplishment.

If one wishes to practice with a painted image, have a beautiful brahmin or kṣatriya girl, complete in all her limbs, who has taken the poṣadha vows, spin cotton that was bought without haggling over the price. On an auspicious lunar day, half-day division, and moment, have a weaver who is complete in all his limbs weave it. Smear a maṇḍala with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground, and worship with perfumes, flowers, incense, and so on. The cloth should be free of hair, even, and smooth. The weaver's weaving implements are also to be washed with the five products of the cow and with scented water. Śāntimati, all of them should also have their bodies protected with the heart mantra of Fierce Vajra.

The painter should be one who has taken the poṣadha vows, who is skilled, endowed with faith and compassion, free of avarice, and complete in all his limbs. In a secluded place free of smoke, clean, and strewn with flowers, burning agarwood incense, have him paint with fresh colors not mixed with glue. Śāntimati, or else the practitioner should paint it himself. Raising parasols, victory banners, and flags, one should make offerings with cymbals, many delightful sounds, vast auspicious songs, and recitations of words of good fortune.

Śāntimati, the practitioner, endowed with a mind of loving-kindness, his head-cloth bound and with a rosary for counting mantras hanging from his hand, should sit behind the painter and visualize the form and color of the Blessed One, the Great Cakravartin. Visualizing also the mudrās and the syllables, one should paint the Blessed One, the Great Cakravartin. Paint him seated on a white lotus, golden in color, ablaze with blazing flames, with his hair in a topknot, wearing a lower garment of silk or cotton and an upper robe of silk, absorbed in meditative absorption, his face gently smiling, and endowed with all the major marks.

To his right paint Vajra of Sacred Giving, saffron in color, with five locks of hair, holding in his left hand a blue utpala lotus together with a vajra, adorned with all ornaments, wearing a lower garment of silk or cotton and an upper robe of silk, surrounded by blazing flames, seated on a white lotus, and smiling.

To the left of the Blessed One, the Great Cakravartin, not very far from him, intent on gazing at his face, paint the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, adorned with all ornaments, wearing a crown, the crown swaying, wearing a short garment, kneeling, his palms joined together with a vajra, receiving the command.

Before the image, as before, one should recite a million times. At the end, making offerings with whatever one can afford, holding a sword or a wheel in one's hand, one should perform a fire offering throughout the whole night with sticks of agarwood smeared with curd, honey, and ghee. Then at midnight or at sunrise, rays of light will certainly come forth from the painted image. Illuminated by them, he succeeds, and as before he goes wherever he pleases. By merely seeing that painted image one will be freed from all evil.

By the power of seeing the painting,
the deeds of the five acts of immediate retribution and the rest,
whatever evil was done before,
all of it will be purified.
That the Cakravartin of great splendor,
the protector, as soon as one wishes to recall him,
performs the activity that accomplishes
the aims of all the world, what need is there to say?
If the mantra practitioner, before the painted image,
performs a hundred thousand fire offerings of lotuses,
Śrī, who grants the supreme accomplishment as one wishes,
will be seen face to face.
The mantra practitioner, wherever he may be,
will see inexhaustible treasures,
and will travel even through the sky;
he obtains what he desires according to his wish.
Bezoar and realgar,
and likewise collyrium:
abstaining from food and having purified himself,
the wise one should recite over them a hundred thousand times.
Even without the preliminary approach practice,
the mantra practitioner will succeed, without doubt.
He becomes invisible in the three worlds,
and cannot be overcome by any person.
He roams throughout all the lands
together with their mountains and forests.
He lives for an eon with great splendor,
and is wholly free of old age and death.

Śāntimati, further, this is the practice of the Great Rudra Vajrapāṇi. Staying in one's own country, having purified oneself and made one's mind endowed with the awakening mind, one should go to a mountain peak. Eating alms food, changing one's clothes three times, bathing at the three times of day, restrained by the bodhisattva vow, respectful and wearing attire suited to that, one should recite a million times in silence.

After that, take a sword, a wheel, a club, a noose, an axe, a pestle, a hammer, or a short spear. Wash it with the five products of the cow and also with scented water, and, holding it in one's hand together with its sheath, whether standing, sitting, or walking, one should recite for one month. Even when lying down, one should be protected by the Great Cakravartin.

Then in the following month, from the first day until the full moon, one should give away all one's possessions. Then on the day of the full moon, making offerings with whatever one can afford, smear a maṇḍala with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground. On that same mountain peak, before a stūpa containing relics, set up an image of the Sugata a span in height, make offerings, and to one side draw the maṇḍala of accomplishment. In its east place the king of vidyādharas Citrāṅga. In the south place the king of vidyādharas Viśvasena. In the west place the king of vidyādharas Crest-Bearer. In the north place the king of vidyādharas Kīlikila. In the four corners place three-pronged vajras of four colors, with blazing flames. In the center draw an eight-petaled lotus with its anthers, and on its center draw whatever is being accomplished. On top of that, place whatever substance is to be accomplished upon a leaf of the aśvattha tree. Leaving a space of about a cubit from the lotus, draw a crossed vajra, sit on it, protect oneself, and recite until it blazes. Having blazed, it will move. Or else it will resound with the sound of a great drum. Or else a rain of flowers will fall. Śāntimati, or else the practitioner will be illuminated by a great light.

Or else the flower garland will move. If one then offers guest water and takes up the substance, one will succeed. One will have the color of the rising sun, be as swift as thought, roam through many other realms, make offerings, be skilled in worshiping and serving all the tathāgatas, attain the station of all the lords, and go wherever one wishes. If one constantly recollects him, no fear of nonhumans will arise. One will know one's own death. Vajrapāṇi will show himself. And after death and transmigration, one will obtain the body of a youth in the house of Vajrapāṇi in Alakāvatī.

If every day one drinks water over which this heart mantra has been recited one hundred and eight times, all illnesses will disappear. If one enchants bezoar once and applies it to the eyes, whoever one looks at will come under one's power. If one enchants a tooth stick once with the mantra and cleans one's teeth with it, one will obtain the food one desires. Whatever the color of the flower one throws into water, one will obtain clothing of that color. If one enchants one's mouth with the mantra, whoever one speaks with will come under one's power. Binding the hook mudrā and saying hūṃ, one removes poison. If one threatens with a wrathful finger, they will be cowed. For someone seized by a graha, binding the vajra mudrā, threatening with hūṃ, and merely making it blaze with raṃ, the graha flees, without doubt. For someone seized by a graha, if one visualizes the syllable hā on the palm of one's hand and strokes him, he will recover. If one enchants flowers, perfume, or incense with the mantra, whoever one gives them to will come under one's power. All fierce grahas, too, will flee by this very rite.

If a mantra practitioner with a tamed mind, eating alms food and having made protection, recites three hundred thousand times at a lake of the nāgas, the nāga will appear and grant whatever is asked. If one enchants a blue thread with the mantra and ties it around the neck of someone struck by fever, he will at once be freed from the fever. For diseased eyes, take a shoot of the karavīra tree, enchant it once with the mantra, rub it in a copper vessel, and apply it to the eyes; they will at once be cured. For all diseases of the head, enchant guggulu with the mantra, cover the whole body with it and hold it to the head; the disease of the head will disappear. If food is not digested, drinking seven handfuls of water enchanted with the mantra will cure it. For a pain in the belly, enchant a red thread together with the root of the tnasuci plant and tie it around the waist; the disease of the belly will be cured.

Śāntimati, I shall declare this king of incenses, which pacifies all illnesses and dispels all grahas.

Śāntimati said, "Mañjuśrī, on what grounds are these taught to be accomplished? Or are they not accomplished?"

Mañjuśrī said, "Śāntimati, this king of vidyās is always accomplished. Śāntimati, unless one is separated from the awakening mind, there is no need for prior practice and no need for prior approach. So that bodhisattvas may dwell happily in this very life, I have taught these practices, declared especially for bodhisattvas who are diligent in the yoga of their own deity and who practice the bodhisattva conduct by way of the gateway of secret mantra.

"Incense made of the root of the white arka tree, the root of white girikarṇikā, the root of white karavīra, uśīra, bālaka, sandalwood, black pepper, nirguṇḍī, and guggulu, mixed with ghee, destroys all grahas. It thoroughly pacifies all illnesses. It makes one glorious, long-lived, and fortunate.

"Śāntimati, long ago, in the past, within the teaching of the perfectly complete Buddha Vipaśyin, there was a brahmin called Matimān, learned in the Vedas and the meaning of the Vedas, with full knowledge of all crafts and arts, and skilled in the meaning of secret mantra. On the peak of a certain mountain he undertook diligent effort and practiced this king of vidyās, Great Fierce Vajra, again and again. As he practiced, he was harassed by vicious obstructors and vināyakas, so he went to where the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha Vipaśyin was. Having arrived, he bowed his head at the Blessed One's feet and sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the brahmin Matimān bowed with joined palms toward the Blessed One, related his situation, and said, 'Blessed One, here on the summit of a mountain called such-and-such, I undertook diligent effort and practiced the king of vidyās, Fierce Vajra, again and again. Yet, Blessed One, I have not found, obtained, or perceived its accomplishment, and whenever I practice even a little with substances, all of it is stolen by the obstructors. How, then, should I practice?'

"When he had said this, the Blessed One, the perfectly complete Buddha Vipaśyin, taught the brahmin Matimān this incense and also spoke these words of secret mantra: syādyathedaṃ ruru lele buddhamataṃgi pukkasi śabarika gṛhṇa khādaya svāhā.

"Śāntimati, if you suspect or doubt that the brahmin called Matimān, who at that time practiced this king of vidyās, was someone else, you should not regard it so. Why is that? Śāntimati, it is the one called Vajradhara Abiding Without Elaboration, who has gathered right here in the courtyard of this maṇḍala of the assembly. Skilled in the vast, boundless, endless expanse of elaboration, he has now once again found this secret mantra and incense and practiced them.

"If one wishes to practice with a painted image, then, as before, a painter who has taken the poṣadha vows should, with colors not mixed with glue, paint Vajrapāṇi on cloth, dark blue like the color of an utpala petal, or dark blue like the color of priyaṅgu, or dark blue like the color of emerald. He is turned to the side, half of him visible, adorned with all ornaments, his face slightly wrathful, wearing a short garment, seated properly and auspiciously in a jewel cave on a jewel rock in a half cross-legged posture, swaggering and haughty, with a garland of blazing flames, brandishing a blazing vajra in his right hand."

Above him, to the right of his head and not very far from it, paint the Tathāgata Glory of Fragrant Light, seated cross-legged on a white lotus, endowed with all the major marks, giving encouragement to the king of vidyās. On the left, paint the Tathāgata Utterly Crushing with the Firm Vajra, giving encouragement as before. Below Vajrapāṇi, on the right, paint an attendant wearing a soiled garment the color of chaff smoke, his tawny hair standing on end, his eyes red, his forehead furrowed with rows of wrathful frowns, holding a noose and a sword in his hands, eager to receive commands, kneeling on both knees. On the left, paint a female attendant, kneeling, dark blue in color, holding in her hands a single-edged sword with a hilt and a noose, as before.

In a secluded place free of smoke, before the image, eating the three white foods and changing one's clothes at the three times of day, one should recite three hundred thousand times. At the end, making offerings with whatever one can afford, if one performs a fire offering throughout the whole night with sticks of agarwood smeared with curd and honey, then at midnight or at sunrise rays of light will come forth from the painted image. Or else the painted image will move. Or else the flower garland will move. One will succeed as before, and will also perform all rites. If one always recites one hundred and eight times before the painted image, one will possess the very best of good fortune, be freed from all illnesses, and be rid of all fears.

Śāntimati, furthermore, this is the practice of the great heart mantra of the Terrifying Fire, of immeasurable power and might in overcoming adversaries, worshiped by gods, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kinnaras, great mahoragas, gods, and asuras, and blessed by all the tathāgatas. There is no need to choose the lunar day or the constellation, nor any need to fast. Go to a mountain peak and recite three hundred thousand times. At the end, making offerings with whatever one can afford, if one performs a fire offering three hundred thousand times with oblations of mustard seed mixed with poison, blood, and salt, then in the night a great sound of hūṃ will resound, and one will succeed as before. Once one has succeeded, one roams through many world systems, is as swift as thought, and has unequaled splendor; apart from the buddhas and bodhisattvas, there is no one who surpasses him. If one recites constantly, one becomes invincible to all bhūtas.

If one goes to the entrance of an asura cavern and, in a wrathful state, recites ten thousand times and also performs the fire offering as before, all the abodes of the asuras will be utterly overthrown. One may then enjoy them as one wishes, and whatever one desires will appear of itself. One performs all rites.

If one wishes a great treasure to emerge, one should recite this heart mantra ten thousand times without eating, and at the end strike the spot three times with a shoot of the karavīra tree; the treasure will emerge of itself. If one constantly recollects him, one will delight all people. If, before someone who is angry, one binds the mudrā and recites twenty-one times, he will become calm. All children seized by grahas will be freed merely by seeing it. For all great grahas, make an effigy, press it down with the left foot, and recite one hundred and eight times; that graha will burn, cry out, and flee. Asked a question, it will speak at once.

If one wishes to harm another, take sticks of bibhītaka wood six fingers long, pronounce the person's name over each one, and enchant them one hundred and eight times with the mantra. If at midnight one plants them on the roof or inside the doorway, great harm will befall that household. A great epidemic will also break out. By intention, one pacifies it again.

If one practices with a painted image, then on cloth paint the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi, his radiance the color of a summer cloud, without ornaments, his eyes red, wrathful, biting his lower lip with his teeth, his forehead knit with rows of wrathful frowns, standing with his left foot pressing down the ground, as though stamping with his right foot, as though threatening with the forefinger of his left hand, and with his right hand as though hurling the terrible wrathful vajra. One who has made protection, who has transformed himself into the form of Vajroṣṇīṣa, who is always in meditative equipoise, possessed of compassion, and whose mind is endowed with the awakening mind, should make extensive offerings in a secluded place free of smoke and have a painter who has taken the poṣadha vows paint it. One should have it painted by someone who has been empowered in the great vajra maṇḍala, has obtained the samaya, and is full of enthusiasm, and by no one else. Śāntimati, if anyone who does not know the samaya and has not seen the maṇḍala sees the painted image, he will at once be struck by two kinds of illness. What are the two? They are a violent fever and a great wasting disease.

Śāntimati said, "Mañjuśrī, oh, how wonderful is this supreme heart mantra, endowed with great meaning and endowed with great power, by which such distinctive qualities are revealed! Mañjuśrī, this supreme heart mantra, what is it? What is its name?"

Mañjuśrī said, "Śāntimati, there is no end to the qualities and power of the firm heart of all the tathāgatas. Śāntimati, this Vajrapāṇi is called the Vajra Arisen from the Terrifying Fire of All the Tathāgatas. He tames all who are untamed. He fulfills the aspirations of all the tathāgatas and all the bodhisattvas. He has made the aspiration to protect the supreme teaching everywhere. He fulfills the wishes of all beings, whatever they may be. He enters the gateway of proclaiming the aspirations of all the tathāgatas throughout the boundless oceans of world systems. He runs ahead in all things.

"Before the image, making offerings with whatever one can afford, if one performs a fire offering of one hundred thousand pills of guggulu, each the size of a juniper berry, one will succeed and will do whatever one wishes. Go to a mountain peak or to the shore of the ocean, paint the image, and, eating alms food or food that was not begged for, enter the water up to the throat and recite three hundred thousand times."

At the end, making offerings with whatever one can afford and holding a sword or a wheel in one's hand, if one performs a fire offering thirty thousand times with poison, blood, and white mustard seed mixed together, one will succeed. A sound will come forth from the ocean, and it will dry up. Mountains, too, will shake or crumble. One will become able to perform all rites. Even a hailstorm or a lightning strike will vanish merely by threatening it once.

For all bondage, all contagious diseases, and all harms that arise, paint the image at a crossroads, at a place with a single tree, on the bank of a river, at a place with a single landmark, or at a three-way junction in a town. Make extensive offerings, and fill with white mustard seed a vase that has been worshiped with incense and flowers. Recite the mantra of the Cakravartin, place the heart mantra at one's heart, and gladden the painted image directly by uttering vast, auspicious, and pleasing words; then one should perform the empowerment. Those mustard seeds dispel all harms, all contagious diseases, and all forms of death. If all the beings dwelling within a league see that painted image, they too will be pacified at once.

The Noble Vajrapāṇi Empowerment, the seventh fascicle.

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī spoke these words to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "Śāntimati, listen to the stages of the general practice of these kings of vidyās, set out in order.

"Śāntimati, a practitioner who wishes to practice any king of vidyās whatever should take up the vidyā-mantra as follows. On an auspicious lunar day, half-day division, and moment, having fasted, lighting butter lamps and making offerings with whatever one can afford, one should recite the king of vidyās fearlessly throughout the whole night before a stūpa containing relics or an image. In the morning, having bathed, anointed oneself with fragrant scents, put on clean clothes, and eaten the three white foods, with clear faculties, remaining in an ordinary frame of mind, with a mind endowed with the awakening mind, one should join one's palms, kneel, and make a request to the compassionate master, the unsurpassed object of offering, saying: 'Master, in order to attain unsurpassed wisdom, please bestow the king of vidyās upon me. I shall practice it until I abide at the seat of awakening.' And the master, compassionate toward all and free of avarice, should give it to that disciple.

"Having thus received the vidyā-mantra and the samaya, in places such as those described, one should change into attire suited to the practice. On a mountain peak, on the bank of a river, in a forest, at the edge of a spring, or in any pleasant and secluded place, eating the three white foods or drinking milk, if one recites a million times, the preliminary practice will have been done.

"Then take a sword, a vajra, a short spear, a club, a trident, or a wheel made of the three metals. Smear it with the five products of the cow, place it on a heap of cow dung, and protect it with the heart mantra of Great Fierce Vajra. Then one should recite from the first day of the month until the full moon. Then take whichever implement it is and wash it with scented water. Smear a maṇḍala with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground, draw the maṇḍala as before, make offerings with whatever one can afford, place the implement on a leaf of the aśvattha tree, and recite throughout the whole night. Then at midnight or at sunrise it will certainly blaze, and all the vidyādharas will gather and empower one in accordance with one's merit. One becomes a vidyādhara, assumes whatever form one wishes, roams through many world systems, and makes offerings to all the tathāgatas. If one does not succeed, then as long as one's discipline has not lapsed, by practicing in the same way three times one will certainly succeed, even if one has committed the five acts of immediate retribution.

"If one recites constantly, one will become long-lived. If one recites over medicine and drinks it, one will be free of illness. If one enchants ghee with the mantra and drinks it just before eating one's meal, one's body will become firm for a full year. If one closes one's eyes before an image of the Blessed One and recites one thousand and eight times, the image of the Tathāgata's body will declare what is virtuous and what is nonvirtuous. Likewise, if one covers one's ears and recites, one will hear everything. If, on going to bed, one lies down after enchanting one's eyes one hundred and eight times, one will see light and see everything. As it is by day, so it is by night. If one enchants a tooth stick once and cleans one's teeth with it, one will obtain food when one begs. If one enchants collyrium and bezoar once with the mantra and applies them to the eyes, all people will be delighted. If one recites over perfume and anoints oneself with it, whoever smells its scent will come under one's power. If one sees an enemy, binding the mudrā and reciting once, one will be parted from the enemy. If one enchants perfume, flowers, and so on once with the mantra, whoever one gives them to will be brought under one's power.

"If one wishes to enter battle, one should perform the binding on a man; if he then enters the battle, he will win the battle. If one wishes to paralyze an army, kindle a fire of vikaṅkata wood and perform a fire offering ten thousand times with fuel sticks of grutsal wood; the army will be paralyzed. For all harms, if one performs a fire offering thirty thousand times with oblations of the three sweet substances, they will be pacified. With ash one cuts off hailstorms. With a stick or with pigment one turns back lightning strikes. With a blue thread one pacifies the influence of bhūta grahas. If one makes an effigy and strikes it with ash or with a shoot of the karavīra tree, the spirit will release its victim. One should do the same for great grahas.

"If a child is seized by a graha, make an effigy from ground śāli rice, mix it with mustard seed and salt, and perform a fire offering in a fire of husks; then at all times, merely at the name, all grahas will flee. For someone struck by fever, enchant one's left hand with the mantra and place it on his head; this dispels the fever. If one wishes to dispel all illnesses, gather all the medicines, kindle a fire of bibhītaka wood, and perform a fire offering with oblations twenty-one times; from then on, one will dispel them all."

If one wishes to practice with a painted image, then on uncut cloth free of hair, as before, have a painter who has taken the poṣadha vows paint the king of vidyās with colors not mixed with glue. He is adorned with all ornaments, wears a swaying crown, sits on a white lotus, and bears the attire of a great king. He is ablaze all around with garlands of blazing flames. He holds a vajra in his left hand, and his right hand makes the gesture of supreme giving. He is seated cross-legged, his face gently smiling, gazing at the practitioner. Below him, paint the practitioner holding a censer in his hand, intent on gazing at his face, in attire suited to the practice, kneeling. If one recites a million times before the image, one will succeed as before. This is the general practice of the kings of vidyās.

Śāntimati, furthermore, this is the general practice of the daughters of Vajrapāṇi, who have gained mastery over the great aspiration and who abide in equality with all the tathāgatas. As before, having received any secret mantra whatever by the rite of the Great Cakravartin, go from among the places described to the bank of a river that flows into the ocean and recite a million times; the preliminary practice will then have been done. Then, from the first day of the month, take whatever substance is to be accomplished and recite until the full moon. Then, making offerings with whatever one can afford, smear a maṇḍala with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground and make protection. If one performs a fire offering throughout the whole night with fuel sticks of guggulu smeared with curd, honey, and ghee, then at midnight or at sunrise one will certainly succeed, and the substance will blaze. If one takes it, one obtains the body of a vajra youth in the vajra family. One obtains the great dhāraṇī. If one wishes, one may go. Even if one does not succeed, one will become able to perform all rites. And after death and transmigration, one will be born in Alakāvatī or in a house of great wealth.

If one recites constantly, all people will be delighted. If one recites while gazing at Vajrapāṇi, all evil will be purified. If one strokes the whole body and then sleeps, one will see the buddhas, the blessed ones, in a dream, and will obtain the dhāraṇī right there. When the moon is seized by the wolf, take ghee from a yellow cow whose calf has the same color of coat as she does, put it in a copper vessel, make a maṇḍala of sandalwood, and place the vessel on it. Without looking at the moon, recite until the three kinds of accomplishment appear, and if one then drinks it, one will retain whatever one hears. If it blazes, one will travel through the sky. If it smokes, one will become invisible. If it becomes warm, all beings will come under one's power.

If one wishes to practice with a painted image, then according to the former rite have a painter who has generated the mind set on awakening paint any one of the youths on cloth free of hair, with colors not mixed with glue. He is adorned with all ornaments, his head adorned with three locks of hair, seated on a white lotus in the midst of a sword blazing with light, holding a vajra in his left hand, smiling, encouraging the practitioner with his right hand, the color of gold or dark blue like the color of priyaṅgu. If, before the image, one makes great offerings and undertakes the practice, one will succeed as before.

Śāntimati, furthermore, this is the practice of Nalakūbara, son of the lord of the yakṣas, who is dear to Vajrapāṇi. There is no need to choose the lunar day or the constellation, and no need to fast. Holding a club in one's hand, one should enter a great wilderness. Without straying from the path and without speaking, wearing an elephant hide on one's body, changing into attire suited to the practice, and having made protection with the heart mantra of Fierce Vajrapāṇi or with the Great Cakravartin, eating alms food or not eating at all, if one recites three hundred thousand times, Nalakūbara himself will come and offer a boon, and he will grant whatever boon one wishes. If one enters a lake of the nāgas and recites ten thousand times, a nāga will appear and grant whatever one wishes. If one recites a hundred thousand times at the entrance of an asura cavern, an asura maiden herself will appear and lead one inside, and she will grant whatever one wishes, such as the rejuvenating elixir. If one does not wish to go, one may stay right there together with them, will obtain the bliss of all the lords, and will live for an eon. With that very body one will see the perfectly complete Buddha Maitreya and also hear the Dharma. And after death and transmigration, one will be born in a pure buddha field. If one recites constantly, then at the mere sight of one, all rākṣasas, pretas, piśāca grahas, and the like will flee farther than far.

Śāntimati, in short, however one may practice, even if one practices by the detailed rites found elsewhere, one will certainly succeed.

Next is the practice of Trailokyavijaya. At a place with a single liṅga or in a charnel ground, smear one's body with ash, hold a trident or a vajra in one's hand, and eat alms food. Wandering through the country, wandering without a fixed dwelling, being pure, patient, delighting in inner purity, free of avarice, and heedless of body and life, wearing a lower garment of lion skin, one should recite this heart mantra three hundred thousand times. Then the mantra practitioner should go, in order to practice, to a place with a single liṅga, to a charnel ground, or else to a place with a single tree.

On the full moon, as before,
in a pure spot within a house,
one should recite through one whole night.
At midnight or at sunrise,
through that substance one is wholly transformed into a youth,
with light like a garland of blazing flames.
Once accomplished, one ranges through the sky,
great in yoga, one lives for an eon,
one roams throughout all the worlds,
and one makes great offerings.

Śāntimati, in the three worlds there is no one equal to one who has accomplished the secret mantra. Even without having practiced, the rites will succeed. If one binds the mudrā and looks at any graha, the graha will at once let go of its victim. If one looks at someone struck by fever, he will recover from the fever.

For every purpose, whether one performs it by one's own detailed rite or by another's detailed rite, by whichever one, it will certainly succeed.

Furthermore, Śāntimati, this is the practice of the wrathful one Candratilaka, beloved of Vajrapāṇi. On a mountain peak, or in the great ocean, one enters the water until it reaches the throat. One eats food that has not been begged, keeps silent like a tree, and wears a lower garment of tiger skin. Without turning aside from the path, holding in one's hand a wheel, a sword, a trident, or a raised three-pointed spear, one should recite a hundred thousand times for each of the mantra's syllables. At the end, one makes offerings with whatever one can afford. On the full moon, one binds on a head-wrap and performs the protection. If one then recites throughout the night, at midnight or at sunrise one will certainly succeed, and one will travel through the sky. One's splendor will be without equal, and from then on all accomplishments will long abound. Yakṣas, rākṣasas, piśācas and the like will not overpower one.

If someone has been bound by a ḍākinī's seal, draw the image of the seal inside two clay bowls joined mouth to mouth, empower it with the mantra, and show it to the sick person; the seal will be broken.

If someone is seized by the graha of a brahmarākṣasa, draw its image or its name with charcoal from a charnel ground and beat it with a switch of śalātaka wood. Or, if one threatens it with a cord of cow-tail hair together with the syllable hūṃ, at once it will weep with a loud cry and flee. It will tell whatever it is asked. Whatever rite one applies, it will succeed in this way; even if one performs the set of another's detailed rites, it will succeed without doubt. Whether one recites it once, or does not recite it at all, it succeeds by recollection alone.

Furthermore, Śāntimati, this is the practice of Mahābala, who arose from the power of all the tathāgatas, whose strength and power to overcome adversaries are immeasurable, and who is born in the family of Vajrapāṇi. There is no need to choose the date or the constellation. There is no need to fast; one may be impure or not impure, bathed or not bathed. Wearing a garland of human skulls, holding a khaṭvāṅga staff, dwelling in a charnel ground, and with one's body smeared with ashes, in any place whatever one should recite this heart mantra three hundred thousand times. At the end, in that very place, one should smear a maṇḍala with cow dung and perform the protection. Taking any substance whatever, if one recites throughout the night, at midnight or at sunrise it will blaze. Holding it, one becomes a vidyādhara who moves without obstruction, and none of the vidyādharas can overpower his strength. Apart from bodhisattvas dwelling on the ten grounds and accomplished vidyādharas who have attained the conduct, there is no one equal to him. If one constantly recollects it, one becomes powerful. In the face of every graha, every harm and every fear, by recollection alone one pacifies them all.

One who wishes to enter an asura cave should hold a club of śamī wood and recite three hundred thousand times at the entrance of the asura cave. At the end, one makes offerings with whatever one can afford, mixes poison, blood and mustard seeds, and performs a fire offering ten thousand times; then all the abodes of the asuras will blaze like the fire at the end of an eon. If one empowers that club seven times with the mantra and strikes the door with it, all the asura maidens will come forth, and they will lead the practitioner into their own homes. They will give him everything, elixirs of rejuvenation and the rest. He may do whatever he wishes. It succeeds by another's detailed rite as well. This king of wrathful ones is accomplished by its very nature; by recitation alone it accomplishes everything.

Furthermore, Śāntimati, this is the general practice for all the servants (ceṭas). Eating at night, wearing dirty clothes, with one's pride broken, despised, assuming the guise of a madman, and skilled in laughter, dance and cymbals, in an empty house or where there is a single tree, whether walking, standing, sitting, lying down, singing or dancing, one should recite these heart mantras three hundred thousand times. At the end, one should smear a maṇḍala with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground, strew flowers, and make offerings with whatever one can afford. Holding a sword of hammered iron shaped like a crow's beak, and a noose of human hair twelve cubits, three cubits, or one cubit long, if one recites throughout the night, at midnight or at sunrise it will certainly blaze. Holding it, one becomes a vidyādhara. One will carry out the command of all the tathāgatas. One will take whatever form one wishes, and one could bind even the three worlds and lead them here, to say nothing of ordinary beings. If one constantly recollects it, the servant will always follow behind one and will do as one commands. After death and transmigration, one will be born among the gods, and one will surely be bent upon awakening.

Furthermore, Śāntimati, this is the general practice for the female servants (ceṭīs) born in the family of Vajrapāṇi. Alone, without a companion, in an empty house, a dwelling, or a solitary cave, undisturbed, one should recite the mantra of any one of the female servants three hundred thousand times. At the end, one makes offerings with whatever one can afford. Holding in one's hand a knife whose handle is like a crow's beak, and a noose, if one recites throughout the night, at midnight or at sunrise one will succeed, and one becomes a vidyādhara who ranges through the great trichiliocosm world system and takes whatever form one wishes.

Make an image out of salt and worship it with perfume, flowers and incense. Place it in a secluded spot, undisturbed, and, alone, throughout the night, striking it on the breast with one's hand while reciting, one will certainly succeed in the same way, and she will grant whatever excellent thing one desires. If one empowers a tooth stick of thorny apāmārga once with the mantra and chews it, one will obtain food without begging. In short, by whatever rite one practices, by that very rite it will succeed. These are accomplished by their very nature; by mere wishing they bring complete fulfillment.

Furthermore, Śāntimati, this is the general practice for all the messengers. Eating alms food and entering a river that flows into the ocean, one should recite three hundred thousand times.

At the end, one makes offerings with whatever one can afford, and likewise, if one performs a fire offering ten thousand times with karavīra flowers dipped in ghee, one will succeed and will do whatever one wishes.

Another practice is this. Make an image from fresh butter and worship it with perfume and flowers. On the full moon, smear a maṇḍala with sandalwood, and, without looking at the moon, recite until it blazes. If one then takes it, one becomes a vidyādhara. If smoke rises from it and one drinks it, one becomes invisible, ranges through the great trichiliocosm world system, and takes whatever form one wishes. If it grows warm and one drinks it, one retains what one hears and obtains the dhāraṇī that retains everything heard. One is freed from all diseases. One becomes supremely fortunate. One takes whatever form one wishes. After death and transmigration, one is born among the gods or in Alakāvatī. Śāntimati, these are accomplished by their very nature; they bring wishes to perfection, and they succeed simply by being recited.

Furthermore, Śāntimati, this is the general practice for the female messengers who work for the benefit of all beings. Make an image from the earth of an anthill or from earth taken from both banks of a river. In a secluded, undisturbed garden, worship it with whatever one can afford and recite the vidyā three hundred thousand times. At the end, on the full moon, make an extensive torma offering, smear collyrium, gorocanā or realgar mixed with perfumed water, place it in the image's hand, and recite until it blazes. If it blazes, one becomes a vidyādhara. If smoke rises, one becomes invisible. If it grows warm, one gains control. One will range through a thousand world systems, with a color like the rising sun; one will move like the wind, work for the benefit of beings, and uphold the true Dharma. Even without having accomplished it, it serves for the rites. If one empowers gorocanā once with the mantra and smears it on one's face, one becomes fortunate. If one empowers collyrium with the mantra and applies it to one's eyes, whoever one looks at comes under one's control. If one empowers one's mouth with the mantra, whoever one speaks with comes under one's control. If one empowers flowers, incense and the like with the mantra, whoever one gives them to comes under one's control. If one sits in a solitary spot on a road, pronounces the name of the one one wishes to bring under control, places one's hand at one's heart and recites the vidyā one hundred and eight times, that person comes under one's control within seven days. If one empowers water with the mantra and washes one's face, one becomes fortunate. If one performs a fire offering of salt oblations one hundred thousand times, one becomes supremely fortunate. If one wants small livestock, one empowers karavīra flowers fumigated with agaru, each one once with the mantra, and casts them for seven days into water that flows into the ocean; one will certainly obtain them. If one empowers goat's milk with the mantra and drinks it, one will likewise obtain them. If one wants a son, one performs a fire offering for seven days with kindling sticks of pine dipped in the ghee of a yellow cow, and one will have a son. Śāntimati, in this way, through this set one performs a thousand rites; it bestows accomplishments and increases prosperity as well. These great vajra female messengers succeed simply by being recited.

Furthermore, Śāntimati, listen to the practice of the servants who arise from the vajra family. Sleeping wherever one happens to be, in a monastery, a hermitage, a village or a town; with one's pride broken no matter who mistreats one; taking on the appearance of a servant; wearing dirty clothes; staying amid a rattling noise; not bathing, and of a grimy color, one should recite any one of the heart mantras three hundred thousand times at the crossroads of a great city or in a woman's house. At the end, in that very place, one smears a maṇḍala with cow dung and makes offerings with whatever one can afford. Taking sandals made of the three metals, or wooden sandals made from wood split by lightning, if one recites throughout the night, at midnight or at sunrise they will certainly blaze and the practice will succeed. Wearing the wooden sandals, one goes wherever one wishes. One takes whatever form one wishes and travels like the wind. Even if it is not accomplished, it serves for the rites, and if one recites constantly, the servant will always follow behind one. If one ties a black thread on someone struck by fever, he will recover from the fever. For every disease and every harm, if one sprinkles white mustard seed or perfumed water, the person will recover.
