If one performs a fire offering thirty thousand times in front of Vajrapāṇi with black sesame mixed with curd, honey and ghee, even one who commits evil will become able to perform every rite. Why is that? Because these are accomplished by their very nature. Śāntimati, there is nothing at all that the servants will not do. They guard the sacred commitments, they work for the teaching, and when they are practiced they open the doors to accomplishments of many kinds. Whether one performs another's detailed rite or whatever one can manage, it will certainly succeed.

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī also spoke these words to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "Śāntimati, there are female servants, together with their secrets, who are the heart essence beloved of Vajrapāṇi, accomplished by their very nature, who fulfill every wish and perform various rites, and by the mere remembrance of whom no fears arise. This is their practice. There is no need to choose the date or the constellation. There is no need to fast. Eating at night, eating food that has not been begged, wearing a guise suited to this, and holding in one's hand a single-edged sword with its hilt, one enters water that flows into the ocean up to the waist and should recite three hundred thousand times. At the end, in that very place, one makes offerings with whatever one can afford, and if throughout the night one performs a fire offering three hundred thousand times with white mustard seed mixed with saffron, at midnight or at sunrise one will certainly succeed. Holding it, one becomes swift as thought; in many boundless world systems one makes great offerings in the maṇḍalas of the assemblies of all the buddhas; one travels without obstruction; one benefits all sentient beings; and one fulfills every wish.

"There is another practice as well. Going to a mountain peak, one should likewise recite three hundred thousand times. At the end, one makes offerings with whatever one can afford, and if one performs a fire offering of milk oblations three hundred thousand times, even one who commits evil will certainly succeed."

Even without a painted image or a statue, it will succeed for one who has generated the mind set on awakening, who has seen the great vajra maṇḍala and been authorized by the guru, who is free of avarice, endowed with faith, devoted to the Three Jewels, full of enthusiasm, steadfast in the pledged deity, always in meditative equipoise, skilled in the meaning of the reality of the secret mantra, and respectful toward the guru. Otherwise, even if the great Śakra (Indra) himself were to practice it, the secret mantra would not grant accomplishment. For one endowed with the qualities just described, it will certainly succeed.

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī spoke these words to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "Śāntimati, there is another quality of these male servants, female servants, male attendants, female attendants, male messengers, female messengers and the rest, described as follows: the power to become invisible. Grasp it well and thoroughly, and I shall explain it to you."

When he had said this, the bodhisattva Śāntimati spoke these words to the youthful Mañjuśrī: "Mañjuśrī, this is the time; this is the occasion. Mañjuśrī, if you know that the time for it has come, please speak."

"Śāntimati, for this, one who has done the preliminary practice according to the previous rite and keeps the observance of the vidyā takes any one of spikenard, orpiment or realgar, puts it into a copper amulet box, and seals it so that its scent does not escape. During the waning moon one fasts for three days, and then, at a shrine of the Great God (Śiva), upon the right flank of the Great God's body, one recites throughout the night until smoke rises from it. If one smears one's body with it, one becomes invisible.

"Take ashes from a house where a child has been born and hold them in one's cupped hands. Likewise, during the waning moon, recite upon the right flank of the Great God's body until smoke rises. If one draws the three lines on one's forehead with it, one becomes invisible.

"On the eighth or the fourteenth day of the waning moon, when the constellation Puṣya and the planet Moon coincide, put bibhītaka wood into the fire in which a dead man is being cremated in a charnel ground; let it burn, and quench it with one's own blood. Taking that charcoal, in an empty house, secluded and undisturbed, make a forehead mark with it mixed with cow bile; if one recites throughout the night, one becomes invisible.

"Take collyrium and recite three hundred thousand times according to the previous rite. At the end, make offerings with whatever one can afford, and perform a fire offering one hundred thousand times with the ghee of a yellow cow. Then, on the full moon, recite until it is accomplished; if one anoints one's eyes with it, no one at all will see one's body.

"Again, Śāntimati, take the root of the plant ātmaguptā according to the previous rite and recite three hundred thousand times. During the waning moon, recite throughout the night at a shrine; if one puts it in one's mouth, one becomes invisible.

"With the root of white sweet flag and the root of rgya spos, recite as before until one becomes invisible.

"When the moon is in Puṣya, take a blue utpala and grind it with goat's milk. When the moon is seized by the wolf, put it into a copper vessel or between two aśvattha leaves joined face to face, and recite until the moon is released. If one drinks it, one becomes invisible.

"Furthermore, Śāntimati, grind saffron, turuṣka and lotus stamens with the ghee of a yellow cow whose calf has the same color as she. As before, when the moon is seized by the wolf, recite until the moon is released; if one drinks it, one becomes invisible.

"Śāntimati, grind flowers of the asana tree and flowers of the deva tree with the blood of a black deer. When the moon is seized by the wolf, recite until the moon is released; if one drinks it and also smears it on one's body, one becomes invisible.

"Furthermore, Śāntimati, take black agaru together with the eyes of a black snake and, as before, recite upon the right flank of the Great God's body until smoke rises. If one makes a forehead mark with it, one becomes invisible.

"Furthermore, Śāntimati, take what has been seized by the two-mouthed nāga king and grind it together with black sesame. On the eighth or the fourteenth day of the waning moon, fast for one day and night, and with that sesame oil make lampblack. If one empowers it with the mantra and anoints one's eyes, one becomes invisible.

"Furthermore, Śāntimati, extract the kernel from the stones of the harītakī fruit,
grind it together with honey,
and place it between aśvattha leaves.
The wise one, at a confluence of waters,
within a pure hollow,
if he recites throughout a single night,
will succeed at midnight;
of this there is no doubt.
If, with a steady mind, he makes a forehead mark,
his body then becomes invisible.

"Collyrium and spikenard,
bought without haggling over the price,
placed in copper vessels joined face to face:
if one recites the mantra according to the rite,
it will succeed at midnight;
of this there is no doubt.

If one smears it on one's body,
one will travel through the sky.
Having seized a sixth part of the world,
it then becomes an act of merit.

I shall explain the accomplishment of pills;
Śāntimati, listen to it.
Vermilion, and likewise spikenard,
the root of ātmaguptā,
and the tail feathers of the kurya bird:
the wise one mixes them together,
makes pills the size of juniper berries,
takes the pills and presses them firm.
The mantrin, abiding in the way of mantra,
should wrap them in the three metals.

In a charnel ground, before a stūpa containing relics or before a statue, one makes offerings with whatever one can afford and recites throughout the night.

At midnight, or else
at the time of sunrise,
that substance is wholly transformed
and blazes with garlands of flame.
If the mantrin puts it in his mouth,
he will travel through the sky,
with a color like the rising sun,
taking whatever form he wishes, fearless,
unassailable by any bhūta;
he will live for an eon and have great splendor,
he will work for the teaching and be glorious,
and he will be wholly free of aging and death.

Śāntimati, there is another pill as well.
Taking the heartwood of bakula,
the wise one takes cassia flowers,
collyrium and spikenard,
in equal parts,
wraps them in the three metals,
and, for one night, in a charnel ground,
at a solitary tree, or in caves,
should practice when the summer retreat begins.

Śāntimati, for this, the bodhisattva who practices the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra smears a maṇḍala with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground and makes offerings with whatever he can afford. He places a vajra in the center, and on it, upon a leaf of the aśvattha tree, he places the pill. He protects himself and the substance with the heart mantra of Great Fierce Vajra or with Vajroṣṇīṣa. He performs a fire offering a thousand times without interruption with ghee from a reddish-yellow cow mixed with honey, and recites throughout the night. Then, at midnight or at sunrise, the substance is transformed. If he puts it in his mouth, he will have a color like the rising sun, live for an eon, be wholly free of aging and death, range through many other world systems, and work for the benefit of all sentient beings. If he takes it out, he becomes invisible and swift as thought, and after death and transmigration he will be born in the world system Sukhāvatī.

There is another pill as well.
Roots of balā and atibalā,
likewise gold and silver,
collyrium and sweet flag,
and likewise azurite flakes:
these are taken in equal parts,
made into a dry powder,
kneaded with rainwater,
and made into three pills.
The mantrin wraps them in copper foil
and sets them out as before.
When the moon is seized by the wolf,
he fasts for a single day,
then puts the accomplished pill in his mouth
and becomes permanently invisible.

There is another pill as well. Grind flowers of the tilaka tree, the heart of a black cat and the dried heart of a bat; the pill kneaded with rainwater is likewise made as before, and one becomes invisible.

There is another pill as well. Seeds of the panasa tree, seeds of jīvanta, flowers of the agasti tree, coral, brass and flowers of the kāñcana tree, in equal parts, are likewise made as before, and one becomes invisible. Crystal, lhang tsher, seeds of the varuṇa tree and seeds of drisanta, in equal parts, are likewise made as before, and one becomes invisible.

There is another pill as well. The center of a conch shell, agnijāla and realgar, in equal parts, are likewise made as before, and one becomes invisible.

There is another pill as well. Poison, aconite, lāṅgalī and the root of turuṣka, in equal parts, are likewise made as before, and it will succeed.

There is another pill as well. The heartwood of the madhuka tree, the heartwood of the yellow sāl tree and lodestone, in equal parts, are likewise made as before, and one becomes invisible.

Śāntimati, these were taught and blessed by the tathāgatas of the past for the benefit, welfare and happiness of all sentient beings. Śāntimati, when any bodhisattva great being who practices the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra constantly and earnestly strives to accomplish these pills, he may expect two things in this life. What are the two? They are these: all people will be delighted with him, and he will be blessed by all the tathāgatas so that it is his nature never to decline. There are two more: in this life he will surely be bent upon accomplishment, and after death and transmigration he will obtain the body of a youth in the vajra family. And two more, namely: he will be freed from all diseases, and he will be rid of evil.

Furthermore, Śāntimati, the bodhisattva who practices the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra should apply himself to the domain of the body, speech and mind of the tathāgatas, be free of wavering and doubt, hold the right view, be endowed with loving-kindness, be compassionate, and keep a mind endowed with the awakening mind.

Then the bodhisattva great being Śāntimati spoke these words to the youthful Mañjuśrī: "Mañjuśrī, how is the bodhisattva who practices the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra endowed with moral discipline? How does he abide in the observances? How does he practice the conduct? Mañjuśrī, abiding in what will the bodhisattva who practices the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra come to have a nature that never declines, attain accomplishment in the secret mantras, and never fall away from the Great Vehicle?"

When he had said this, the youthful Mañjuśrī spoke these words to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "Excellent, Śāntimati, excellent, excellent, and again excellent! Śāntimati, therefore listen well and keep it in mind, and I shall explain it to you."

"Śāntimati, when one who practices the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra makes himself into the form of his own deity, gives rise to that pride with a mind free of doubt, and, whether walking, standing, sitting or lying down, always and at all times remains unmoving even as he moves, then, Śāntimati, the bodhisattva great being who practices the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra is endowed with moral discipline. When he acts in harmony with the manner of what the omen has indicated, adopts its forms and strives in yoga, then he abides in the observance. When, within that observance, he performs the activities of recitation, fire offering and the rest, then he practices the conduct. When he strives in the yoga of being inseparable from that deity's form, fully takes up the bases of training as well, and practices the Great Vehicle, then he attains accomplishment in the secret mantras.

"Furthermore, Śāntimati, the bodhisattva great being who practices the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra should always abide in signlessness. Śāntimati, if one asks how one who practices the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra abides in signlessness: Śāntimati, if he cultivates the awakening mind, he abides in signlessness. Śāntimati, that is where signs do not arise, where they are in every way nonexistent and are not apprehended. Śāntimati, because the mind is not apprehended, the essential nature of the mind's prior limit and later limit cannot be apprehended. Therefore the bodhisattva who practices the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra should strive always and without interruption to purify the awakening mind completely.

"When one abides in the observances,
the moral discipline, the conduct and the bases of training
laid down by the perfect Buddha,
who wishes to benefit sentient beings,
then the mantrin will in this way
accomplish the mantra.
Wisdom free of conceptual thought
is what the victors of the past fully awakened to.
In that which is free of conceptual thought,
secret mantra is taught to succeed.
Its result, wholly pure,
becomes luminous by its very nature.
Whoever dwells in conceptual thought
will not gain accomplishment.
Therefore, abandoning conceptual thought,
one should contemplate the form of the mantra."

Then, for the benefit, welfare and happiness of the bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct through the door of secret mantra, the youthful Mañjuśrī entered the samādhi called Treasury of the Utterly Pure Dance of a Child's Playful Conduct. As soon as the youthful Mañjuśrī had entered that samādhi, the entire assembly spoke with one voice:

"Homage, supreme vajra who grants the highest,
gentle-voiced Mañjughoṣa of great strength,
great hero, widely renowned,
who always tames the untamed!
The conduct of mantra and secret mantra,
child's play in every form,
samādhis and dhāraṇīs,
all meditations and all who meditate:
out of love for us,
please explain this maṇḍala,
the Vajrapāṇi Empowerment,
the source of great knowledge of secret mantra,
foremost among great maṇḍalas,
the basis for teaching every conduct."

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī entered the samādhi called Unborn from the Very Beginning and spoke the syllables of his own heart mantra, the great vajra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā varad vajra maṃ

As soon as he had spoken this great heart mantra of the Vajra of Sacred Giving, the entire assembly was amazed and said with one voice, "Oh! This Vajra of Sacred Giving has arisen from his past conduct."

Then Mañjuśrī spoke the vajra syllables of his own vow:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā he he kumāraka vajra vajra smara smara pratijñā svāhā

Mañjuśrī also spoke the secret mantra that is the basis for the perfect accomplishment of his own speech:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vākye da tāre kumāra rūpa dhara svāhā

Mañjuśrī also spoke the secret mantra that is the door to the perfect accomplishment of his own speech:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vākye da vajra he svāhā

The youthful Mañjuśrī also spoke the secret mantra that accomplishes the aims of his own speech:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vākye da vajra kovida svāhā

The youthful Mañjuśrī also spoke the great king of wrathful ones, Vajra Free of Extremes:

namaḥ sarva tathagatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvatha taṃ vajra hī svaha

The youthful Mañjuśrī also spoke the heart mantras of his own host of female messengers:

namaḥ sarva tathagatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajrob kiṣini svāhā

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā vajrotakrakeśiṇi svāhā

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā sumati vajrīṇi svāhā

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā citrākṣi vajriṇī svāhā

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā ākarṣaṇi vajrotagi svāhā

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī spoke his own maṇḍala of the supreme vajra:

"The great vajra maṇḍala
is square and even on every side,
marked with the emblem of the vajra.
What is made at its center
is a triangle;
it should be laid out as a triangle.
Draw the maṇḍala that arises from fire,
its color red throughout,
marked with a blazing vajra.
Having laid out its three portions,
draw the form of an utpala,
and on it draw a vajra,
blazing and adorned with dots.
What is made all around at the outer edge
is a garland of utpala forms.
At its center the mantrin
should place one in the form of a young child,
with five locks of hair on his head
and a complexion the color of saffron.

"In his left hand he holds an utpala together with a vajra, and with his right he grants the highest. Or else one places the form of his hand emblem, or else his syllable.

To draw his emblem:
an utpala, most excellent,
and on it draw a vajra,
blazing and marked with dots.

To its right place the heart mantras. To its left place the female messengers. Below it is the king of wrathful ones, Vajra Free of Extremes. In the south is the bodhisattva Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin. In the west is Ākāśagarbha. In the north is the bodhisattva Kṣitigarbha together with his retinue. They should be placed just as they appear in their attire. Or else their emblems. Or else their syllables.

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī spoke of the practice of this supreme heart mantra: "Having generated the mind set on awakening, having received empowerment, entered the maṇḍala and taken the vows, one who keeps the observance of the vidyā should recite this heart mantra three hundred thousand times, in a dwelling or a secluded cave, without slackening.

"Then take any one of these: a chisel, a trident, a sword made of sandalwood, a wheel, a hook, a club, a battle-axe, a short spear, a noose, or a great arrow. Wash it with the five products of the cow, wash it again with scented water, place it on a heap of cow dung, and protect it with the Great Cakravartin. At the end, having made extensive offerings right there, smear the maṇḍala with cow dung that has not fallen to the ground. Abiding in the maṇḍala of Vajra of Sacred Giving, perform a fire offering throughout the whole night with kindling sticks of agaru smeared with curd and honey. At midnight or at sunrise great signs will appear, the substance will be transformed, and it will be accomplished.

"Holding it, one becomes a vidyādhara, a holder of spells: one is swift as thought, roams through many other world systems, makes offerings to all the tathāgatas, attains the dhāraṇī of the limbs of awakening, has a complexion like the rising sun, is free of old age and death, and works for the benefit of all sentient beings. Except through the power of aspiration, with that very body one holds sway over the earth until one abides at the seat of awakening.

"He is a vidyādhara with the form of a youth, bearing the mark of Vajra of Sacred Giving, to whom all the gods and others pay homage; one who tames the untamed, like a jewel of manifold forms; attended by a retinue of many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of vidyādharas, dwelling in a great mansion born of mind. Seeing him, the vidyādharas think of themselves as like paupers, and they make the aspiration, 'May we too become like that!'

"Even without performing the practice, it takes effect: if one recollects it every day, even deeds such as the five of immediate retribution will be purified.

"If every day at sunrise one binds the mudrā and recites it one hundred and eight times, in six months it bestows a district.

"If one casts the mantra once on each of a number of karavīra flowers and strikes an image of the Sun with them, in one month it bestows eight excellent villages.

"If one goes to a lotus pond, enchants the lotuses with the mantra a hundred thousand times, offers them to the Blessed One Vajra of Sacred Giving, and with that same supreme heart mantra performs a fire offering of rice porridge a hundred thousand times, it bestows a kingdom.

"If one enchants one's face with the mantra, whoever one looks at comes under one's power.

"If one binds the mudrā toward someone who is angry and recollects the heart mantra, he will become gracious.

"If one sees a river full of crocodiles, enchants a stone with the mantra and throws it, all that move in the water will be paralyzed.

"If one ties a knot at the hem of one's garment and puts the garment on, the mouths of others will be bound. Likewise, where there is danger in the wilderness or from bandits, if one ties a knot in the same way, they will be paralyzed.

"For one seized by venom, if it is set at the doorway or on the neck, he will be free of poison. If one visualizes the supreme heart mantra with its dot from the soles of the feet upward, one will be free of poison.

"For one stricken by a ḍākinī, visualize it at one's own heart and visualize one's own form at the head of the sick person; he will recover at once.

"Likewise for all grahas and all bhūtas: bind the mudrā, enchant ashes or white mustard seed seven times with the mantra and strike the head with them, and they will all flee.

"If one sees a great wind rising, enchant water with the mantra and scatter it, and it will immediately subside.

"If one sees lightning striking, enchant the forefinger of the left hand with the mantra and make the threatening gesture with it, and it will be turned back; there is no doubt.

"If one wishes to pin down with a kīla one who brings death, enchant a kīla of khadira wood or of iron eight hundred times with the supreme, most fierce heart mantra. Take earth from both banks of a river and make an effigy eight finger-widths in size. Take thread from a bier on which a corpse has been laid, bind its major and minor limbs, and strike it at the heart with that kīla. It is then pinned by the kīla and can do nothing at all. To release it, pull out the kīla and fill the place with butter and honey, and the kīla will have been withdrawn.

"rājānam baśikartu gamola baṇma yiṃ prakṛtri rakṛttva rahāsa babigta parpata ākre bata kṣiṇa cara nad abarbhyaḥ yathā bibothā pūja kṛtta chittva jusū yattṛtsa badha rājas paribāro baśaṃ agacchati

"If one wishes to make someone fortunate, perform a fire offering of oblations of salt every day for twenty-one days, and among all two-footed and four-footed beings he will become the most fortunate of all.

"If one performs a fire offering of food, food will appear.

"If, gazing at Vajra of Sacred Giving, one recites a hundred thousand times without eating, he will bestow whatever supreme thing one desires.

"Make a maṇḍala at the foot of a nyagrodha tree, strew flowers and perform the protection. Burning agaru incense, without eating, and keeping the yakṣiṇī in mind, if one recites a hundred thousand times, the yakṣiṇī will come and give whatever is asked of her.

"If every day one makes offerings, takes hold of the hand of Maṇibhadra, and recites one hundred and eight times, in six months he will bestow a hundred dīnāras.

"If one takes hold of the big toe of the foot of the Buddha, the Blessed One, and recites with eyes open from sunrise until sunset, the past karma of beginningless time will be purified.

"If one gazes at Vajra of Sacred Giving and recites without closing the eyes, one will become long-lived.

"If one gazes at the noble Avalokiteśvara and constantly recollects him three times every day, he bestows the supreme.

"If one takes hold of the vajra in the hand of Vajrapāṇi and, without eating food, recites three hundred thousand times, one will be led to his own abode, and one will obtain the body of a youth of the vajra family. Likewise, if one gazes at the bodhisattvas Samantabhadra, Maitreya and the rest and, without eating, recites three hundred thousand times, one will be led to the abode of each of them.

"If the rain does not fall, perform a fire offering of eight thousand kindling sticks of gla ba wood dipped in milk, and the rain will fall.

"If one holds one's breath and recites one hundred and eight times in the water, in six months one will obtain dhāraṇī.

"If one faces the sun, turning as it moves, and recites three hundred thousand times, one will obtain the office of a great minister or a great treasure.

"If one carries a trident in one's hand, eats food gained by begging, makes offerings in a shrine of the mātṛkās with whatever one can afford, and recites three hundred thousand times, all the mātṛkās will be accomplished and will do whatever one wishes.

"Incense of guggulu dispels all grahas.

"Sesame oil enchanted with the mantra one hundred and eight times dispels pain in the belly.

"For severe diarrhea, enchant a blue thread with the mantra and tie it around the waist, and the severe diarrhea will be cured.

"For pain in the head, grind the fruit of the bilva, enchant it twenty-one times and smear it on, and it will be cured.

"For pain in the eyes, enchant the juice of wheat flowers once and pour it into the nose, and it will be cured.

"If one enchants a paste of sandalwood one hundred and eight times, it dispels burning fever.

"Incense of agaru or incense of guggulu dispels the quartan fever.

"Boil rice porridge over bandhūka wood and give it to drink to a man or a woman who is childless, and a son will be born even without union with a man.

"Make a torma of four colors, enchant four vases of four colors with the mantra a hundred times, and at a crossroads or at the foot of a solitary tree bathe a eunuch or a barren woman, and a son will be born.

"If one wishes to defeat an army, combine Keśinī with the supreme heart mantra and recite it one thousand and eight times; then bind the mudrā of the life-pole and threaten with the forefinger, and the army will be paralyzed.

"If one wishes to make it fall back, with a mind of loving-kindness bind the mudrā of Vajra of Sacred Giving on the head and contemplate, and the enemy will be made to fall back in retreat.

"If one wishes to destroy an enemy, having performed the practice, at midnight loosen one's hair, press the right foot with the left, and sit squatting. Looking off to the side, take an oblation of poison, blood, salt and mustard grains mixed together and, with Great Vajra Free of Extremes, perform a fire offering twenty-one times into a fire of bilva wood kindled by blowing. The enemy will be destroyed; of this there is no doubt.

Whether it be a god, a nāga or a human,
or Brahmā, lord of the Sahā world,
or Śakra, lord of the yakṣas,
he will die; of this there is no doubt.

"Śāntimati, in short, whatever rite one accomplishes it by, through that very rite it bestows accomplishment.

"Śāntimati, twenty-one eons ago I pleased the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the perfectly awakened Buddha Fully Blossomed Flower, Lord of Victors. Śāntimati, seeing me practicing extremely difficult austerities, he blessed me through the power of his great compassion and gave me this supreme heart mantra.

"Śāntimati, as soon as I obtained this supreme heart mantra, I obtained samādhis as numerous as the atoms of ten buddha fields, ten hundred thousand great dhāraṇīs, and ten koṭis of hundreds of thousands of great superknowledges. Tathāgatas as numerous as the atoms of ten buddha fields came and appeared before me, and those tathāgatas taught me this Dharma discourse called Wholly Transcending the Three Times. Through the power of the blessing of this very supreme heart mantra, I retained and took up that Dharma discourse in an instant, a moment, a mere second. And why? Śāntimati, at the very moment I obtained this supreme heart mantra, Śāntimati, I obtained countless hundreds of thousands of gateways of dhāraṇī, such as the Ornament Arranging Melodies with the Melody of the Buddha.

"Śāntimati, by this enumeration too you should understand it thus: whatever bodhisattva great beings dwell in the boundless world systems of the ten directions, correctly teaching the secret mantras, accomplishing the gateways of samādhi and dhāraṇī, and freed from aging, sickness and death, all of them, blessed by this very supreme heart mantra, all become of manifold colors, take on the form of youths, and perform miraculous displays of every kind.

"Śāntimati, this Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the perfectly awakened Buddha Fully Blossomed Flower, Lord of Victors, too is accomplished by his own nature, without the rite of the youth, without implements, and without a painted image.

"If one wishes to accomplish it through a painted image, on a lunar date, a karaṇa and a moment known to be auspicious, buy thread without haggling over the price. Having performed the rite of protection with the heart mantra of Great Fierce Vajra, make a maṇḍala with cow dung and strew flowers. Then have a weaver whose limbs are complete, who bears an auspicious name, who keeps the poṣadha, eats the three white foods, has restrained senses, is endowed with Dharma and always delights in the Dharma, weave a cloth of five or six cubits, with no hair falling into it, using weaving implements smeared with the five products of the cow. Having bathed, anointed with unguents, bound on a headband, and made fragrant and scented, make extensive offerings, burn incense of agaru, give the weaver a generous fee, and take the cloth down from the loom.

"Having taken it down, consecrate it with the mantra of the Great Cakravartin. In a clean place strewn with scattered flowers and perfumed with fragrances and incense, put it into scented water and leave it for seven days. Then take it out and dry it in the sun. Then rub the cloth many times with a fine powder mixed with camphor and saffron. It should also be rubbed with flowers such as nāgakesara, campaka and skya snar.

"Having kept a fast, on a lunar date, karaṇa and moment that are auspicious, free of bad dates and of the faults of inauspicious conjunctions, while the sounds of many kinds of music ring out and verses of good fortune are recited, make extensive offerings in a maṇḍala strewn with flowers. Having paid homage to all the tathāgatas and made supplication, as before, a painter who has entered the great vajra maṇḍala, who has generated the mind set on awakening, who is compassionate, skilled and free of laziness, endowed with faith, dressed in clean clothes, eating food in moderation, and who has been given protection, using new brushes and colors not mixed with glue, should paint here the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, the Arhat, the perfectly awakened Buddha Fully Blossomed Flower, Lord of Victors. He is seated on a seat of white lotus, golden in color, endowed with all the major marks, absorbed in samādhi, encircled all around by rays of light of many colors, a great light shining forth from his uṣṇīṣa, and his face should be made gently smiling.

"Below him paint Vajra of Sacred Giving, the color of saffron, wearing a lower garment of silk or cotton and an upper robe of fine silk, with five locks of hair on his head, kneeling on a seat of white lotus, adorned with every ornament. With his right hand he pays homage to the Blessed One, and in his left he holds an utpala together with a vajra. He has the form of a youth. The rays of light of the Tathāgata fall upon his head and make their bodies shine, and he sits in the midst of a net of his own light, gazing at the Blessed One. Paint him seated in a jewel cave on a great jewel mountain. Paint that jewel mountain adorned with trees that flower in every season. Below, paint the great ocean.

"On another cloth, paint Vajra of Sacred Giving seated with legs crossed in a jewel cave on a great jewel mountain, absorbed in samādhi, in the form of a youth, wearing a skirt of silk or cotton and an upper garment of fine silk, holding in his left hand an utpala together with a blazing vajra, seated within a net of his own light, adorned with every ornament. At his heart paint the heart mantra in a peaceful guise, blazing with tongues of flame, like the rays of the autumn moon, granting the supreme with the right hand and holding a vajra in the left, dressed in white, with a stream of water flowing from the fingers of the right hand. Below him, paint the practitioner, in whatever attire he wears. Paint him holding an incense bowl in his hand and kneeling.

"In solitude, make extensive offerings before it with whatever one can afford and recite three hundred thousand times, and the preliminary practice will have been accomplished.

By merely seeing this painted image
one is freed from all wrongdoing.
Whatever the substances for accomplishment,
all of them will surely be accomplished.
Even someone who, through his evil deeds,
has been born into an outcaste's birth
is at that very moment freed
from all his former evil deeds.
In front of this painted image
all rites will be accomplished.
In this world and in the next,
nothing is hard for him to obtain.
If one always sees the painting,
one will be endowed with long life, and likewise with splendor,
with wealth and with renown,
and will become fortunate and wise."

Śāntimati said, "Vajra of Sacred Giving, sentient beings who have faith in this painted image are extremely rare. Vajra of Sacred Giving, if those who listen to and enter this very mode of the Dharma discourse that is the gateway into the great maṇḍala will have honored many buddhas, then what need is there to speak of those who have received the great Vajrapāṇi Empowerment and have entered this great vajra maṇḍala? They are certain to attain unsurpassed, perfect awakening."

Mañjuśrī said, "Śāntimati, it is so; it is just as you have said. Those who listen to this mode are Vajrasattvas. Śāntimati, those who have not produced roots of virtue will not even see, or hear with their ears, the great maṇḍala, the great realization of the wheel maṇḍala. Śāntimati, it is like this: the great precious wish-fulfilling jewel that fulfills every wish does not come within sight of those with inferior roots of virtue so as to fulfill all their wishes. But, Śāntimati, that precious jewel, king of jewels, makes those sentient beings who possess many roots of virtue fortunate ones whose wishes it fulfills, just as they intended, merely by being seen and touched. Śāntimati, this Dharma discourse should be regarded in just the same way."

The Noble Vajrapāṇi Empowerment, fascicle eight.

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī again spoke these words to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "Śāntimati, furthermore, this is the practice of the king of secret mantras of my own pledge. Following the rite as before, go to the summit of a mountain and remain in one's own dwelling. Keeping silent, bathing at the three times of day and changing one's clothes three times, one should recite the syllables three hundred thousand times.

"At the end, on the full moon, make offerings with whatever one can afford, take any one substance at all, and recite throughout the whole night, and one will certainly accomplish it. As before, one is surrounded by many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of vidyādharas, while a thousand koṭis of divine maidens display cymbals and phegdo. One roams through many world systems, makes offerings to all the tathāgatas, produces many emanations, works for the benefit of all sentient beings, is like a wish-fulfilling jewel, goes wherever one wishes, is born with certainty from aspiration, obtains the dhāraṇī of the melody that proclaims infinite sounds, fully realizes the five superknowledges, has a complexion like the rising sun, and becomes swift as thought. In the manner described before, whatever one wishes will certainly be accomplished.

"If one wishes to accomplish it through a painted image, following the rite as before, paint the Blessed One dwelling in the world system Endowed with Flowers, having entered the supreme seat of awakening, seated with legs crossed and teaching the Dharma, golden in color, endowed with all the major marks, illuminating everything with the rays of light that come forth from his uṣṇīṣa. At the foot of the tree of awakening, the whole tree of awakening, made of gośīrṣa sandalwood, should be beautifully adorned like a great wish-fulfilling tree. Paint the world system beautifully adorned with arrays of every kind.

"To his right paint the bodhisattva called Golden Flower Heap, golden in color, wearing all the ornaments, holding a yak-tail fly whisk in his hand and dressed in a garment of fine cloth. To his left paint the bodhisattva called Sandalwood Heap, wearing a garment of fine cloth dark blue like the priyaṅgu flower, adorned with every ornament, holding a yak-tail fly whisk in his hand. Below, paint the eight great śrāvakas: Steadfast Gift, Vast Gift, Essence Gift, Jinamati, Vimaladatta, Buddharakṣita, Community Gift and Sumati. On the right side paint eight bodhisattvas adorned with every ornament, of different shapes, different attire and different colors. Above, paint vidyādharas with their bodies enveloped in clouds, letting fall a rain of flowers.

"Below the Blessed One paint a vidyādhara taking the form of a youth, sturdy, adorned with every ornament, holding an utpala in his hand, kneeling, with five locks of hair, receiving commands, ablaze, his face gently smiling, encircled by the light rays of the very being of the Tathāgata.

"Before it recite a million times, and combine it, as before, with the rite of the supreme heart mantra. In the same way one performs all the rites. If one recites constantly, the power will not decline; it is not otherwise.

"Śāntimati, if one who wishes for the conduct of a bodhisattva, the wealth of a bodhisattva who practices by way of the secret mantra, wishes to accomplish any heart mantra at all, then one who has seen the maṇḍala should take any one of them, following the rite as before. Bathing at the three times of day, changing one's clothes three times, washing and then anointing oneself, one should recite it six hundred thousand times with a mind full of joy. Then, at the end, make offerings with whatever one can afford, and perform a fire offering throughout the whole night with śuti dipped in curd, honey and butter, and one will obtain the office of a great minister. If one does not accomplish it, it will become effective in the rites, and it bestows a district.

"If one enters water that flows into the ocean until it is up to the throat and recites three hundred thousand times together with the supreme heart mantra, one will see a treasure.

"If one performs a fire offering of eight thousand kindling sticks of guggulu dipped in butter, it bestows eight excellent villages.

"If, at the entrance of an asura cave, without eating, one performs a fire offering a hundred thousand times of an oblation of mustard grains and salt, together with the heart mantra of Vajra Free of Extremes, asura maidens will come out, lead one inside, and give whatever one desires.

"If one wishes cattle to appear, at an anthill or at a confluence of waters, eating the five products of the cow every day and binding the vajra mudrā, one should recite this heart mantra thirty thousand times. Then make offerings with whatever one can afford, make a cow with her calf, four finger-widths in size, out of the flour of sāli rice, kindle a fire of varuṇa wood, and perform the fire offering fifty thousand times. At midnight or at sunrise, a cow will appear right there, playing about with one. If one sprinkles it with water enchanted with the heart mantra of Fierce Vajra, it will stay without wandering off. Buy, without haggling over the price, a cow to which one has given the name Fragrant, and groom it every day. If every month one fills a vase with water mixed with ground pine and worships the cow with offerings of incense and flowers, the cattle will increase to a hundred thousand; of this there is no doubt.

"Likewise, if one wishes horses, elephants, camels, sheep or buffaloes to appear, kindle a fire of gla ba wood every day and perform a fire offering of barley, milk and so forth, and they will appear. If one performs a fire offering of food, food will appear. If one performs a fire offering of rice mixed with butter, inexhaustible food will appear. Likewise, if one performs a fire offering of every kind of grain, every kind of grain will appear: whatever one makes the fire offering of, that will appear.

"If one enchants one's face with the mantra, one will always have an answer ready.

"When the moon is seized by the wolf, make offerings, take white sweet flag and put it in the mouth, and gazing at the moon, recite until the moon is released; if one then eats it, one will retain whatever one hears.

"When the moon is seized by the wolf, take the milk of a tawny cow whose calf is of the same color, smear a maṇḍala with cow dung, make offerings with whatever one can afford, and gazing at the moon, churn it until butter appears; if one drinks it, one will retain whatever one hears, and one will be free of timidity in all the treatises.

"If one enchants goat's milk mixed with honey eight hundred times and drinks it, a great chronic illness will be healed.

"If one enchants butter eight hundred times and drinks it, one will be freed from all diseases.

"If one enchants any medicine at all once with the mantra and eats it, one will be free of disease.

"If one recites it every day, one will become fortunate.

"If every day one performs a fire offering of one hundred and eight oblations of apāmārga, all people will be pleased.

"Enchant incense or flowers with the mantra, and whoever they are given to will come under one's control.

"atha rājanima baśvakartu kamo gaura sariśapa kukuma saṃmiśira nirahāro lakkhaṃ juhūyad rāja baśi bhabati

"If one wishes to bring a woman under control, speak her name, enchant a palmful of water once with the mantra, and if she drinks it she will come under one's control.

"If one wishes for small livestock, perform a fire offering of śuti one hundred and eight times at the three times of each day, and one will obtain small livestock.

"If one wishes to cross water, perform the binding of the person and then cross, and all that move in the water will be paralyzed.

"If one wishes to paralyze an army, make a club sixteen finger-widths long from a saptaparṇa tree, worship it with incense and flowers while speaking the army's name, and plant it in the ground knee-deep. Over it, for seven days, perform a fire offering a hundred thousand times of turuṣka fruit mixed with white mustard seed, and the army will be paralyzed.

"Śāntimati, even without performing the practice, it will become effective for such rites as these.

"Śāntimati, furthermore, this is the practice of Vajra Free of Extremes. There is no need to choose a lunar date or a constellation, and no need to keep a fast. Go to a great wilderness or a great charnel ground, wear blue clothes, eat food gained by begging without straying from the road, and, having set aside pride, first recite the supreme heart mantra a hundred thousand times. By this, the preliminary approach of this great wrathful one will have been done ten million times. Then go to the summit of a mountain and recite a hundred thousand times. Then on the full moon make extensive offerings and recite throughout the whole night, and at sunrise the king of wrathful ones himself will come.

"The signs of his coming are these: rays of light come forth from the practitioner's body, both eyes quiver, and the hands and feet move. When these occur, one should make offerings of flowers and incense. Then a great sound of drums arises in the north. After the sound of the drums, the king of wrathful ones himself will come, baring his fangs terrifyingly, his hair standing on end, as if threatening the practitioner. The practitioner should not be afraid. By merely seeing that wrathful one, one becomes a vidyādhara, surrounded by many koṭis of vidyādharas and surrounded by many koṭis of divine maidens. With drums beating, one will go to honor the buddhas. If one does not accomplish this, he bestows the five supreme things: kingship, invisibility, the wish-granting cow, the excellent vase, and the wish-fulfilling jewel. If one goes to a mountain and recites a hundred thousand times, he bestows whatever one wishes.

"rājānama baśikadurgamo salakṣa mekanda rājā baśo bhabati ādambhidtya ipa ājñā karoti

"If one wishes to make it descend into a vessel, recite a hundred thousand times, and it will descend into the vessel. If one wishes to bring out a treasure, recite three hundred thousand times, and it will bring out the treasure.

"If one accomplishes pills, grind lac and realgar with coriander oil, make pills, and then wrap them in copper. Having wrapped them, leave them for seven days and offer two or three pills to the Buddha, the Blessed One. If one holds one in the hand, one will become invisible.

"This king of wrathful ones possesses the splendor of immeasurable qualities: he tames the untamed, and is great in diligence and in overpowering adversaries. If one recites his mantra, even transgressions of the four root downfalls are purified, and the five deeds of immediate retribution are also purified without remainder. What need is there to speak of those who do just as has been taught? If even those who merely hold his mantra will have produced roots of virtue under many hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of buddhas, what need is there to say that everything contemplated by those who recite it and practice it will be fulfilled? Whoever keeps a fast on the fourteenth or fifteenth day and recites it casts off four eons of saṃsāra.

"Son of good family, the very name of this king of wrathful ones, who is worshiped by a hundred thousand koṭis of buddhas, is meaningful: by holding his name, all sentient beings become irreversible. This king of wrathful ones is accomplished by recitation. Through him all the buddhas have brought about deliverance.

"Take a man's corpse, and at midnight go to a riverbank, wash it with water, adorn it with flowers and so forth, and lay it with its head pointing east. The practitioner, together with a good companion, sits on a spread of kuśa grass. Enchant white sweet flag seven times with the mantra, put it into the corpse's mouth, and recite the king of vidyās until the corpse's mouth makes a sound. Then the practitioner, without fear, presses its chest with his left foot. Even though a fierce sound arises, he should not be afraid of it either; the vidyādhara should recite the vidyā until smoke arises. Then strike its head with white mustard seed over which the mantra has been recited seven times. As soon as it is struck, a snake appears from the corpse's mouth. Without being afraid of this either, recollect the vidyā and touch it. As soon as it is touched, it becomes a sword. Recollecting the heart mantra, take it up. As soon as one has taken it, one's hair becomes coiled into locks. One becomes like a sixteen-year-old. One becomes the lord of all vidyādharas. One goes about without obstruction. One lives for sixty thousand years.

"The practice of the short spear is this. The practitioner, taking milk gruel as his food, goes to the summit of a mountain and recites the vidyā three hundred thousand times. Then the vidyādhara kneads ash from a charnel ground and earth from an anthill with milk and makes a short spear one sugata-span in length. It is painted with colors not mixed with glue. Then go to a charnel ground, smear a maṇḍala with cow dung, make offerings of perfume, flowers, incense and the rest, and offer all the tormas for the bhūtas. Having made oneself clean and put on clean clothes, sit on a human skull, place the short spear on another human skull, and recite the vidyā until the short spear blazes. Then offer the guest water with flowers that other mantrins cannot overcome.

Or else, if one recites the vidyā one hundred and eight times, beings will come from all directions. Without becoming afraid, one should call to mind the heart mantra of the King of Wrathful Ones and touch the short spear. As soon as it is touched, it turns into a woman and says, "Son, what shall I do?" One should say to her, "I go to you for refuge." Then she vanishes into the palm of the mantrin's hand. At that very moment his hair becomes coiled into a topknot, and he becomes like one sixteen years old. Holding the short spear in his hand, he becomes a vidyādhara, and except for the Cakravartin, the vidyādharas come under his power. All those he looks at touch their heads to his feet.

If one would accomplish wealth, by offering eight thousand fire sticks of bilva wood in a fire offering one obtains a hundred dīnāras. By offering in the fire a hundred thousand pills of guggulu the size of a juniper berry, one obtains a thousand stamped coins. Having made a fire of agaru wood, by mixing the fruit of the pata tree with curd, honey and ghee and offering it a thousand times in the fire, one obtains a dīnāra every day without asking for it. If one wishes to bring a woman under one's power, by offering eight hundred oblations of chaff in the fire, she comes under one's power. rājāmatyabaśikaranebalanahūtinan aṣṭasahasranjuhūyaṅbaśobhabati. Entering water that flows into the ocean, if one scatters a hundred thousand flowers of various colors, without asking one obtains a pair of cloths every day. Going to a charnel ground and making a maṇḍala, if one offers human fat in the fire for seven days, one who has been seized by a piśāca is released merely by being looked at. By offering mongoose hair in the fire six thousand times, all piśācas come under one's power, and all bhūtas too come under one's power. By offering black sheep's wool smeared with ghee in the fire a hundred thousand times, a woman comes under one's power.

If one wishes to see the piśācas, one should make a maṇḍala the size of a cowhide in a charnel ground and make tormas of fish and the like. Facing east, if one offers human hair smeared with blood in the fire a hundred thousand times, all the piśācas will come. One should not be afraid, and one should not perform protection. One should say, "You will come under my power." They say, "So be it," and vanish. Every day they give him five dīnāras each, and these too should be spent.

By enchanting one's finger seven times, one turns back the clouds. By enchanting ash seven times, speech is paralyzed. By enchanting white mustard seed seven times, a mountain shakes. By enchanting a mālatī flower or a kuṭaja flower seven times, the woman who holds it, or to whom it is given, or on whose head it is placed, comes under one's power.

If one wishes to bring a yakṣiṇī under one's power, one should eat alms food in a charnel ground and recite the mantra. There, on dry ground, one should make a maṇḍala the size of a cowhide and make an extensive torma. Then, when the vidyādhara offers in the fire seven thousand fire sticks of arka wood smeared with blood, a yakṣiṇī in a hideous form will come. Without being afraid of her, recalling the vidyā, one should say, "Stay! Stay!" She grants one of three excellent things: she becomes a mother, a sister or a wife. If she becomes a mother, she does everything he needs, as for a son. If she becomes a sister, she gives him a dīnāra every day. If she becomes a wife, she takes her pleasure with him. He becomes able to live for five hundred years.

Then the vidyādhara is to accomplish the wheel. Having done the preliminary practice, he should have a smith who is clean, keeping the poṣadha, and not lacking any limb make a hammered wheel eight finger-widths across, encircled with blazing flames. Then the vidyādhara places that wheel in the five products of the cow and leaves it there for one day. Then he takes it out, washes it, anoints it, wraps it in clean cloth, and sets it down below and in front. He makes offerings and tormas, takes up the vessel of guest water, and with the root mantra invites all the deities. Having offered them guest water as well, again with the root mantra he should request them to depart. By casting the mantra seven times on ash, it becomes a fence of ash. By enchanting a grass fire seven times, it becomes a fence of fire. Then, having made protection according to the rite, he binds the ground by enchanting white mustard seed seven times, and binds the sky by enchanting water seven times. Then the mantrin fills the vessel of guest water with scented water and puts white flowers in it. With the root mantra he invites his own deity, offers guest water, places the deity on a seat, makes the offerings complete, and first offers in the fire seven oblations of ghee, honey and milk. Then the mantrin takes up the wheel with both hands and recites until it blazes.

Then the sound of drums resounds. The thunder rumbles, "diriri." The flames of the lamps grow long. The wheel also moves. From the sky too a voice resounds: "Son of noble family, excellent, excellent! It is excellent that you have accomplished this wheel, that you have entered the path of awakening, and that you have crossed the ocean of existence!" Then the mantrin should recite again and again until Vajrapāṇi comes. When Vajrapāṇi has come, he says to the practitioner, "Son, it is excellent that you have conquered the army of Māra and entered the path of awakening." Then Vajrapāṇi strokes the crown of the practitioner's head with his right hand. The moment he has stroked it, there before that son of noble family is a celestial palace a thousand yojanas in extent, hung with nets of bells and small bells, festooned with golden threads and strings of pearls, beautified with sapphires, great sapphires, rubies, great emeralds, the finest perfumes and other special jewels, strewn with arrays of various vajras, and adorned with hosts of divine maidens, vidyādharas and others; and they too are singing, and playing sweet-sounding lutes, drums and flutes, and clapping their hands.

Then, through the power of the King of Vidyās, noble Vajrapāṇi himself takes the practitioner by his right hand, leads him into the celestial palace, and says, "Son of noble family, go forth to the infinite buddha fields!" Then, as a cakravartin of the vidyādharas whose movement is unobstructed, through the power of that great King of Wrathful Ones he becomes lord of all the cakravartins of the vidyādharas. Then Vajrapāṇi places that vidyādhara in the celestial palace and vanishes. Then the king of the vidyādharas, surrounded by a retinue of many koṭis of vidyādharas, with many kings of vidyādharas paying homage, showing reverence and making offerings, goes forth, the cakravartin of the vidyādharas at the front giving commands to all.

This wrathful one is supreme and glorious, always follows all the buddhas, and performs every activity: by merely being recited he performs every activity. He terrifies all bhūtas. He summons them. He brings them under control. He dries them up. He harms them. He accomplishes whatever all vidyādharas desire.

If one wishes to accomplish a mount, then one should make a goose, a bull, a peacock, a garuḍa, an elephant, a horse, or whichever of these one likes. One should have a painter who keeps the poṣadha paint it, and set the painted figure down facing west. Having made extensive offerings of guest water, offered lamps as well, and performed the rite of protection too, one should burn fire sticks of udumbara wood and make a fire offering a thousand times. With scented water one should invite the deity, request the deity to depart again, and offer guest water. Having offered guest water, one should press down with one's hand on whichever mount it is and recite until it blazes. Then the mantrin should recite again and again. If it is a goose, the sign of its accomplishment is that the goose cries out. The bull bellows. The peacock calls, "lhung lhung." The garuḍa flies. The elephant makes the sound "ngur ngur." The horse neighs, "tsererere." These are the signs for these.

If one rides the goose, one becomes a second Brahmā, lord of the Sahā world, and is able to live for an eon, and even beyond that. If one rides the bull, one becomes a second Great God. If one rides the peacock, one becomes a second Kārttikeya, son of the Kṛttikās, brave and possessed of great power to vanquish opponents, and is able to live for a great eon. When he dies and passes on, he is then born into a brahmin family and remembers his former lives. If one accomplishes the garuḍa and rides it, one becomes a second Viṣṇu and is able to live for an eon. When he dies and passes on, he is then born into a family like a great sāl tree and remembers his former lives. If one accomplishes the elephant and rides it, one comes to have the diligence of Śakra, rules as a king of the gods, is free of illness and handsome, and sports with the goddesses. When he dies and passes on, he is then born into a family like a great sāl tree and remembers his former lives. If one accomplishes the horse and rides it, one becomes a second Sun, of great power, and is able to live for an intermediate eon. When he dies and passes on, he is then born into a family like a great sāl tree and remembers his former lives.

If one wishes to accomplish a vajra, one should have a smith or a carpenter who keeps the poṣadha make a single-pointed vajra eight or sixteen finger-widths long, of copper, agaru, sandalwood, red sandalwood, pine, khadira, banda wood, nimba wood, crystal, or torch wood. One should anoint it with the five products of a tawny cow, and on the fifteenth day of the waxing moon, before the Blessed One, make extensive offerings and give tormas as well. Having washed the vajra with scented water, one should offer oneself to all the bodhisattvas, make aspirations, recite the heart mantra of the Wrathful One, invite the deity with guest water, offer guest water and a seat, and make a fire offering of seven oblations of ghee and honey. Then the mantrin should take the vajra in his right hand and recite until it blazes and crackles. Then the mantrin should recite again and again until, from the east, the King of Wrathful Ones, Vajra Free of Extremes, comes, surrounded by all the perfections and surrounded by nine koṭis of vidyās. Each of those vidyās in turn is surrounded by a retinue of five hundred koṭis. That King of Vidyās holds a vajra in his left hand, and with his right hand and a sweet, deep voice he reassures the practitioner. Blazing with splendor like the sun, honored by all the gods, who rain down showers of flowers, he comes. Then the practitioner should offer guest water to the one who has come, and offer a seat as well. Having made aspirations, he should make this supplication: "Blessed One, please be compassionate! Please grant me accomplishment!" Then the great bodhisattva touches the practitioner's head with his right hand and says, "Son, what shall I do?" Then the practitioner offers guest water, kneels on both knees, and says, "Please grant me the vajra accomplishment. Let it be known that through it, by your kindness, I shall have great diligence and great strength, become a king of vidyādharas, and be lord of all the vidyādharas." Then the Blessed One says to the practitioner, "Son, you have accomplished this vajra. You will have diligence and the power to vanquish opponents, and will become the great king of all the kings of the vidyādharas." Just as with the vajra, so too is the accomplishment of the short spear and the rest.

The unobstructed detailed rite of the Blessed One Vajra Free of Extremes is complete.

Then the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi said this to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "O great being, there is a maṇḍala called Vajra Empowerment, and the Blessed One explained it to me alone.

At the time of his great parinirvāṇa, he entrusted to me this supreme secret as well: the teaching and the most secret of secrets, the great and sacred secret which even all the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas do not comprehend, let alone other, worldly people. Śāntimati, what is the supremely secret great maṇḍala? It is this: it is called Vajra Empowerment. It pervades all of this without remainder, with everything moving and unmoving. Śāntimati, there is not even a buddha field that it does not pervade."

When he had said this, the bodhisattva Śāntimati said this to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "O great being, please explain that supremely secret maṇḍala, so that bodhisattva great beings, having heard such supremely secret maṇḍalas, will act for the benefit of sentient beings, and so that, with regard to the people of the future, the teaching will endure."

When he had said this, Vajrapāṇi said this to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "Śāntimati, listen to that supremely secret maṇḍala by which bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct through the gateway of secret mantra accomplish great benefit by easy means. Śāntimati, first of all the master should take possession of any suitable ground and draw the maṇḍala. That ground should be purified with the awakening mind.

Whatever mantrin, while abiding in the way of focusing,
acts in some other way,
will not swiftly gain accomplishment,
nor will he attain accomplishment at all.

Śāntimati, listen.

The laying out of the king of maṇḍalas,
the sacred maṇḍala, by seeing which
accomplishment is attained,
I shall explain to you, O you of excellent vows.
For the sake of the samaya, to embodied beings
the buddhas of the past have taught it.
I shall explain the rite for drawing it;
listen with complete attention.
With an enclosing wall five cubits all around,
set out four doors with porticoes.
Then what the mantrin does
is to divide the maṇḍala into two tiers.
One should enter by the western door,
or else enter by the northern door.
With a mind endowed with compassion
one should make the enclosing wall likewise.
Having entered within, the mantrin
calls the tathāgatas to mind
and, to bring benefit to sentient beings,
draws the king of maṇḍalas.

Śāntimati, listen.

The laying out of the king of maṇḍalas,
just as the Buddha taught it,
I shall relate to you in full;
listen with an undistracted mind.
In its center the wise one
draws a supremely beautiful mudrā:
a great lotus with eight petals,
held to be variegated and beautiful.
The eastern petal is white,
and in the west it is green;
in the north one draws it red,
and in the south it is black.
The remaining petals, it is said,
the Victors have taught are to be drawn.
In its center, its heart,
one should draw an upright vase;
upon it a great lotus,
and on the lotus an upright vajra.
At its outer edge one draws
a most beautiful circle of light rays.
Whatever maṇḍala it may be,
one should furnish it with its seed syllable.
Upon that the wise one
sets a beautiful wheel of Dharma
upon a white lotus,
and surrounds it with a ring of vajras.
In the northeast the learned one
draws a supremely excellent mudrā:
on something like a lunar disk
he places a disk of a wish-fulfilling jewel.
Upon that one draws
a supremely beautiful white lotus,
peaceful and encircled with garlands of flames;
draw it as ever praised by the buddhas.
In the north one draws a conch,
and makes it white and brilliantly shining;
it is like Lokeśvara, Lord of the World;
draw this beautiful mudrā.
On the conch one draws a vajra,
and on the vajra a lotus.
Going to the south,
one should carefully draw a mudrā
resembling a disk of wind,
and on it place wind.
Upon that is a lotus;
draw it with light like the fire at the end of time.
Upon that the wise one
draws the vajra of Vajrapāṇi.
Going to the west,
one draws a most beautiful mudrā
called the utpala,
and places it upon a lotus.
This is the bodhisattva of great power
called Mañjughoṣa.
Going to the southwest,
one should draw a single sword,
place it upon a white lotus,
and encircle it with light rays.
On both sides of the door, right and left,
one draws two kings of wrathful ones:
the protector Trailokyavijaya
and the wrathful Candratilaka.
The wrathful Candratilaka holds a hammer;
Trailokyavijaya holds the vajra.
Then the mantrin, settled in equipoise,
draws on the outer tier.

There, in the east, one draws Īśāna, lord of the bhūtas, with his retinue. In the north one draws Śakra, lord of the gods, with his retinue. In the south one draws Yama with his retinue. In the west one draws the Four Great Kings with their retinues. To the right and left of the door one draws the two, Durdharṣa and Abhimukha. In the four directions one should place the four great protectresses, namely: in the east, Abhayaṃdadā; in the north, Aparājitā; in the south one draws Marīcī with the mudrā of the noose; in the west one draws Bhayabhairavā with the mudrā of the club. Then in the four directions one places four vases with neck ribbons, and in the center one places a single vase.

Then the master,
in the manner of the former rite,
takes the disciple with great care,
and, just as in the section on the earlier maṇḍala,
should give perfume and flowers.

Then the bodhisattva Śāntimati, his heart moved with longing by wonder, sat gazing at the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi and spoke these words:

"How wonderful, great being, you who
in all the buddha fields
have shown a great proclamation!
What is the conduct you practice?
Having seen a maṇḍala such as this,
by which accomplishment is achieved,
you have taught this supreme and sacred
Vajra Empowerment, most marvelous."

When he had said this, the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi said this to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "Śāntimati, excellent, excellent! These words you have spoken are good. Śāntimati, the Tathāgata himself said this: 'Vajrapāṇi, this maṇḍala of Vajra Empowerment is supremely and most secret.'"

When he had said this, Śāntimati said this to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Oh, great being, how is what is called Vajra Empowerment taught?"

When he had said this, Vajrapāṇi said this to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "Śāntimati, excellent, excellent that you have asked this! Śāntimati, listen to the far-reaching power of the Vajra Empowerment. Śāntimati, leave aside this maṇḍala: all the tathāgatas also gave me a vajra that has emerged from many samādhis and is accompanied by a retinue of many dhāraṇīs. This wisdom of all the tathāgatas is more ancient even than the buddhas. Śāntimati, this wisdom vajra the mantrin should give to any son or daughter of noble family with whom he is pleased, imagining it upon the head; this itself is their samaya. Therefore it is called Vajra Empowerment. Why is that? Because this vajra of the lord gives supreme protection, guards the teaching everywhere, is itself the Tathāgata, and destroys all obstructors. Therefore the mantrin should never, in any way whatsoever, give up the vajra praised by all the buddhas."

When he had said this, Śāntimati said this to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Vajrapāṇi, excellent, excellent that, wishing to benefit sentient beings, you have explained the maṇḍala of the supreme great secret!

This unsurpassed wisdom vajra,
the very self of the tathāgatas,
abiding in the form of wisdom,
the foolish do not know."

When he had said this, the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi said this to the bodhisattva Śāntimati: "Śāntimati, I shall explain the great secret to you. Śāntimati, do not show this king of maṇḍalas to anyone who lacks faith. Do not give this samaya to anyone except those who are completely pure."

This concludes the section on the Vajra Empowerment from the Vajrapāṇi Empowerment.

Then at that time the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi surveyed the maṇḍala of the entire assembly and said to the youthful Mañjuśrī: "Mañjuśrī, in the true teaching of what is meaningful in the vajra maṇḍala for bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct through the gateway of secret mantra, there are vast arrays of mudrās. They are the signs of the definitive emergence of meaning; they have arisen from the supreme vajra and manifest in various kinds of shapes; they fully satisfy the realms of sentient beings, with their differing dispositions, just as they are. Emanating bodily forms adorned with variety, in the differing destinies, families, places of birth, colors and shapes, the differing conducts, the differing dispositions, the differing modes and etymological expressions, and the differing paths of speech, they bring beings definitively into the sameness of body, speech and mind in the infinite, vast world systems that are definitively taught. They possess bodies generated by the dharmadhātu, and they arise from the magical display of the bodhisattvas. For the sake of bodhisattvas who practice the bodhisattva conduct through the gateway of secret mantra, I shall explain them to you; listen."

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī said to the bodhisattva great being Vajrapāṇi: "Son of noble family, this is the time for it; this is the occasion for it. Son of noble family, please explain the vast array of mudrās of the gateway of the ornament of the heart of all the tathāgatas." Then that entire assembly spoke with one voice: "Bodhisattva, if you know that the time has come, this is the time, this is the occasion."

Then at that time the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi entered into equipoise in the samādhi called the Ornament Essence of All the Tathāgatas. As soon as he had entered into equipoise, the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi at that time spoke the vast array of mudrās that arise from the natural outflow of the wisdom of all the tathāgatas and are the ornament of all the tathāgatas:

First, join the two hands with hollowed palms, place the two ring fingers inside, and put the two middle fingers side by side and raise them upright. Place the two index fingers level with the third joint, held just short of touching. Raise the two little fingers and the two thumbs upright as well, inclined toward them. This is the vajra mudrā of all the samayas, and its mantra is namaḥsarbatathāgatesamayabajrahūṃ.

Then all the tathāgatas and all the bodhisattvas, their eyes opened wide with wonder, said, "Great sage, this samaya of all the buddhas and bodhisattvas is marvelous!

Likewise the śrāvakas of the Victor,
and whatever pratyekabuddhas there are in the world,
solitary like the rhinoceros,
those of great magical power such as Brahmā,
whatever gods of the world there are,
and the others, the yakṣas, gandharvas,
garuḍas, kinnaras and the rest,
rākṣasas, pretas, oppressors,
and those known as shadow-oppressors and causers of forgetting,
all of them spoke these words:
"The sacred samaya is marvelous!
Those who transgress this samaya
of all the buddhas, the protectors,
those possessed of hostile minds, their heads
the glorious Vajradhara,
who benefits all sentient beings,
always crushes with the vajras,
fierce in their wild garlands of flame.
Those who transgress the samaya will surely
go to the lower realms and the like.
Having displeased the protectors, the bodhisattvas,
and the perfect buddhas,
they have no accomplishment in this life,
nor any fruit here in other lives.
If you desire the supreme fruit,
therefore, in every way,
the intelligent should make a firm effort
and guard this samaya."

From this very mudrā, joining the two index fingers in that same position is the mudrā of all the Vajradharas. Swiftly spreading the two index fingers apart again is the mudrā of Vajra Jhigara. Spreading the two index fingers out sideways is the mudrā of the all-proclaiming fire. From this very mudrā, placing the two thumbs inside is the mudrā of the vajra fence. From that very mudrā, bending the two index fingers, pointing them downward and placing them together with the thumbs is the mudrā of the vajra kīla. From this very mudrā, bending the two little fingers and keeping the rest as they were is the mudrā of the vajra net. From that very mudrā, bringing all the fingers together in the manner of a blossom just opening is the mudrā of the vajra utpala. From the mudrā of the vajra utpala, embracing all the fingers with the two index fingers and pressing the two thumbs at the base of the index fingers is the mudrā of the vajra bracelet. From the mudrā of the vajra bracelet, pressing the two thumbs against the sides of the index fingers is the mudrā of the wrathful Vajra Mouth. From that very mudrā of the Vajra Mouth, placing the two index fingers on the inner side and raising them is the mudrā of the vajra fang. From that very mudrā, drawing the two thumbs slightly in along the sides of the index fingers is the mudrā of the heart of all the tathāgatas. Bending the two little fingers inward and raising the two ring fingers upright side by side is the mudrā of all the bodhisattvas. From that very mudrā, embracing the two ring fingers crosswise with the two little fingers and keeping the rest as they were is the mudrā of the vajra that fully protects all beings. From this very mudrā, embracing the two ring fingers with the two middle fingers and keeping the rest as they were is the mudrā of the Blessed Lady Māmakī. From that very mudrā, pressing the two thumbs at the base of the ring fingers is the mudrā of Vajra Grace. From this very mudrā of Vajra Grace, spreading the two index fingers is the mudrā of the vajra flower.

Making both hands into fists and bending the two index fingers in the shape of a vajra, with the two middle fingers side by side, is the mudrā of the vajra trident. Likewise, interlacing the fingers of both hands in reverse, with the two index fingers side by side, is the mudrā of the vajra tip. From this very mudrā, pressing down the right thumb with the left and keeping the rest as before is the mudrā of the vajra chain. From this very mudrā, spreading the two middle fingers side by side and placing the two thumbs facing their sides is the mudrā of the vajra source. From this very mudrā, extending the two ring fingers is the mudrā of the Vajra Clawed One. From this very mudrā, extending the two little fingers and hiding the two thumbs is the mudrā of the Vajra Beauty of the Family.

Placing the two hands side by side at the heart, putting the two thumbs inside and extending all the rest is the mudrā of the vajra canopy. From this very mudrā, bending the two ring fingers and the two little fingers inward as well is the mudrā of the vajra crest. From that very mudrā, placing the two index fingers on the tips of the two thumbs and keeping the rest as before is the mudrā of the vajra hand. Likewise, joining both hands with hollowed palms, shaping all the fingers like a bell, and placing the two ring fingers inside and moving them is the mudrā of the vajra bell. Likewise, making both hands into fists and raising the two little fingers side by side is the mudrā of the vajra great drum. From that very mudrā, raising the two thumbs and bending the two little fingers inward is the mudrā of the vajra hammer. From that same mudrā, raising the two middle fingers side by side in the manner of an embrace and bending the two thumbs slightly is the mudrā of the vajra club. From this one, placing the two thumbs inside is the mudrā of the Blue Club. From that one, bending the two index fingers in against the sides of the middle fingers and pressing them close is the mudrā of Vajra Red Body. Likewise, making both hands into fists, squeezing them in a circling motion and raising the thumbs is the mudrā of Vajra Kīlikīli.

From that very mudrā, bending the two thumbs inward, raising the ring fingers and bending them at the third joint in a beckoning gesture is the mudrā of the youth Subāhu. From that very mudrā, putting the two index fingers side by side and bending them at the third joint is the mudrā of the youth Vajrasena. From this very mudrā, raising the two middle fingers side by side and keeping the rest as before is the mudrā of the youth Vajra Uṣṇīṣa. From this very mudrā, bending the left index finger inward is the mudrā of the youth Vajra Diadem, the playful servant. Likewise, turning both palms upward, raising the two ring fingers and the two thumbs, and bending the tips of the two middle fingers in the center is the mudrā of Nalakūbara. From that very mudrā, bending the two little fingers inward and extending the two ring fingers straight out is the mudrā of the wrathful Candratilaka. Making the right hand into a fist and placing it between the eyebrows, setting the index and middle fingers against the forehead in the manner of eyes, is the mudrā of Vajra Fearsome Gaze. Likewise, joining both hands with hollowed palms and embracing the two middle fingers, placed side by side, with the two index fingers is the mudrā of the wish-fulfilling jewel of Samantabhadra and the others.

Clenching both hands into fists in the manner of a bracelet, extending the two index fingers and bending them just slightly, is the mudrā of the club of Mahābala. From that very mudrā, joining the tips of the two index fingers to make a circle is the mudrā of the vajra noose. Crossing those same index fingers and joining them is the mudrā of the vajra bracelet. From that very mudrā, bending the left index finger just slightly is the mudrā of the vajra shaft.

Likewise, joining both hands with hollowed palms, bending the two index fingers into the palms, and bending the two middle fingers at the third joint so that they point downward is the mudrā called Vajra hūṃ. Likewise, joining both hands with hollowed palms, bending the two ring fingers and the two little fingers inward, and embracing the two thumbs with the two index fingers is the mudrā of the vajra uṣṇīṣa. Gathering in all the fingers of the right hand and extending the middle finger just slightly is the mudrā of all the vajra uṣṇīṣas. From this very mudrā, extending the two index fingers is the mudrā of the vajra boundary. From this very mudrā, raising the middle and index fingers side by side is the mudrā of the vajra sword. From this very mudrā, bringing them close to the two thumbs and keeping the rest as before is the mudrā of the vajra female messengers.
