By this method, in this sequence,
One should harm the obstructors.
Unless they are harmed for their evil,
Even when urged, they will not become well.

If the great Vajrasattva,
Being possessed of a mind of compassion,
Wishes to make the obstructor peaceful,
He should act in the manner of this rite.
From cow dung
He should make a form resembling him,
And the mantrin, with a peaceful mind,
Should wash it with milk;
At once he will become just as he was before.
Thus with all the obstructors,
With each, one should act according to the rite.
In the manner of this sequence
One should apply it to the secret mantras too:
Whether they have been cut off or suppressed,
Or blocked or bound,
If one wishes to accomplish them,
They then become just as they were before.
Or else, for the secret mantras
In particular, one should use incense,
Or make a fire offering
Of the milk of a tawny cow, or of butter.
Or else with a hundred and eight
Fire offerings, all will be pacified.
With a peaceful mind, pacifying;
With a joyful mind, increasing;
And whatever is a fierce, violent activity,
That one should undertake in wrath.
Just as each action is fitting,
So the mantrin should apply it to the secret mantras.
Otherwise he should not do it,
For the action will bear no fruit.

This is the stance of the vajra wheel.

Then again, in the vajra manner,
Having assumed the vajra arising,
Joining his palms at the crown,
He should perform the vajra dance intently.
In the manner of the posture with heels together,
Moving his eyebrows, he should touch with his foot,
And smear both hands upon the ground.
Likewise in the four directions too,
In the manner of circling with the mind,
He turns his feet like a wheel.
Placing both hands at the waist,
Bursting into vajra laughter,
He should raise his right hand
And threaten fiercely with the vajra finger.
He should utter hūṃ while drawing himself up.
When the one possessed of the eyes of the vajra sun
Has threatened with the vajra finger,
Even the lord of Māras himself,
On seeing it, flees at once,
Like a crow seeing a stone being thrown.

This is the vajra laughter.

Then the great vajra mantrin,
Having assumed the vajra arising
And taken an uneven stance,
Crosses his hands and makes fists.
Raising the thumbs upward,
He should put the middle fingers together and raise them,
And raise the two index fingers as a pair.
This is called the vajra mouth.
He should lift the right foot,
And plant the left, and do likewise.
The vajra tongue blazes like fire;
He should make it manifest like the mare's mouth.
Uttering hūṃ like rolling thunder,
He should place it directly before his mouth.
If it can drink the waters of all four oceans
Of all the four directions,
What need to speak of all the hostile ones
Who appear before his eyes?
When he does this, in all four directions
All that arises on every side dries up.
All the whirlwinds, the thunderbolts,
And the clouds gathering from every side
Are utterly dispersed
By the opening and the scattering of the vajra mouth.
Whatever omens have arisen in the world,
Whatever has come to settle on every side,
The mantrin, transforming them into flames
Like the flames of the vajra fire, pacifies them all.
With the mudrā of the vajra mouth
He should devour all the obstructors.
With the mudrā of the blazing vajra
He should chew up all the obstructors.

This is the stance of the gaping vajra mouth.

Then the great vajra yogin,
Having transformed the vajra wisdom,
Restores the obstructors;
In this there is no fault.
Since the vajra master born
From the wisdom of the Victors restores them,
He is praised as being like myself:
As the mantrin is, so is the Victor.
He should make firm compassion for all beings
And the mind of awakening,
For by method one attains the supreme
In all the magical displays.
Otherwise the buddhas
Dwelling in the directions will not praise him.
One devoid of the mind of awakening
Is not worthy of the worship of the gods.
The children of the Victors who are devoid of method and wisdom,
The śrāvakas,
And whatever pratyekabuddhas there are,
Abide in the state of austerity,
And, having minds bent on benefiting their own aims,
Have no part in this.
The mind of awakening, born
Of method and wisdom, is compassionate.
Relying on the chariot of the mind of awakening,
The reins of loving-kindness and compassion,
The grounds and the perfections,
The vajra of the four means of gathering,
The ground of omniscience,
And the limbs of the factors of awakening,
The children of the Buddha
Play in the delight of benefiting beings.
In the same way the master Vajradhara,
Skilled in method and wisdom,
Should thoroughly restore
The minds of all the hostile ones.

Then again, in the vajra manner,
Having assumed the vajra arising,
Setting the feet in an even position,
And joining the vajra palms,
Having performed the vajra dance,
He raises his right hand upward.
Resting the left upon the hip,
He puts the middle and index fingers together
And spreads apart the little finger and the thumb.
With the right forming the gods' triple banner,
Pressing down with the right extended foot,
In the manner of vajra laughter he says,
"Ah! I myself am the vajra bearer;
I abide in my own nature!"
Uttering hūṃ with haughtiness and wrath,
He should send it forth with the vajra dance.
This mudrā threatens with the vajra.
When he threatens with vajra laughter,
Gods and asuras, together with guhyakas,
At the time of the maṇḍala or of accomplishment,
Will not come to obstruct.
Bound first by the vajra holder,
Even the one who arose from the crown
Will not come to obstruct.
Knowing the vajra holder directly,
The obstructors speak to one another,
And for the sake of protection all of them
Go together before the maṇḍala.
Since they are urged on by the vajra stance,
To the greatly fortunate vajra master
Who has gathered the accumulation of merit,
All the others pay homage.

Then once again, in the vajra manner,
Having fully performed the vajra dance,
And having joined the palms in the lotus way,
One should spread them out toward all directions.
Taking the stance with the right leg extended,
Like the light of the fire at the eon's end,
In the manner of clapping the palms together,
One should strike the hands together in wrath.
With both hands in the vajra manner,
Joining the little fingers and the thumbs,
Resting the left hand at the hip
And raising the right hand upward,
Urging them on by uttering āḥ hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ,
The mantrin also utters the all-pervading sound.
When the vajra sound has arisen,
All who are hostile are brought to ruin.
Śakra, Brahmā, the Moon and the rest,
And even the great planets of great magical power,
Upon merely hearing it, are all shaken,
And by the all-pervading sound their minds give way.
At that time they join their palms and bow.
To wherever the great vajra sound may be
They all come together,
And, tormented by the vajra sound,
Their minds fallen from their seats,
They say, "Lord, what task do you require?
Give us your command quickly!"
And so they receive their instructions from the mantrin.
When the mantrin, having reflected, performs the dance,
The gods are swiftly set free.
Then, generating a mind of loving-kindness,
The one who keeps the observance releases the mudrā.
This is the vajra sound that resounds everywhere.

Seated in the vajra-arising posture,
Having made the vajra dance blaze forth,
And having joined the palms in vajra fashion,
Spreading them out toward all directions,
Taking up the uneven stance of the feet,
Crossing the hands and clenching them into fists,
Raise the two thumbs side by side;
Place the two middle fingers side by side,
Bend the index fingers at the middle joint,
And join them to the two middle fingers.
By forming the vajra mouth,
All who are hostile are pacified.
If it is placed directly before the mouth,
The vajra sound will also arise:
"I am the one who has the great vajra,
Endowed with the power of vajra wisdom.
hri ha hūṃ hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ phaṭ."
In the manner of the vajra stride,
With the steps of vajra trampling,
Moving by setting the feet down and lifting them,
One should recite this toward the four directions.
Urged on by the arising vajra sound,
The great obstructors, the renowned ones,
And also the gods of great magical power
Go before him, saying:
"We are burned by the vajra sound!
Because the vajra sound has urged us on,
We are shaken from our own abodes.
Lord of the vajra, give your command quickly!
Do you wish for the door to fortune?
Kingship, mercury, eye ointment,
Or do you wish to behold treasures everywhere?
Give your command!" So they speak.
Having joyfully given these to the practitioner,
They pay homage, make offerings, and depart.

Then once again, in the vajra manner,
The vajra master of great might,
Having fully performed the vajra dance,
Binds the mudrā as the vajra bearer.
Setting the foot against the knee,
Clench both hands into fists;
Raising the two middle fingers,
Join the two index fingers to their sides.
Taking the stance with the right leg extended,
Swiftly, with the vajra stride,
One should strike all the devices
And open all the doors.
The asuras, the yakṣa kings,
And the rākṣasas of great magical power
Are all shaken from their own abodes,
Thinking, "Has the eon come to its end?"
Struck by the vajra mudrā,
Their minds become dazed,
And to wherever he may be,
The one greatly endowed with vajra wisdom, they draw near:
"We are burned by the blazing vajra!
Does the mantrin wish for the door to fortune,
Or does he wish for the elixir of mercury?
Lord of the vajra, give your command quickly!"
Having spoken, all of them at once
Grant whatever the mind desires.
If one wishes to employ
Gods, asuras and guhyakas,
By that wish they are summoned,
And they carry out tasks as one wishes.
As for those who act against the command,
If, though bound by their pledge, they do not come,
The master, the vajra holder,
Fully endowed with the vajra mudrā,
Wrathful, pressing his lip with his teeth,
Terrifying like a blazing fire,
Swiftly, with the vajra stride,
Should strike the ground everywhere.
Taking the stance with the right leg extended,
If, though bound by their pledge, they do not come,
He should strike with the vajra mudrā.
When struck by the vajra mudrā,
They cry out, "a la la!"
Their bodies too are cast down from their seats,
Their bodies blaze fiercely with fire,
And to wherever he may be,
The great vajra yogin, they draw near:
"Greatly fortunate one, protect us!
Give us your command quickly!"
The nāgas, guhyakas and asuras
At once do as they are commanded.
Having accomplished what they were commanded,
They go each to their own abode.
"If it is so, you lords among the gods may go;
If it is not so, I will not let you go."
Then, having done as they were commanded,
They swiftly go to their abodes.
The master, the vajra holder,
Then releases the mudrā,
And in the manner of the vajra dance
Should place the hands at the waist.

Then once again, in the vajra manner,
Having fully performed the vajra dance,
With the mudrā of the vajra noose
One should bind all who are hostile.
Having urged them on at the four times,
If they contravene the command,
One should summon them with the vajra hook
And bind them with the vajra noose.
This is the manifold vajra sound.

Having joined the palms in vajra fashion,
And standing in the even stance,
One should strike behind with the hand
And toss the vajra upward.
Then, gazing at the vajra,
Uttering hūṃ like slow thunder,
In the manner of shifting the feet,
Shift the right and likewise the left.
Joining the index finger of the left hand
With the thumb, make a noose.
With the index finger of the right hand,
Its tip bent as a hook, hold it firm.
With eyes proud as a vajra,
Wrathful, pressing the lip with the teeth,
Wherever the obstructors may be,
Go there swiftly with the vajra stance,
And taking the stance with the right leg extended,
With the mudrā of the vajra hook
One should summon all the obstructors
And bind them with the vajra noose.
Even if it be the Wheel-Bearer (Viṣṇu),
Or the mighty Rudra together with Brahmā,
Even if Vajradhara protects them,
The mantrin draws them out from their very ground and summons them.
Having summoned them and bound them with the vajra,
One should put them in a vajra cage,
And keep them there
For as long as it takes for the wish to be fulfilled.
If they do not heed the command,
And the wish is not fulfilled,
Then, wanting one's own wish to succeed,
One should inflict punishment on them.
Having made an effigy of the one concerned,
If one torments it with the vajra foot,
Crying out, "a la la!"
Even the lord of the gods will come.
Then give him the command as well.

Then once again, with the vajra dance,
Having firmly joined the palms,
And standing in the Mahendra stance,
One should spread them out toward all directions.
In the manner of shifting the feet,
Shift the right and likewise the left.
Joining the two hands,
Cross them over each other and clench them into fists;
Placing the two middle fingers side by side,
Fix the index fingers against them.
"This vajra mudrā of mine
Is the pledge of all the obstructors."
With the stance as before,
Having summoned the obstructors with the mudrā,
With this vajra of the wrathful one
One should give them the pledge.
Bind the mudrā of the vajra stance,
And sending forth the sound hūṃ,
Transforming oneself into a vajra,
One should summon all the obstructors.
If they do not give an answer,
They should not be released.
If they say nothing at all,
One should inflict punishment on them:
From tree poison, buckthorn,
And white mustard seed mixed together,
Having made an effigy of the one concerned,
If one casts it into the fire,
Even if Brahmā protects him,
That obstructor will come that very instant.
The mantrin should give him the command,
And then should make him flourish.
Generating a mind of loving-kindness,
If one thinks of releasing the mudrā,
Those tormented, bound and summoned
Are freed and dwell at ease.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Wholly intent on great wisdom,
Out of compassion for the welfare of beings,
Again transforms into the vajra itself.
Taking the even stance of the feet,
Having fully performed the vajra dance,
And having joined the vajra palms well,
One should spread them out toward all directions.
In the manner of shifting the steps,
Shift the right and likewise the left.
With the hands in the manner of dancing,
Then join the palms firmly.
In the manner of moving the steps,
The wise one turns toward the four directions.
With the mudrā of vajra paralysis
One should paralyze all the obstructors.
Swiftly, with the vajra step,
One should make the directions rigid as a vajra.
One should visualize great Meru with its eight peaks
As marked with a vajra.
Below, the hand holding a vajra,
Above, the right hand too holding a vajra;
Performing the vajra paralysis,
If one turns toward all directions,
Whatever obstructors have hostile minds
Will all be paralyzed.
Because the vajra paralysis has been performed,
The obstructors will not do harm again.
If one visualizes a vajra
Set firmly on the crown of their heads,
From as far as the abode of Brahmā
Down to the seventh underworld,
They are paralyzed by the vajra paralysis,
Stupefied and without consciousness.
For as long as the skilled mantrin wishes,
So long does he keep them paralyzed.

Then once again, in the vajra manner,
Having fully performed the vajra dance,
Having joined the palms in a cupped hollow,
If one spreads them out toward all directions,
Even the waters that arise on every side
The mantrin is able to dry up.
Taking the even stance of the feet,
Drawing in the vajra palms,
One should stretch the right hand upward
Holding the noose.
Likewise, the left leg
Should be raised together with the left hand.
Likewise, toward the four directions too,
In the manner of taking steps,
Manifestly like the fire at the eon's end,
Wrathful, pressing the lip with the teeth,
Gazing with the eyes downward,
Go swiftly with the vajra step,
And bending the left leg,
One should set it on the summit of Meru.
The lower hand thrusts forth forcefully,
And the right likewise from above;
Joining the stance of the feet for offering,
One should remain in the manner of a mouth.
In the manner of the mouth of the mare,
One should drink up the waters of the ocean.
If the mantrin is endowed with the meditative concentration
Taught by the vajra bearer,
The waters, sounding "a la la,"
Gather together from every side;
What need is there to mention streams, rivers,
And what comes from the clouds?
If he sets to it, even what arises
From the waters of the ocean of milk will dry up.

Then once again, in the vajra manner,
Join the vajra palms in a cupped hollow,
And spreading them out toward all directions,
One should expand them fully like a cloud.
Taking the Mahendra stride,
In the manner of shifting the feet,
One should lift the left foot
And then set it on the ground.
Having lifted the right foot,
One should turn toward the right.
With both hands in the manner of fists,
Raise the right hand holding the vajra,
And resting the left against the cheek,
Whirl it all around like a wheel.
Likewise, having set the right hand down,
Whirl all around with the left hand;
Resting the right against the cheek,
Raise the left hand holding the vajra.
Go swiftly with the vajra step,
And with the stride of the extended right leg
Set the foot on the summit of Kailāsa.
In the manner of the joined stance of the feet,
Having bowed the body,
Applying the root of paralysis,
Wrathful, pressing the lip with the teeth,
Oneself become like fire,
Of great power, with a mighty sound,
The great fierce one who subdues adversaries,
Circling above in the vajra manner,
One should surely take up the trampling.
The mantrin, fearless in mantra,
Wherever he casts his wrath,
In the manner of that vajra trampling,
Planting the left foot,
When he strikes with the vajra trampling,
Even Mahendra is crushed to powder.

When he strikes with the vajra trampling,
All the lands shake violently.
When Meru, Mandara and Kailāsa
And the encircling mountains are shaken,
What need is there to mention the gates of the asuras?
So it is, as taught by the vajra bearer.
Through the strike they shatter into a hundred pieces,
Like a handful of chaff scattered by the wind.

The master, the vajra holder,
To undertake the purification of the ground,
Having joined the vajra palms,
Should place them at the heart and spread them out.
Having joined the heels of the feet,
With the hands performing the dance,
And in the manner of setting down the feet,
One should set down the right and the left.
Having moved the feet,
Then join the heels once more,
And with that same manner of dancing
One should turn all around toward the four directions.
Wrathfully, to the central place
Go swiftly with the vajra step.
Having joined the vajra palms,
With the middle fingers embracing the ring fingers,
And both clenched into fists,
With the stance of joined heels,
Set the feet on the ground and lift them,
And extending fully to the right,
One should clear away all disturbances.
Uttering, "Fire! Fire!"
Whatever there may be within,
The mantrin should clear it all away.
The secret mantra with hūṃ and with phaṭ
The wise one should recite.
Below and on the surface of the ground
All disturbances are pacified,
And when no obstructors remain anywhere,
The mantrin should perform the rites in meditative equipoise.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Well trained in setting down the vajra,
Having fully performed the vajra dance,
And taking the Brahmā stance of the feet,
Having joined the lotus palms,
Should circle them directly before the heart.
In the manner of dancing, both hands
Should be clenched into vajra fists.
The left should be stretched out to the left,
The right should be stretched out to the right.
Taking the even stance of the feet,
Uttering the sound hūṃ,
The mantrin whirls about like a wheel.
Go swiftly with the vajra step,
And in the manner of the step with the right leg extended,
In the manner of setting down the feet,
With that very movement he whirls again.
Sending forth the sound hūṃ,
Likewise toward the four directions too,
By whirling the vajra fists
All the obstructors are made to tremble.
If the one endowed with vajra intelligence
Wishes to gather up the ground and cast it away,
With the gait of setting down the steps,
Swiftly extending the right leg,
He sets one foot on the summit of Mount Meru
And the other on the summit of Kailāsa.
Setting the stance of the feet in this way,
He fully performs the casting away on every side,
The left hand beneath the ground,
The right above the ground,
And sending forth the sound "a la la,"
If he hurls away all the world systems,
What need is there to mention other obstructors?
What need is there to mention hurling
The fierce and hostile vināyakas
From this human world to somewhere else?
If he wishes to burn up
Even the world systems entirely,
With that same manner of stance
He enters the four directions.
Going swiftly with the vajra step,
In the manner of the stance with the right leg extended,
Like twelve suns,
Unbearable, with garlands of flames,
Making the left foot firm,
The vajra holder raises his hand.
Having bowed the body,
And lifting the right foot,
The mantrin whirls about like a wheel.
Gathering in all the winds as well,
And transforming the sky to be like fire,
He enters in like the eon of burning.
Visualizing the vajra held in his hand
As entirely like Mount Meru,
Pressing down with the sound hūṃ,
When he hurls the vajra downward,
All the lands, together with
Their mountains and forests, are thrown into turmoil.
Then they turn to nothing but ash,
Like cotton put into fire.

The master, the vajra holder,
Then undertakes the gathering.
The manner of the stance is the same;
In particular, the hand is raised.
Going swiftly with the vajra step,
Having leapt and circled about,
One should take the posture of the mudrā.
In the manner of the posture, from behind,
One should go to the central place.
Likewise toward the four directions too,
One should beat with vajra strikes.
With that same manner of posture,
The intelligent mantrin, by the movement of the hand,
Takes up all the lands
And sets them down in that very place.
Gathering them in from every side,
The wise one recites the secret mantra,
And as the vajra bearer has taught,
One should set the lands down everywhere.
By this very sequence
The wise one sets everything in place.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Well trained in the vajra noose,
Should set down and tame
Hostile gods, asuras and humans.
Well trained in the vajra wisdom
That arises according to the power of intention,
One should thoroughly tame
All the diverse hosts that are to be tamed.
A world that is to be tamed by torment
Is brought to accomplishment by torment itself.
In the manner of right and left,
One should perform the five heroic postures throughout.
Taking the even stance of the feet,
One should press down with the stance of the extended right leg.
In the manner of setting the stance of the feet,
Looking at the target, one releases with the hand.
Joining the ring fingers and little fingers,
Make the two hands into a chain.
Join the left middle finger with the thumb;
The index finger of the right hand
Made in the manner of being extended:
This is what is called "the bow and arrow."
Behind the middle finger
Place the index finger in the manner of an arrow.
In the manner of the posture with the right leg extended,
Reciting with hūṃ, shoot so as to strike.
Whether one dwells beneath the earth,
Or dwells in the abode of Brahmā,
Or even is protected by Rudra,
When the vajra has been shot,
If he is struck by the vajra arrow,
He cannot be thought to survive.
This is the posture of shooting.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Becoming skilled in knowing the means,
Well trained in the manner of shooting,
Should tame hostile beings.
Then the master, the vajra holder,
Well trained in the vajra noose,
With a body that pervades the three realms of existence,
Should tame all the obstructors.
Taking the even stance of the feet,
In the manner of setting the feet,
With the right hand and the left
One should join the palms with a hollow between them.
Having bound that mudrā,
If one circles it toward all directions,
Even the three realms of existence are summoned;
What need is there to mention the vināyakas and obstructors?
This is the vajra mudrā of summoning.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Taking the even stance of the feet,
And having joined the lotus palms,
Should spread them out toward all directions.
In the manner of setting the stance of the feet,
Swiftly taking up his position,
He places the vajra fist at his heart,
And wrathfully uttering the sound hūṃ,
He should strike with the vajra hand
At the nāgas of the four oceans.
The nāgas, terrified, with joined palms,
Come forth out of the ocean.
Those who dwell beneath the earth,
The asuras, rākṣasas and guhyakas,
With minds eager for the command,
Take their seats, saying, "Ask for whatever you seek!
We are tormented by the blazing vajra.
Is it a yakṣiṇī, or a rākṣasī,
An asura woman, or a nāginī,
An elixir, or mercury,
Precious jewels, or the king of gems,
Fortune, or abundance as you desire?
Whatever it is your heart may wish,
Give us your command quickly!"
The mantrin, accepting whichever he pleases,
Should set the guhyakas to their tasks on every side.
If they contravene the command,
If, though commanded, they do not come,
With that same manner of stance,
One should press down with the stance of the extended left leg.
Setting the foot upon Meru,
With the hand held in the manner of a staff,
Roaring aloud, one should press down
All the abodes beneath the earth.
Sending forth the sound "ha ha,"
Whoever contravenes the command
Is seized by all the others
And led forth with great swiftness.
The mantrin should punish that one alone
And let all the rest go.
Having set him to the command,
The wise one should train him well.
Toward all the guhyakas and rākṣasas
One generates a pleased mind once more,
And having given them the pledge,
One should release them all.

The Noble Empowerment of Vajrapāṇi, the eleventh fascicle.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Possessed of a mind of affection and compassion,
Sees those who harm sentient beings,
Humans, nonhumans and the like,
Gods who do great harm,
Asuras together with guhyakas,
And other hostile grahas and the rest,
By means of diseases, snakes, poisons and so on;
Those who do not abide by their pledges,
Who have arrogant minds,
Who harm sentient beings
Through disease, famine and the like,
Who delight in harming others,
Who are without compassion,
Who act against the teaching,
And who are exceedingly given to arrogance.
Having seen them, in order to subdue them,
The one skilled in mantra should apply the rite.
Those who do not reflect within themselves,
"This will become a sin for me,"
Who have themselves entered a wrong path,
And bring ruin upon one being after another:
Toward those who harm others,
The mantrin should show no forbearance.
He should terrify them and bind them,
He should cut them down and slay them.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Well trained in the vajra noose,
Leaving no room in space,
Transforming himself into the form of a vajra,
With reverent concentration and grace,
With a smiling face and a proud bearing,
Having fully performed the vajra dance,
With the movement of the hands
One should dance toward all directions,
Taking up the form in accord with the actual deity,
And setting the mind in meditation.
With a body firm, of vajra nature,
In the manner of the uneven stance of the feet,
The vajra holder raises his hand,
And gazing everywhere with vajra eyes,
He presses down all of the three realms of existence.
Standing in the hero's position,
In the manner of the raised foot,
Uttering hūṃ, fearsome and terrifying:
One should imagine his two eyes as the Sun and the Moon,
His mouth as the mouth of the mare,
His navel as Nanda and Upananda,
His belly as the ocean.
Meru should be made his head;
Likewise his arms are the encircling mountains,
The earth is his two legs,
And Vāsuki, an emanated son, is kept at his secret place.
The wind is his two ears,
His hair is fire,
His tongue the lightning of great terror,
And he has the great sound "a la la."
Sending forth the thunderous sound hūṃ,
He circles fully toward all directions.
Then, intensely urged on,
Knocked down by the vajra sound,
The nāgas, gandharvas and rākṣasas,
The gods, the asuras and all the rest come.
Terrified by the uttering of hūṃ,
They will die, without doubt.
Burned by the vajra sound,
Dazed, and looking abject,
To wherever he may be,
The one greatly endowed with vajra yoga, they draw near:
"Great vajra one, give your command!
Do not treat us all in this way!"
Then, having established the pledge,
One should release all the gods.

The vajra terror, greatly fierce,
Great terror that terrifies,
Producing fear in those to be terrified,
Wondrous and worthy of worship by the great:
Against great terrors for humans and nonhumans,
Ill omens and great calamities,
Gods, nāgas and piśācas,
Planets and merciless mātṛkās,
Disease, famine and the like,
Disturbance of the country and death-dealers,
The māras and the vināyakas,
Pūtanas, rākṣasas and so on,
And many other harms as well
Arising from one's own country or another's:
In order to destroy them utterly,
One should apply the vajra terrifier.
In the manner of spreading the feet wide,
When one utters the sound hūṃ,
Urged on by the vajra terror,
All are swiftly brought to destruction.
This is unfailing and unobstructed,
The unbearable vajra terror.
When the mantrin applies it everywhere,
Even the three realms of existence come under his power;
What need is there to mention
Making the human world his servant?
By urging them even slightly with words,
Those who are exceedingly haughty,
The rākṣasas, asuras and guhyakas,
All come under his power.
Well trained in vajra wisdom,
He attains all lordship and all qualities,
And becomes the king of all the gods.
The wise one establishes them under his power.
In the abode of the nāgas he becomes the lord
Of the nāgas, the yakṣa kings and yakṣas,
And the gandharvas.
In the abode of the asuras, of the asuras,
In the abode of the garuḍas, of the garuḍas,
He becomes the lord, and of the kinnaras.
Gods and asuras, together with humans,
With reverent minds, as he commands,
All come under the power
Of the one possessed of the great sound of vajra terror.
This is the mudrā of the great sound
Of vajra terror.

The vajra that displays its magic to all
I shall explain next.
The glorious one who makes offerings
Offers in every way to the buddhas
Who dwell in the ten directions.
For the great-souled ones in existence
There is nothing that is not an inconceivable offering.
Smiling, with eyes wide open,
In the manner of right and left,
Having joined the vajra palms,
He bows in every way to the ten directions.
Having joined the palms at the forehead,
And circling fully toward all directions,
One should pay homage to all the buddhas
Who abide throughout the three times.
One should pay homage to all the sugatas and make offerings.
The offering that tames beings
Proceeds according to their dispositions.
The realm of beings has differing dispositions;
Various dispositions proliferate,
Various dispositions, conceptualizing.
With this and that unattached intention,
The mantrin should tame in every way
The many realms of beings.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Arisen as the great knowledge of all,
With the erotic, the heroic, the compassionate,
The comic, the wrathful, the terrifying,
The dreadful, the wondrous and the peaceful,
With passionate intention contemplates the nine modes.
Then the master, the vajra holder,
Who causes wisdom to arise for all,
With the right foot and the left
Graceful in their steps,
And with the right hand and the left
In the manner of dancing,
Joining the tips of the index finger and the thumb,
Should look toward the left.
In the manner of joining right and left,
Smiling, expansive, with reverent concentration,
Brandishing the vajra in his hand,
He should dance with passion.
All the gods and the guhyakas,
The asuras and the humans,
The sovereign lord of the desire realm,
And those who dwell in the three worlds he enamors,
And with sidelong glances and gazes
All of them come under his power.
When he summons all the three worlds,
What need is there to mention the humans on the earth?
Having summoned the king of the gods and the rest,
He places them under the power of the one who holds the vows;
All of them do as he commands,
And he establishes them as his servants.
The one greatly endowed with the vajra, knowing all,
Should sing the vajra song.
When they are urged on by the vajra song,
Passion and great passion arise.
With devotion to Vajrasattva
One should sing and dance.
With passionate intention multiplied a hundredfold,
One should enamor the realm of beings.
This is the vajra song:

"You whose body blazes in the three worlds, who tames by enamoring,
Possessed of a thousand rays sharper than ten thousand suns,
Who with forms of many guises enamors the beings who wander,
Vajrasattva, supreme lord, beautiful and powerful, please dance!"

When songs such as the ṣaṭaba
Are performed with the vajra dance,
And he summons all the three worlds,
What need is there to mention the humans on the earth?
This is the mudrā of the vajra dance.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Again transforming into the vajra itself,
With the practice of the vajra dance
Should become a dancer before all.
With all his steps graceful,
He makes the mudrā of summoning with the right hand
And the mudrā of releasing with the left.
With sidelong glances and gazes,
And with the right hand and the left
Endowed with the gesture of embracing,
He brings Brahmā and the other gods
Under his power in every way;
They serve him fully as servants,
Diligent in the command of the vajra bearer.
Enamored by the vajra passion,
The gods, humans and guhyakas
Intoxicated with pride through intense passion,
Those who are to be won over, the mantrin wins over.
Through complete attachment to the vajra body,
All desire is entirely abandoned.
With the melodious vajra song
One should enamor the realms of beings.
Because they are enamored by passion,
All the gods come under his power.
With all the melodious sounds of the vajra song,
Vajrasattva, pure by nature from the very beginning, is urged on.
With many illusory things,
And with that very melodious sound,
And with all the magical displays of the buddhas,
He brings them under his power, and then the buddhas appear.
He guides all the worlds,
He tames all the māras,
He purifies all consciousness,
He brings all beings to maturity.
When one sings this song
And fully performs the vajra dance,
The gods and whatever yakṣas there are,
And the asuras together with the nāgas, are all won over.
This is guiding by the vajra song.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Again transforming into Vajrasattva,
Wholly impassioned with great heroic intention,
The hero should display the five postures.
Taking the even stance of the feet,
With the right leg extended he subdues adversaries;
In the manner of the extended left leg
He should swagger like a lion.
Standing once again in the Brahmā posture,
With the vajra snapping of the fingers,
And in the manner of the vajra striking posture,
One should gather in Brahmā and the rest.
Singing the vajra song:
"Possessing a body that blazes in the three worlds,
Equal to the blaze of ten million suns,
Equal to space, and without equal,
Chief of the Dharma and of the supreme victors,
I bow to that supreme body of the Buddha."
Pressing the lip hard with the teeth,
With eyes on every side, as if frowning in wrath,
Resting the vajra at the waist,
One should gaze toward all the ten directions.
In the vajra manner with the right hand,
Proclaiming loudly to all directions,
Terrifying to behold, blazing with rays,
Like another eon of burning,
The vajra blazing with fire, brought to accomplishment,
Unbearable like a great terror,
Should be applied to the obstructors.
It crushes the three worlds to ash.
Except for a grave offense,
This mudrā should not be applied.
When the need is extraordinary,
It should be applied at the last resort.
Even if they are protected by Vajradhara,
By the uttering of hūṃ they are crushed to ash.
Unbearable like the fire of destruction,
This is the mudrā of the great vajra.

Then the master, the vajra holder,
Well trained in vajra wisdom,
Having placed the two vajra fists at his heart,
Should reflect in this way:
"Hell beings, pretas and animals,
And whoever else suffers suffering,
By the gesture of drawing them up with my hand,
I shall furnish them with every happiness.
Rains of food, drink and the like,
And rains of clothing and precious ornaments
I shall let fall upon them everywhere."
Having furnished them fully with whatever brings them ease,
Like Avalokiteśvara, lord of compassion,
One should spread out fully everywhere,
And one should think, "May all beings
Attain unsurpassed happiness."
This is the mudrā of compassion.

Then the vajra master,
Well trained in vajra wisdom,
Should bind the vajra mudrā
And join the palms at the crown of the head.
With a movement of raising and of grace,
He should then swiftly lower them.
With this mudrā of great vajra laughter
One laughs just like Vajradhara himself.
This is the mudrā of great laughter.

Then, raising the right hand,
Place the left at the side of the waist.
Having taken the stance with the left leg extended,
Then join the palms once more,
And, sounding forth a terrifying sound,
Bow down with palms joined.
This is the mudrā of the great terrifying sound.

Taking the stance with the right leg extended,
With both hands behind the back
One should hold two fists.
In order to make all obstructors tremble,
The neck must be bent down,
And when one utters a great sound,
Through the application of this mudrā
All the obstructors will tremble.

Having taken the even stance,
Join the palms at the crown of the head
And place both hands at the belly.
With the stance of the right leg extended,
The mantrin should whirl them round like a wheel,
Adorned with ha ha.
This mudrā is vast:
Since it crushes mountains to powder,
What need is there to speak of hostile beings?
It turns out just as the mantrin wishes.

Having taken the even stance,
Raise the hands upward
And bind the bracelet mudrā.
Likewise press the lower lip with the teeth.
With the left leg extended and well applied,
One should strike at all the obstructors.
This mudrā is the great sound;
It turns back all the obstructors.
By applying this mudrā well,
All of them are turned back.

Having taken the even stance,
From joining the two hands at the chest,
Raise the right hand,
And with the left leg well extended,
By the mudrā of the hammer
Strike, even if it be Vajradhara himself.
Since one strikes with the vajra hammer,
In that very instant he turns to powder.
This is the hammer stance.

Joining the right and the left as well,
Place the hands in the armpits.
With a gaze like the blazing Sun,
Uttering huṃ, look down with pride.
Transforming into Vajrapāṇi himself,
And letting out a terrifying sound,
One should make all the lands,
Together with the oceans and Mount Mandara, shake.
Pressing the lip with the teeth,
One should strike with the vajra foot.
By applying this mudrā well,
All of them will tremble.

Having taken the stance with the right leg extended,
With both hands in the manner of holding fists,
Place the left at the chest.
Raise the right upward,
And swiftly raise the index finger;
When one lets out the vajra sound,
Even were they buddhas themselves,
All obstructors shall be thrown down.
So the Vajra Holder has declared.
What need is there to speak of hostile beings?
When this mudrā is set in motion,
All of them are tamed.

Having taken the even stance,
Raise the right hand upward
And extend the hand with the palm open.
Place the left at the chest.
With a swift striking motion of the hand,
Strike the ground together with hūṃ.
Like the sky ablaze with fire,
Bind the sword mudrā
And cut all the obstructors to pieces.
So the Vajra Holder has declared.

Having taken the even stance,
Put the hand underneath,
Grasp the foot at its middle,
And lift it upward.
With the vajra sound hūṃ,
Wrathful and with a frowning brow,
Strike down again with that foot,
And imagine the obstructors there.
When one strikes with the vajra foot,
Even Mount Merus are crushed to powder.
This is the vajra-foot stance.

With the stance of the right leg extended,
Stretch out both hands in the manner of a wheel.
Wrathfully pressing the lip with the teeth,
Hurl the wheel in every direction.
The buddha fields abiding
In all directions will be destroyed.
From the fields being destroyed
Even the holy Victors come forth,
And the bodhisattvas of great miraculous power,
Those who have fallen under another's power,
Dazed, those wise ones
Went before Vajrapāṇi:
"For our lives to end would not be right.
O only son, what are we to do?
Whatever you wish,
We shall swiftly accomplish."
So saying, from the wise Vajrasattva
They fully received his command.

Become like the fire at the end of the eon,
Having taken the stance with the right leg extended,
By applying well the blazing mudrā,
One should set all three worlds ablaze.
Seeing the three worlds ablaze,
Those who always harm mantrins,
The greatly savage obstructing ones,
The hostile rākṣasas, are terrified.
Having seen such a mudrā,
They then become pacified.

Having taken the even stance,
Then, taking it up for the decisive strike,
The mantrin should fling it upward.
Then, pressing the lip with the teeth,
And swiftly taking a single step,
When one strikes with the decisive strike,
The hostile, obstructing rākṣasas
All turn to powder.
With the mudrā of the decisive vajra strike
One should turn them all back.

Abiding in the posture of Mahendra,
Raise both hands,
Take up Meru, king of mountains,
And, having placed it in both hands,
Strike the hostile beings
Who obstruct the mantrin.
Struck, they are utterly destroyed
And die, without a doubt.
When some obstructor creates obstacles
For a mantrin devoted to yoga,
Then the mantrin of great power
Should apply this mudrā.
Seeing this mudrā fully applied,
They will tremble.

Then the master Vajradhara,
Well trained in vajra wisdom,
Having paid homage to all the Victors,
Explained the method of the diadem stance.
The mantrin, facing east,
Places the right hand at the crown of the head
And places the left at the side of the waist.
Letting out the sound hūṃ,
With a mudrā like slow-rolling thunder
He should turn toward all directions.
And in the manner of the right leg extended,
Through the application of the swan's prowess,
When the right hand is raised aloft,
Even the oceans are lifted up.
This is the mudrā of the swan's prowess.

Raising the right foot,
Plant both hands upon the ground;
Adorned with a la la,
When one lifts up all three worlds,
Even Mount Meru together with the oceans,
All of it, is lifted high.
Mount Mandara and Kailāsa,
And whatever other mountains there may be,
When struck by the mudrā of ha la ha la,
Do not form again.

Joining the right and the left as well,
Place both hands at the belly.
Uttering hūṃ, look down with pride,
And press the lip with the teeth.
By the vajra diadem mudrā
One should devour all the obstructors.
With the posture of the left leg extended,
At that moment raise the left,
And by setting down the foot in wrath
One should draw in all three worlds.
This is the mudrā of the summoning diadem.

Having taken the even stance,
Raise both hands,
And, binding all the obstructors,
Whirl them toward the directions like a wheel.
By applying this mudrā well,
They are bound and tormented.
It dispels all the obstructors;
The Vajra Holder himself declared it.
This is the mudrā that terrifies the obstructors.

Seated on the yoga seat,
Raising the right foot,
Make both hands in the manner of a wheel,
And whirl the one foot all around.
By the wheel diadem mudrā
One should throw down all the obstructors.

Having taken the even stance,
In the manner of joining the palms in the flower gesture,
At that moment place them on the ground,
Then swiftly raise them again.
Since one makes offering with this,
Nothing at all creates obstacles for the mantrin.
This is the mudrā of the offering diadem.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of the Guhyakas, there is a stance called the Terrifier. When I was a bodhisattva, at that time, in that age, countless eons ago, I heard it from the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Buddha Ornament Melody. Listen to it well and thoroughly and keep it in mind, and I shall explain it to you.

Terrified by it, the obstructors,
All the vināyakas, are tormented.
They flee in all directions
And die, without a doubt.
Crossing the two hands over one another,
And applying fully the left leg extended,
One should whirl toward all directions,
Then press down once more with the foot.
This Terrifier is a great terror;
It turns back all the obstructors.
Through terror they are utterly destroyed
And die, without a doubt.

Then, in the manner of the foot as well,
Cast the eyes downward,
Raise the right hand,
And bring the left close to the chest.
Slapping the hand upon the ground,
One should strike with the vajra palm.
This is the vajra-palm stance.

Seated once more on the yoga seat,
Join the palms at the crown of the head,
And, raising the right foot,
Whirl it all around like a wheel.
This is the great binding of the wind,
Taught by the Victors of the past.
Even should a scattering wind arise,
It too will remain unmoving.
This is the stance of binding the wind.

Well trained in the manner of dance,
And shifting the feet as well,
Lower the hands downward,
Touch the thumbs and ring fingers of both hands together,
Apply well the left leg extended,
And dance with the right hand.
Through the dance all the obstructors
Come to feel the greatest joy,
And out of joy, from then on,
They create no obstacles for that mantrin.

Having taken the even stance,
Raise the right hand,
Make the left in the manner of a sword,
And join the two together.
Even were it Vajradhara,
One should draw in all the obstructors.
Having drawn them in thoroughly from every side,
Then one should release the mudrā.
When they are bound with the vajra noose,
Harmed on every side,
Crying out the sound a la la,
With minds gone dazed,
They fully receive the command
From the wise Vajrapāṇi.
"From now on make no obstacles!"
So Vajrapāṇi commanded them.
This is the mudrā of vajra torment.

Just as the mantrin wishes.
Then, once more generating compassion,
The mantrin, in meditative equipoise, upon the ground,
Joins the palms in the flower gesture,
And at that time, to them,
When the mantrin rests in equipoise,
He should say, "Making no obstacles,
Go now to your own abodes!"

Having taken the stance with the right leg extended,
One should raise the hand upward.
In the manner of the right and the left as well,
Place the fists in both armpits.
With the sound hūṃ,
The mantrin, performing the fierce rite,
In every direction, on every side,
Should torment them with the vajra torment.
"Whatever other vināyakas and obstructors there are,
Whatever rākṣasas are renowned,
And in the gaping mouth of the mare,
May all of them be tormented!"
Tormented by the mudrā of vajra torment,
They come, and
Trembling unbearably,
Saying, "For our lives to end would not be right;
Quickly tell us whatever command you wish,"
They receive the instruction he commands.
If they receive the instruction
According to whatever the mantrin wishes,
Then they will be set free;
Otherwise, never.
Because they are tormented unbearably,
"Whatever you desire,
That we shall do; grant us your command!"
So they speak at that time.
Having placed the vajra palms at the crown of the head,
One then gives them the pledge.
Drawing the figure of the obstructor on the ground,
And pressing it down with the right leg extended,
With the vajra-sword mudrā
One should cut all the obstructors to pieces.
Being cut to pieces, they all
Go to him for refuge.
Then, with a mind endowed with compassion,
One should release the mudrā,
And with a compassionate mind
One should say to them, "Do not be afraid!"
Just as the mantrin wishes,
They then seek their own abodes.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana said to the whole assembly: "Sons of noble family, there are three mudrās. When I practiced the conduct of a bodhisattva for ninety-one eons, at that time I obtained three mudrās from the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Essence of the Ornament of Conduct. For many eons, merely by displaying them, I was not harmed by the obstructors. So focus your minds one-pointedly and listen to them.

Having taken the stance with the right leg extended,
Place the left hand at the heart,
And with the right, together with a dancing gesture,
Place it well at the crown of the head.
This is declared to be the pledge
That always destroys the obstructors.
Merely by displaying it,
They flee, without a doubt.

Having performed the turning back once more,
One should slap the hand upon the ground.
Raising both hands,
At that time puff out the cheeks.
By binding the vajra mudrā,
The obstructors are then destroyed.
If even the Vajra Holder himself
Would be destroyed by the vajra mudrā,
What need is there to say that all the obstructors
Are ruined merely by seeing it?

The mantrin, seated facing east,
Rests the right hand on the hip,
Extends the left well,
And with the left should summon them all.
Summon even all who abide on the ten grounds.
Having summoned them from every side,
One should strike with the vajra mudrā.
Since one strikes with the vajra mudrā,
In that very instant they turn to powder."

Then the Blessed One Vairocana said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Vajrapāṇi, apart from my disciples, you should not explain this to anyone. Why is that? Because those faithless beings do not trust this way, and they will say, 'This was not spoken by the Buddha.' It should not be proclaimed even within their hearing, so what need is there to speak of the mudrās? Lord of the Guhyakas, the master should bestow it only after knowing the characteristics of the disciples, and not otherwise."

Then Vajrapāṇi touched the feet of the Blessed One and said this to the Blessed One: "Blessed One, what is the karmic ripening of those who show contempt for the master?"

The Blessed One said: "Vajrapāṇi, do not say that, for the world with its gods would be terrified. Yet, Lord of the Guhyakas, I shall say a little. Hero, gather your attention and listen.

The unbearable hells I have taught,
Those of immediate retribution and the rest,
Are declared to be their abode,
And there they stay for endless eons.
Therefore, in every way,
One should never show contempt for him.

Lord of the Guhyakas, if you ask how a disciple should regard the master: just as one regards the Buddha, the Blessed One.

When his mind is like this,
Joy will always arise,
And he will become a buddha
Who benefits the whole world."

This completes the section on the sequence of stances from the Vajrapāṇi Empowerment, the king of the lords of discourses.

Then the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, his very being expanded by the great and utterly vast wisdom of all the tathāgatas, entered by focusing on the vast expanse of phenomena, in order to enter the maṇḍala of the wheel of their speech. Having blessed himself as the Blessed One Vairocana, and manifesting the arising of buddhas, he entered into equipoise in the samādhi called Essence of the Ornament Arrayed with the Melodious Speech of the Buddhas. The moment the Blessed One had entered into equipoise, in that very instant, tathāgatas as numerous as the particles in the atoms of ten buddha fields, having understood this, for the sake of accomplishing immeasurable and utterly vast wisdom, stretched out their hands while remaining right where they were, and placed them on the crown of Vajrapāṇi's head. Having placed them there, they spoke these words to Vajrapāṇi:

"Excellent, excellent, great being,
You who bring vajra wisdom to perfection!
You repay what the buddhas have done,
Son of the buddhas, of great miraculous power.
The supreme wisdom of omniscience,
The holy secret: you are to speak it.
Set it forth in the manner of dhāraṇī-mantras,
And explain here the maṇḍala
Upon entering which, at once,
All misdeeds cease to be."

Then all those tathāgatas, as numerous as the particles in the atoms of ten buddha fields, drew back their hands.

Then Vajrapāṇi arose from that samādhi and spoke this supreme great dhāraṇī-mantra, which gives birth to all the tathāgatas, purifies all misdeeds, fully pacifies all illnesses, utterly destroys all obstructors, and gives birth to every excellence:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā he buddha matiri sarva vāpaniṣaṇi garja garja bhañja bhañja marda marda gambhīri gambhīra hasa hasa dama dama matha matha vidhvanasya vidhvanasaya sarva śatrūṃ taramati vimale pūraya pūraya pratijñāna sarva bhūta rūpasite bhagavati gana gana jahi jahi vimalasani kramaṇi hadavani ramaṇi mataṃgike svāhā

As soon as Vajrapāṇi had spoken this secret dhāraṇī-mantra, in that very instant this great earth quaked. It rumbled, it rumbled violently; it heaved, it heaved violently. When the south rose, the west sank; when the north rose, the east sank. The Sun and the Moon too fell to the ground, and the vidyādharas fell as well. Even those bodhisattvas who had gained mastery over all dhāraṇīs were stupefied. That whole assembly too was bewildered, and remained bowing in their minds to the youthful Mañjuśrī.

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī, seeing that the assembly was bewildered, said to them, "O friends, call to mind the great dhāraṇī-mantra of empowerment into great wisdom!"

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, "Vajrapāṇi, since this dhāraṇī has great power, how should one understand the power of any practitioner who retains it, keeps it in mind, and makes offerings to it?"

When he had said this, the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi said to the youthful Mañjuśrī: "Mañjuśrī, what is the power, the potency, the majesty of this dhāraṇī-mantra like? Mañjuśrī, in the eastern quarter there is a world system called Stainless Light Rays. In that world system the Tathāgata, the arhat, the perfectly complete Buddha called Stainless Light Rays appeared in the world, and that Blessed One gave this dhāraṇī-mantra, called Source of All, to the bodhisattva, the great being Vajraketu, so that he would retain it. As soon as it had been given to that bodhisattva, in that very instant, by the power of this dhāraṇī-mantra, forms of buddhas came forth from all his pores and entered them again, so many that no one could reach their end. Apart from those blessed by the Buddha, even the bodhisattva great beings who have gained mastery over all dhāraṇīs cannot show what this is like. Those forms of the tathāgatas remained, filling the sphere of space like a pod full of sesame seeds, and those forms of the tathāgatas empowered that bodhisattva great being with light rays and dissolved into that very body. Why is that? By the power of this very great dhāraṇī-mantra."

When he had said this, the youthful Mañjuśrī said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Great bodhisattva, since you have been empowered by all the tathāgatas as lord of all phenomena, you too, in order to reveal the maṇḍala of the great dhāraṇī-mantra, explain the maṇḍala of the great dhāraṇī-mantra!"

When he had said this, the bodhisattva, the great being Vajrapāṇi, said to the youthful Mañjuśrī: "Mañjuśrī, so that it may be accomplished to good purpose, listen to those wisdoms of all the tathāgatas that abide in the form of dhāraṇī-mantras and are blessed by all the tathāgatas. What are they?

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā amari amari ṭata vañja jha jha mici mici sphoṭa sphoṭa mata mata duradānta damka svāhā

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhya sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā he he kaṇiṭi kaniti sarva tathāgata vanake bodandide traṭaṭa traṭaṭa hūṃ phaṭ hūṃ phaṭ garja garja varmati vihūṃrika svāhā

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā bhagavate prajñāpāramitā ā sarva pāpakṣayaṃkari jñāna apaspharaṇi bhañja bhañja matha matha dama dama ānaya ānaya sara sara māraya māraya sarva bhūte janatriye svāhā

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā garja garja matha matha hāma hāma bhañja bhañja dundubhisvara nirghoṣe hana hana vidhvansaya vidhvansaya moṭya moṭya daha daha vipula nirmala nirajāte āhara āhara āgaccha āgaccha sarva buddha sarūpapite mā vilaṃba svāhā

Mañjuśrī, this great dhāraṇī-mantra is called Dhātvīśvarī of All the Tathāgatas. It gives birth to all the tathāgatas, it reveals the essence of awakening, and it is the unerring speech of all the tathāgatas."

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī, gazing unblinking at the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, said: "Son of noble family, you too should be regarded as a tathāgata. Why is that? Because you keep in mind this dhāraṇī-mantra endowed with empowerment into great wisdom, so that the great wisdoms of all the tathāgatas never seen before, and the deeds of the buddhas such as the pledges, may continue unbroken from one to the next."

Then, in that very instant, the youthful Mañjuśrī realized the vajra of great wisdom that arises from ninety-nine koṭis of wisdoms, such as the dhāraṇī-mantra of great empowerment. As soon as he had realized the vajra of great wisdom, great clouds of offerings arose in the maṇḍala of the assembly of all the tathāgatas. "How wonderful! Through empowerment into great wisdom, this Mañjuśrī has been empowered!" This sound rang out with a roar throughout all the world systems of the great trichiliocosm.

By its power, the youthful Mañjuśrī saw forms of the tathāgatas, as numerous as the particles in the atoms of ten buddha fields, entering into a great utpala. As soon as the forms of the tathāgatas had entered, then, by the power of realizing the dharmakāya, in that very instant forms of the tathāgatas came forth from and entered into all the spaces between the pores of the youthful Mañjuśrī, his diadem, his ornaments, his limbs and all his lesser limbs. Since even the bodhisattva great beings abiding on the ten grounds, who have obtained every mastery of mind, could not fathom the extent of even a single pore, what need is there to speak of the śrāvakas, the pratyekabuddhas and other worldly beings?

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī let go of that attention and, once more gazing long and unblinking at the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi, uttered this inspired utterance:

"These fields are to be fully purified;
The power of the aspirations of all the Victors' heirs is to be fulfilled.
For the benefit of beings, with tender love, free of sickness,
Teach the wisdom, the peaceful nectar so hard to find.
Having gained this precious and holy wisdom,
Beings by the koṭis of niyutas from the buddha fields,
Countless, as you have declared, you are to set free.
You are to make the teaching of samādhi supreme."

Then the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi said to the youthful Mañjuśrī: "Excellent, Mañjuśrī, excellent! Mañjuśrī, these words you have spoken are most weighty, and you too are excellent. Mañjuśrī, in keeping in mind the bases of the secret mantra of such a great dhāraṇī-mantra, you are doing something extremely difficult. Moreover, Mañjuśrī, those great bodhisattvas who keep such great wisdoms in mind, who retain them, accomplish them, and, having made them into a volume, pay homage to them every single day, are doing something extremely difficult. They will certainly attain unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening. When the time of their death draws near, tathāgatas as numerous as the particles in the atoms of ten buddha fields will grant them the sight of themselves, and they will always be blessed by the tathāgatas."

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī said to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Great being, explain the maṇḍala of the dhāraṇī-mantra of empowerment into great wisdom, the turning of the wheel of Dharma that has never occurred before, through which those who, having understood here this Dharma way of the bodhisattvas, strive earnestly to attain the state of omniscience, come to attain that state."

When he had said this, the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi said to the youthful Mañjuśrī: "Mañjuśrī, listen to the arrangement of the maṇḍala of the dhāraṇī-mantra of empowerment into great wisdom, merely by entering which the five deeds of immediate retribution are completely purified, all aims are achieved, all glory is produced, and one is fully liberated from all misdeeds. Mañjuśrī, I shall explain the maṇḍala. Mañjuśrī, I shall explain the maṇḍala that bears the names of these dhāraṇī-mantras of all the tathāgatas: it holds the great secret for all embodied beings, it is the supreme great secret of the masters, and it is more secret even than the secret.

Mañjuśrī, as for the measure of the maṇḍala:
Its measure is a full eight cubits,
Made with the master's own cubit.
In that maṇḍala, source of wisdom,
One should make three divisions.
In the three divisions the wise one
Should make the gateways there,
And there open four doors,
Square, with its borders and portals.
As before, in the three divisions,
One should make eight parts each.
Having made the eight parts, the skilled one
Should enter the central part.
Contemplating, moment by moment,
That all phenomena are unborn,
One should draw the king of maṇḍalas,
Which brings about every excellence.
In its center the mantrin
Draws a supremely beautiful lotus,
Entirely like emerald;
It is said to have eight petals.
Upon it is a vase,
And upon the vase an utpala.
Upon the utpala draw a conch,
Beautiful, wreathed with garlands of flame.
That Perfection of Wisdom
Is endowed with empowerment into great wisdom.
The eight parts that have been mentioned
Also arise from the central part.
Drawing them too in the manner of dhāraṇīs,
One should make them accord with the sequence of the rite.

Then, in the east, one should draw the Blessed Lady Mahāpratisarā, abiding in the maṇḍala of Mahendra, in the form of the utpala emblem. One should spread utpalas around the edges and set her in the center of a maṇḍala of blazing flames. This is the dhāraṇī-mantra of the Blessed Lady Mahāpratisarā:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathārā oṃ bhagavati sarva tathāgati janike surāya pratin tataṭaṭa marda marda āhara āhara hūṃ hūṃ jahi jahi svāhā

In the direction of Īśāna, one should draw the dhāraṇī called the Blessed Lady Invincible Vajra Flame:
In the center of a water maṇḍala
Draw a most beautiful lotus;
Upon it is a wheel,
Beautiful, wreathed with a garland of flames.
Its dhāraṇī-mantra is this:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvatha huṃ hriḥ sarva padma ta hrīḥ bhañja bhañja matha matha damamani dammani garghari garghari hrīḥ svāhā

Resting in the direction of Agni, one should draw the dhāraṇī called the Blessed Lady Who Abandons All Misdeeds:
One should draw the forms of the emblems
Endowed with water and with Mahendra:
Upon a lotus is a vajra,
Most beautiful amid the flames.
Its dhāraṇī-mantra is this:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā traṭa traṭa muhū muhū sarva pāpaṃ śodhane svāhā

In the south one should draw the dhāraṇī called Hard to Tame:
Endowed with fire and with wind,
An utpala is to be drawn there.

Upon it is a jewel
That fulfills every wish,
With a dense and peaceful garland of flames,
Encircled all around by lotuses.
Its dhāraṇī-mantra is this:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā dhaka dhaka moṭa moṭa daha daha sarva tathāgata jñāna nirjūte svāhā

In the direction of Nairṛti one should draw the dhāraṇī called Destroyer of All Māras:
Its maṇḍala is a maṇḍala of fire,
Set in the center of a lotus.
Upon it the mantra practitioner
Should draw a most beautiful hook.
Vajras linked one with another
The wise one should set out there.
Its dhāraṇī-mantra is this:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā haha mihu mihu dama dama khāhi khāhi dhuna dhuna mici mici durdāntadmani svāhā

In the north one should draw the dhāraṇī called the Blessed Lady Who Accomplishes the Infinite Gateways:
The maṇḍala to be drawn for it
Is like the disk of the moon.
Upon it is a lotus
Whose light is like the fire at the end of time.
Upon that is a sword,
Encircled all around by rays of light.
With utpalas bearing dense garlands of flames
It should be filled on every side.
Its dhāraṇī-mantra is this:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā mici mici garja garja sphoṭaya sphoṭaya matha matha dama dama svāhā

In the direction of the wind one should draw the dhāraṇī called Ratnākara:
Its maṇḍala is a maṇḍala of wind;
Upon its three portions rests an uṣṇīṣa.
All of it should be covered with hooks
And furnished with a garland of blazing flames.

In the west one should draw the Blessed Lady Who Impels:
Its maṇḍala is that of Īśāna,
And on it a vajra is well placed.
Upon that is a great lotus,
And upon that a rosary;
These are entwined with garlands of flames,
And above, they are covered with lotuses.

Then in the third tier
The bodhisattvas of great miraculous power
Should be drawn by the mantra practitioner as is fitting,
And in the eight directions as before.
There in the east the yogin
Should draw Lokeśvara of great light;
A most beautiful lotus
Is declared to be his mudrā.

In the direction of Agni one should draw the mudrā of the bodhisattva, the great being Samantabhadra, a wish-fulfilling jewel. In the direction of Īśāna one should draw the mudrā of the youthful Mañjuśrī, an utpala. In the south one should draw the mudrā of Vajrapāṇi, a vajra. In the north one should draw the mudrā of Maitreya, a water flask. In the direction of the wind one should draw the mudrā of Sarvanīvaraṇaviṣkambhin, a lotus. In the direction of Nairṛti one should draw the mudrā of Ākāśagarbha, a sword. In the west one should draw the mudrā of Kṣitigarbha, a lotus.

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī said this to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Vajrapāṇi, excellent, excellent! Vajrapāṇi, it is excellent indeed that you, for the benefit and happiness of all sentient beings, have proclaimed the maṇḍala of the great dhāraṇī-mantra of empowerment by great wisdom."

When he had said this, Vajrapāṇi said this to the youthful Mañjuśrī: "Mañjuśrī, whoever holds in hand this great maṇḍala of empowerment into great wisdom will become free of afflictions. He will become free of fear and of enemies. Whether he stays among bandits, among rākṣasas, among enemies, or in the midst of battle, weapons will not wound his body. He will not be overcome by bandits, nor harmed by rākṣasas and the like. No fear of untimely death will arise for him. He will always be endowed with glory. All his misdeeds will be thoroughly purified. The roots of virtue he has accumulated under hundreds of thousands of koṭis of niyutas of buddhas will become manifest to him as though in a single day. At the time of death, too, he will be watched over by the buddhas and bodhisattvas, and after death he will be born in Sukhāvatī. That being so, what need is there to speak of one who holds it, reads it, keeps it in mind, or retains even just its name? All his obscurations will be cleared away. He will close the doors to the lower realms, and the lower realms and the bad destinies will become far distant from him.

The person who rises in the morning
And recites it a single time
Will be freed from the misdeeds
Committed amid the perils of existence."

The Noble Empowerment of Vajrapāṇi, the twelfth and final fascicle.

Then the bodhisattva, the great being, the youthful Mañjuśrī, said this to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Lord of the Guhyakas, if someone draws this king of maṇḍalas and enters it, how great will his heap of merit be?"

Vajrapāṇi said, "Mañjuśrī, I am not able to set forth the measure of his heap of merit. Nevertheless, Mañjuśrī, you should know that the heap of merit of that son of good family or daughter of good family is as great as the number of particles of dust in the earth. Mañjuśrī, in the future there will be sinful beings who have no faith in this way. Mañjuśrī, those beings who are full of doubts, whose nature is given to all kinds of conceptual thought, who are in the grip of unwholesome friends, who are disrespectful to their fathers and disrespectful to their mothers, who revile their masters and gurus, and who seek out faults in their gurus, must not be allowed to hold in their hands this maṇḍala of the dhāraṇī-mantra of empowerment into great wisdom. It should not even be allowed to reach their ears. Mañjuśrī, one should not even talk about it in front of them. Even when it is written in a volume, it should not be opened in their presence, for if those sinful beings reject it even slightly, they will swiftly go to the lower realms, to the hells. They will say, 'This was not spoken by the Buddha,' and so they ruin themselves and ruin others as well. I declare that for beings who act in this way there is no accomplishment of secret mantra. The buddhas and bodhisattvas, too, will always turn their backs on them, and once these have turned their backs on them, there is no deliverance at all and no accomplishment of secret mantra."

When he had said this, the youthful Mañjuśrī said this to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Those bodhisattva great beings who have faith in this way of secret mantra are doing what is extremely difficult."

"Mañjuśrī, it is just as you have said. They are certain of unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening, and the tathāgatas will bless them."

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī said this to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "How great should one understand the heap of merit to be of a great bodhisattva who writes out in letters, reads and retains this great maṇḍala of the great dhāraṇī-mantra of empowerment into great wisdom?"

Vajrapāṇi said, "Mañjuśrī, as great as the heap of merit of as many tathāgatas as there are minute particles of dust in ten buddha fields, so great should his heap of merit be understood to be. Why is that? Because the Tathāgata himself abides as the very nature of this dhāraṇī-mantra, but those fools who have not understood this way do not know it.

"Moreover, Mañjuśrī, all the bodhisattvas who have attained dhāraṇī and attained mastery over all sentient beings, who perform various feats of miraculous power and wondrous display, and who display clouds of offerings of every kind in the maṇḍalas of the assemblies of all the tathāgatas, all of that, too, is the power of these dhāraṇī-mantras. Those great bodhisattvas who defeat Māra, who fully awaken to perfect awakening, and who turn the wheel of Dharma, that, too, is the power of these dhāraṇī-mantras.

"Mañjuśrī, whoever is empowered in this maṇḍala of secret empowerment into great wisdom has entered into all the samayas. Why is that? Because the Tathāgata himself abides as such a nature, as the nature of the dhāraṇī-mantra, or as the nature of the maṇḍala. Therefore, apart from this maṇḍala of the dhāraṇī-mantra, there is no other. Why is that? Because this maṇḍala is the nature of the Tathāgata. Therefore, whoever is empowered here has received the empowerment of all maṇḍalas.

"Mañjuśrī, in whatever place this maṇḍala of the dhāraṇī-mantra of empowerment into great wisdom is made, or whoever holds it in hand, or for whose sake it is written, or whoever rises in the morning every day and recites it once, for him fears will not arise. All the tathāgatas, too, will look upon him every day. At the moment of death, too, all the tathāgatas will reveal their bodies to him.

"Mañjuśrī, look: all the trees and forests that stand upon the earth, if there were no earth, they too would not exist. Mañjuśrī, in just the same way, all the tathāgatas and the great bodhisattvas who have attained mastery over mind abide in these dhāraṇī-mantras. Blessed by them, they find the state of omniscience. They are victorious over Māra. They turn the wheel of Dharma. They fully awaken to perfect awakening. Then they also accomplish the aims of sentient beings in many different ways. All of this is the power of these dhāraṇī-mantras.

"Therefore, Mañjuśrī, you too should take it up! Read it! Keep it in mind! Teach it correctly and extensively to others! By that, benefit and happiness will come to all sentient beings.

"Mañjuśrī, the place where this great maṇḍala of the great dhāraṇī-mantra of empowerment into great wisdom is made will become like a stūpa. And toward the mantra practitioner who holds it in hand, one should give rise to the perception that he is the Teacher. Mañjuśrī, all the tathāgatas will always bless that mantra practitioner. That mantra practitioner, too, should always guard this great maṇḍala of the great dhāraṇī-mantra like a casket of jewels. Why is that? Because, Mañjuśrī, there are beings whose nature is to lack respect for the buddhas and bodhisattvas and who seek out faults in their master. Mañjuśrī, if for such as these one should not recite it even in the slightest in conversation, nor even show them the volume, what need is there to speak of forcibly empowering them in the maṇḍala, having them read it, or having them look at it? Mañjuśrī, to let such people see it and to let it come out in words is that bodhisattva's error. Mañjuśrī, it should be understood as a great heedlessness on the part of the bodhisattva. It is a great error and an exceedingly great wrong.

"Mañjuśrī, to deceive the buddhas and great bodhisattvas is a light matter, but rejecting these words of the dhāraṇī-mantra is not so. Mañjuśrī, the appearance of buddhas is rare, like the udumbara flower. Rarer still than that is the teaching of dhāraṇī-mantras such as this. Rarer still than that is practicing it with diligence. Rarer still than that are those beings who hold it in hand, who listen to it, read it and retain it, or who draw the maṇḍala. If even the limit of the heap of merit of these beings cannot be fathomed, what need is there to speak of those who rise in the morning and recite it once? Their five acts of immediate retribution and the rest will be completely purified. No fears of any kind will arise for him. No untimely death of any kind and no fear of adversaries of any kind will arise. No diseases of any kind will arise in his body.

"Suppose someone, in a place secluded from activity, smears a maṇḍala with cow dung, offers flowers, and on an auspicious lunar date, at midnight, at an auspicious moment and a good time, writes it on birch bark; or else, on the eighth or the fifteenth day of the waxing moon, offers incense, flowers, fumigants and food offerings for the deities, sets out eight butter lamps and five golden vases, writes it on birch bark, and ties it on the neck, the upper arm, the wrist or the waist. Then even if he goes into the midst of battle, sets out on the road, or goes to a king's palace, none of these fears will arise for him."

"Mañjuśrī, this is the supreme protection for sentient beings. In whatever house, monastery or dwelling these dhāraṇī-mantras are kept, it becomes the supreme protection in that place. Mañjuśrī, in the world with its gods and its māras I have not seen anyone who would transgress against a protection made with any one of these dhāraṇī-mantras. I, Vajrapāṇi, will crush his head with a vajra blazing all over and become a single mass of flame.

"Mañjuśrī, when the rain does not fall, one should recite these dhāraṇī-mantras. When enemy armies approach, one should recite them. When harm from human epidemics has arisen, if they are recited, all fears will be completely pacified; they will be pacified at once."

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī said this to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Great bodhisattva, this exceedingly vast maṇḍala of the great dhāraṇī-mantra, this exceedingly profound one, hard to fathom, more secret even than the secret, not fit to be taught to sinful beings: Vajrapāṇi, what you have spoken is something exceedingly rare. Since it has never been heard before, to what sort of beings should it be explained?"

Vajrapāṇi said, "Mañjuśrī, there are those who have embarked upon cultivating the awakening mind, and when their awakening mind has become accomplished, then, Mañjuśrī, those bodhisattvas who practice the conduct of a bodhisattva through the gateway of secret mantra should be brought into the maṇḍala of the dhāraṇī-mantra of empowerment into great wisdom. But those whose awakening mind has not become complete should not be brought in; they should not even be allowed to look at the maṇḍala. Neither the mudrās nor the secret mantras should be shown to them.

"Mañjuśrī, listen, and I shall explain the great mudrā by whose mere binding, in order to bless this maṇḍala of the great dhāraṇī-mantra, everything becomes blessed. Joining the two hands and bending the right middle finger is the mudrā that summons all the dhāraṇī-mantras. Its secret mantra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvatha āgaccha punitti bari pūraṇi sami sami maṃgali maṃgali hūṃ phaṭ

"If that same middle finger is placed as before, the dhāraṇī-mantras will bestow their blessing in the maṇḍala.

"The mudrā of the bodhisattvas: if both hands are clenched into fists and the two index fingers are bent, all of them will be summoned. Its secret mantra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā tathāgata bodhisatva madhari āgaccha āgaccha sanidadhaṃ kuru kuru hūṃ hūṃ phaṭ phaṭ

"Then one should bind the mudrā from which supreme offerings arise. Joining the two hands and extending the two index fingers is the mudrā from which offerings arise; with it one should bless the supreme offerings. Its secret mantra:

namaḥ sarva tathāgatebhyaḥ sarva mukhebhyaḥ sarvathā mahābūja pravartani hūṃ phaṭ pravartaya pravartaya sarva tathāgata samaye svāhā

"Mañjuśrī, then the master should take the disciple into his care according to the rite, as before. To one who has accomplished the cultivation of the awakening mind he should show this maṇḍala and the mudrās. Anyone else, who is not such, should not even be allowed to look at the volume.

"Mañjuśrī, if a bodhisattva who practices the conduct of a bodhisattva through the gateway of secret mantra shows the maṇḍala or shows the mudrās to a being who does not know the samaya, he will have deceived the buddhas and bodhisattvas. He will also be committing the five acts of immediate retribution. Mañjuśrī, for a bodhisattva to commit the five acts of immediate retribution is a light matter, but to teach it to one who does not know the samaya, whose body has not been thoroughly purified, and who has not accomplished the cultivation of the awakening mind is not so. Mañjuśrī, one should never speak of it in words in front of such beings. Why is that? Because those sinful beings are ungrateful toward the buddhas and bodhisattvas and are without faith."

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī was filled with wonder at this in his mind. Thinking, "This domain of the buddhas is inconceivable," he fell silent.

Then the youthful Mañjuśrī said this to the bodhisattva Vajrapāṇi: "Great bodhisattva, great being, to hear these dhāraṇī-mantras, to enter the maṇḍala, to read them and to keep them in mind, or at the very least merely to have them reach one's ears, is exceedingly difficult."

Vajrapāṇi said, "Mañjuśrī, so it is. It is just as you have said. Mañjuśrī, you should know that those beings who make offerings to this dhāraṇī-mantra and write it out in letters are exceedingly rare. Whoever writes it on birch bark or on something else and wears it on any limb or lesser limb, for him no fears of any kind will arise, he will be freed from all misdeeds, and every day his roots of virtue will be accumulated as though from a thousand buddhas.
